Issue #1863 supplies the real venue transport, joined quotes, depth, venue valuation and conversion and the booking change for zero and negative confirmed fees; issue #1875 supplies the spread policy evaluator, its process runner and parameter validation (Pair policy section below), #1877 the authoritative rounded bounds, #1878 the canonical registration and #1881 and #1882 the replay wiring.

**Admission.** Root `pairs` and a member `pair_id` are research-only.
Every ordinary route refuses them in every mode, paper with acknowledgement included, before any configuration migration can write: ordinary, read-only, probe and ledger loads, inspection admission, reload, initialization (`pair_id` has no wizard or `InitOptions` support) and operator routes (the add-strategy command refuses a production-refused pair policy name through `productionRefusedStrategies` before the pair-token check, which refuses `pair_member`; the entry operator refuses a pair member).
Refused references are the root `pairs` key, a strategy `pair_id`, the `pair_member` positional strategy or open strategy and the `--pair-member-json` flag.
An ordinary load also refuses a state file that holds pair records.
Only the unexported research-admission context activates a pair: `newPairResearchAdmission(doc, runID, stateDir, resume, deps)` registers a one-time admission, scans the whole document strictly and checks the raw `pairs` entry (`pairResearchRawPair`, which the controlled fixture also calls), loads the configuration in memory through the internal loader, builds the contract, binds the frozen evidence source (`deps.Evidence`, Evidence source below) and checks the transports before any write.
Only then does `checkStateDir` claim the state directory: a new run creates it with one `os.Mkdir` (an existing path, an empty directory included, refuses) and creates `pair-research.json` exclusively and synced (Offline pair replay below); a resume needs the saved `pair-research.json` run manifest with the same run id, pair id, contract fingerprint, `pair_v` and `evidence_digest`), a synthetic account binding (`synthetic:<name>`) and a virtual transport (a `pairVenue` whose `Virtual()` is true and `LiveShaped()` false).
No configuration field, flag or environment variable grants it.
Issue #1864 wires it into the isolated replay runner and its paper extension.
A controlled live-shaped fixture (test-only) keeps both members in the live partition and replaces every dependency before it opens a member; only it accepts the venue account formats and builds live-shaped venues (Pair bounds, transport and the position barrier below).

**Contract.** A pair entry carries `id`, `underlying`, `policy` (validated through `pairPolicyStrategies`, `pairPolicyParamKeys` and, for raw parameter values, `pairPolicyParamValidators`; `cross_exchange_perp_arbitrage` is the one research pair policy beside the test-only fixture policy since #1875), `params`, `members` (`strategy_id`, `account_key`, native `instrument`, `source`, `modeled_delivery_ms`, `cost_policy`, `meta`: native unit, base asset, contract size, quote, settlement and price currency, tick, lot, minimum quantity and notional, trading status, linearity, position mode, margin mode, maximum leverage, initial and maintenance margin rates, price rule, size decimals, tick and lot step text, fee currencies), `limits` and `book_need` (Contract version 2 below).
The member identity key is `(partition, provider, account binding, native instrument, member id)`; the underlying is a separate aggregation key.
Load refuses: anything but exactly two members on two venues, a different underlying, a missing or duplicate member, mixed partitions, a live member outside the fixture, conflicting venue-account-instrument owners (a standalone strategy trading or hedging the underlying in the partition included), baskets in the same research config, a member with `basket_id`, an unsupported mapping (Hyperliquid: base units, contract size 1, `one_way`, `cross` or `isolated` margin, the bare coin, USDC currencies; OKX: contracts, `net` position mode, `cross` margin, `<UNDERLYING>-USDT-SWAP` with USDT quote, settlement and price currencies).
Load also refuses a member without the `pair_member` positional strategy, explicit `--mode=paper`, `direction: "both"`, finite positive `capital`, an explicit native close and a resolvable stop owner (Hyperliquid: a stop field or the default ATR stop; OKX: `atr_stop` or a close with `sl_after`), and any independent open evaluator, scale-in, inversion, hedge, replay mirror, regime or Hurst gate, higher-timeframe filter, closed-bar decisions, LLM entry analysis, `risk_per_trade_pct` or `margin_per_trade_usd`.
Pair identity, membership, policy, parameters, limits, accounts, instrument mappings, source and storage are restart-required; reload refuses every change, and startup refuses a changed contract while a position, an unapplied fill, a funding gap or recovery evidence remains, and a removed pair that still holds recovery evidence.

**Configuration limit rules** (`pairOperationalLimitErrors`, called by `pairConfigErrors`; #1869 added `quote_age_ms`, `collection_timeout_ms`, `receive_skew_ms`, `envelope_max_bytes` and `protective_width_bps` to the same validator, with the rules in the Contract version 2 table).
Raw types are checked before typed decoding: a missing field, `null`, a boolean, a string, an object, an array or an unknown key is refused, and a timing field must parse as an integer (`1.5` and `1e3` are refused).
The dollar fields (`gross_budget_usd`, `hyperliquid_notional_cap_usd`, `okx_notional_cap_usd`, `unmatched_cap_usd`), each member's `contract_size`, `tick_step`, `lot_step` and `capital` must be finite and above zero; each venue cap and the unmatched cap must not exceed the gross budget (equality passes); the timing fields (`decision_cadence_ms`, `acceptance_deadline_ms`, `recovery_age_ms`, `max_hold_age_ms`, `retry_initial_ms`, `retry_max_ms`) must be in [1, 2147483647] (`pairMaxIntervalMs`); `retry_initial_ms` must not exceed `retry_max_ms`; and `recovery_age_ms` must be at least `acceptance_deadline_ms` plus the 120000 ms absence grace (`entryRecoveryGrace`).
Every time-plus-limit sum uses one overflow-checked addition and holds the operation on overflow.

**Cadence slots and quote events.** A slot is a `basket_rebalances` row reserved before collection (`reserved`); `collectPairSlot` (#1871, Pair quote collection and envelopes below) rechecks the reserved row and contract fingerprint after unlocked collection (`recheckPairSlot`) and freezes the envelope (`freezePairEnvelope`: cutoff, input fingerprint and evidence; `targets_frozen` with an empty decision).
The decision record is written once into `targets_json`: `targets_frozen` with an `enter` or `exit` record waits for its first action, `executing` has one, `held` keeps a policy hold, coordinator hold, invalid output or an interrupted unfrozen slot (a restart turns `reserved` or `ranking` into a durable hold; an undecided frozen slot is decided again from its saved envelope), `completed` reached its targets or served only as fresh evidence (`evidence_only`), and `superseded` was replaced (an older undecided slot, or a decided slot with no action).
Pair mode does not use the basket deadline hold or supersede rules; a new slot is reserved while actions are unresolved and never supersedes an `executing` slot.
A quote event is `(pair id, contract fingerprint, slot time, cutoff, input fingerprint)` (id `pq-<hash>`); the first leg uses its origin event, and each later member increase needs a newer event that member has not used.

**Decision entry point.** Only the coordinator calls the policy runner (`pairPolicyRunFn`, which is `runPairPolicyProcess` since #1875; tests replace it per coordinator through `basketCoordinator.pairPolicyRun`). `decidePairSlot` runs for the newest undecided slot when the pair is `flat` or `matched` and no coordinator exit is due (otherwise the slot ends `evidence_only`), sends a `pairPolicyRequest` (version 2, #1871) outside `mu` within the policy budget (`pairPolicyBudget`, #1873, Pair cadence, clocks and host order below).
It then discards the result if the slot or pair snapshot changed, decodes it strictly (`pairDecodePolicyResult`, Pair policy section below), validates it (`validatePairPolicyResult`: identity equal to the request, `enter` only when flat with opposite equal-size legal-lot targets, a matching orientation and positive price bounds, `exit` only when matched with zero targets; `invalid`, unreadable output, a runner error, a result past its useful decision window or a timeout hold the slot; a timeout also saves the `policy_infrastructure_timeout` marker) and freezes it (`freezePairTargets`, one changed-row-checked write with a read-back).
An entry whose full fill at its price bounds, converted with the verified envelope's price-currency conversions, would break a cap holds.
Since #1871 `decidePairSlot` first verifies the saved envelope (`verifyPairEnvelope`); damaged, changed or missing evidence holds the slot and no policy runs.

**Boundary rules** (`pairAgeWithin`, `pairSkewWithin`, `pairBeforeDeadline`, `pairReached`; issues #1863 and #1864 call these and add no second comparison). Caps pass at `basketWithin` (equality passes; an elective reduction during a breach passes when no reachable outcome is worse). Collateral, margin and fee reserve refuse only when the available amount is below the requirement. Quote age passes when the receipt is not after the reference and the age is at most the limit; skew passes at most the limit. A submission or venue acceptance is permitted only before the deadline (at equality nothing is sent and the action ends as proven never sent). Uncertain absence releases only after the deadline plus the grace, with fill history covering at least that time, complete matching history and no contrary evidence. Maximum hold age (from the first confirmed fill) and recovery age (from the saved recovery start) trigger at equality; a retry is permitted at its retry time.

**Planning.** Before each tranche the coordinator includes booked exposure, confirmed unapplied fills and the zero-to-remaining possible fill of every unresolved elective or protective request, and proves every independent no-fill/full-fill endpoint against gross notional, each venue's notional, unmatched aggregate base exposure at each member's own upper valuation, free collateral with fees and margin (the fee reserve uses the member binding taker rate, which is also the view `FeeRate`; it is a reserve, not a booked cost), and maintenance margin (Pair valuation view below, collateral rule); the upper valuation is the member's own best ask from its checked reference converted with its price currency, widened by every pending buy bound (a sell floor alone never proves an upper bound; with no own upper valuation every elective action holds).
Quantities are normalized base units with multiplier one; native quantity is computed once at the request.
Tranches are exact lot multiples; the first leg (the member with the coarser base lot) must fit alone, a hedge never exceeds confirmed exposure, exits alternate bounded tranches.
Dispatch needs both members' published views valid at the reader time (an increase: mark, conversions, capability and account; a reduction: the mark only); a frozen decision whose view fails at its first dispatch ends `held`.
An exit without an exit decision (a hold-age exit) bounds each tranche at the member's own reference depth: the native price of the level where the visible depth on the order side reaches the tranche quantity, else the deepest visible level, and the next step plans the rest.

**Lifecycle and recovery.** States are `flat`, `opening`, `matched`, `exiting`, `recovery` and `operator_held`.
An increase reserves its action, its linked entry intent (`decision_kind: quote`, `owner_mode: pair`) and its quote use in one transaction (`ReservePairAction`, which also checks the version-2 request against the selected envelope, #1871), and one later transaction commits both submitting transitions (`MarkPairActionSubmitting`) before transport.
Every unresolved action is queried in a stable order by its saved account-bound client id; an unknown elective action blocks later elective orders but never another member's protective query or reduction.
Evidence is validated (account, instrument, client, side, monotonic cumulative quantity and notional, no overfill, the incremental price within the saved bound, acceptance before the deadline, fee identity and version) and persisted before settlement; each confirmed delta is booked once at the price from the notional delta through the platform owner (Hyperliquid: the Hyperliquid result path; OKX: the shared perps owners), in the same partition save as the applied totals, quote consumption, intent and expected quantity.
An open order stays unresolved after an incremental booking.
A proven zero-fill first leg ends the entry; a rejected or failed hedge, an unexplained book change (stop, take-profit, liquidation, kill switch or operator close; growth or flip) or bad evidence freezes increases and opens recovery, which reduces every remaining leg toward zero with reduce-only protective actions and never reopens the closed side.
A proven terminal failed protective reduction retries under a new identity after capped exponential backoff (`retry_initial_ms` doubling to `retry_max_ms`); a query failure keeps the identity.
At recovery-age expiry the pair becomes `operator_held` with one CRITICAL alert while queries and protective reductions continue; it returns to `flat` only when both books are flat, every action is terminal and accounting is complete.
Pause, a quote outage and the persistence, daily-loss, notional and kill-switch holds stop increases only.
A latched kill switch keeps flattening priority: the kill-switch Hyperliquid and OKX closers (`pairKillSwitchHLCloser`, `pairKillSwitchOKXCloser`) and the circuit-close owners (routed per strategy through `pairCircuitHLCloserFor` and `pairCircuitOKXCloserFor`) always call the native close, and pair bookkeeping never gates it.
Since #1914 the coordinator is the only book writer for a pair member's emergency close: it books only confirmed venue fill records through the live settlement ledger, and every generic close owner skips pair members (Native emergency evidence and accounting (#1914) below).
The native close carries no pair client id, so a native action is never queried by its saved id; an unconfirmed outcome ends `held` (never `committed`) with a durable `native_fill_unknown` obligation when the member book is flat, when a later native close supersedes it, or at the acceptance deadline plus the absence grace.

**Pair settlement evidence (#1874).** Code: `scheduler/pair_settlement.go`. It applies to live-shaped members only (`pairLiveShaped`, native emergency actions included since #1914); a virtual research member keeps its valuation conversion and modeled fee, and its booking is unchanged.
An outcome may carry `fill_coverage` (`[]pairConfirmedFill`) and `fee_coverage` (`[]pairFeeFill`); records hold decimal strings, sort by the scoped fill id (partition, member, provider, account, instrument, client order id, venue fill id), and each record's `evidence_hash` is the SHA-256 of its canonical JSON with that field empty.
Execution records must be valid (printable id, venue time at least 0, quantity and native notional above 0, matching hash), unique, a superset of the accepted set with every retained record unchanged, and not above the cumulative totals; a violation holds and freezes like any inconsistent evidence. Outcome validation keeps coverage optional, so native closes never need it.
The execution set is complete when its exact decimal sums match `cum_qty` (`basketQtyEqual`, relative 1e-9) and `cum_notional` (relative 1e-9); the records stay the accounting authority.
The fee set is a subset of the execution set with equal execution fields; its version (`fee_evidence_version`) is the number of fee-covered fills; `fee_evidence_id` must equal `pairFeeEvidenceID(action)` (`pfe-` plus 32 hex of the saved order scope); each fee currency must be one of the member's frozen `fee_currencies`; a known zero fee is an explicit record and a negative amount is a rebate.
A higher version is accepted only as a strict superset with every retained record unchanged; the same version must be identical; a lower version that is a clean subset is stale and changes nothing.
Any other fee set (a dropped, changed or same-version different record, a changed identity, or new fills after finality) is a conflict: the accepted evidence stays, nothing is charged, increases freeze, a `fee_conflict` obligation stays and one durable CRITICAL alert (`fee_conflict|<action>`) is sent; authoritative reconciliation is required before release.
A malformed fee answer (bad record, hash, decimal, currency, duplicate, a version that is not its record count, or a record that matches no execution record) changes nothing and keeps a visible `fee_invalid` obligation without freezing; valid execution evidence in the same answer is still accepted, so confirmed unapplied exposure stays visible to risk and recovery.
`fee_final` advances only on an accepted or identical answer that is terminal, has complete execution coverage and covers every execution fill; a higher-version superset may set it; a stale, conflicting or incomplete claim cannot, and nothing clears it.
Quantity settles only with complete execution coverage, a nonempty accepted fee set and every needed conversion, and it books the fees of the newly booked fills with that quantity (`with_quantity`).
Missing coverage holds with `fill_coverage`, an empty fee set with `fee_unconfirmed`, a fill with no venue time and no `accepted_at_ms` with `conversion_unavailable`, a missing rate with `settlement_conversion`, and a legacy action (a fee version without saved records, or applied quantity without a ledger) with `legacy_settlement`; none of them replaces confirmed evidence with a modeled fee.
Partial fee coverage keeps `fee_incomplete` visible and the action unresolved; a later superset charges only the newly covered fees of already booked fills as a correction (`correction`), also while another quantity increment holds; fees of unbooked fills wait for their quantity.
A booked row whose fills are not all fee-covered keeps the confirmed amount with no modeled fee and `fee_source = userfills_pending`; the correction that covers every fill of that row sets `userfills`.
When an action ends (`committed` or `no_fill`), every per-action settlement obligation except `fee_conflict` and a `legacy_settlement` whose legacy cause remains is cleared before the save (`clearPairEndedObligations`), so a refused fee answer or a quantity hold never outlives its action.
A settlement pass does one kind of work: a quantity booking, or corrections of durable booked rows.
Each fill converts at its own time: the venue fill time, else the saved `accepted_at_ms` (the ledger records which).
The increase's saved request conversion is tried first (hash, member key, partition, currency, positive rate, observed by the fill, received by settlement, inclusive validity window around the fill).
Otherwise `pairFrozenEvidenceSource.SettlementConversion(ctx, member, currency, fillAtMs, settlementAtMs)` selects from the frozen schedule with `ObservedMs <= fillAtMs`, `FirstReceiptMs <= settlementAtMs` and `ValidFromMs <= fillAtMs <= ValidThroughMs`, preferring the latest receipt, then observation, then the smaller evidence id for an equal rate; tied different rates refuse. USD is the unit. The decision-time `Conversions(ctx, member, refMs)` is unchanged.
The coordinator captures the action, coverage, ledger, applied totals, finality and book under its lock, runs the bounded lookups unlocked (the `pairConversionLookup` hook is for controlled tests only), takes `settlementAtMs` from `pairNow` after the lookup (never earlier than the clock reading the lookup used), rechecks everything and discards a stale result without any change. A source error, timeout or refused record holds visibly; no cycle valuation or private fetch substitutes.
The incremental native price is checked against the saved bound before conversion; the USD price is the sum of separately converted native notionals over the booked quantity. An unbooked remainder (a booking below the increment) books at the saved fills' USD ratio with no fee, because each fee is charged once at its fill's first application.
The ledger (`settlement_json`) saves per fill the scoped id, hash, selected time and source, settlement time, quantity, native notional, price currency, complete conversion evidence, rate, USD notional and booking row, and per fee the record, its conversion, rate, signed USD value, mode and target row. Saved entries are never rewritten; a later fee uses only its own saved rate.
A correction targets each fill's own booking row (grouped per row, one `pairFeeFix` each with its row id and member stamp), never the action's last trade. One owning-partition transaction writes cash, the trade fee and its source (`userfills` once every fill booked in that row has a fee record, else `userfills_pending`), the closed position it ended, the linked intent, the applied totals, the coverage, the ledger and the applied version.
A fee correction on any close row (partial or full) changes the daily PnL by the same delta once and never changes the consecutive-loss count.
A provenance refusal, or a guard miss on an action row with a staged correction, undoes the staged cash, daily PnL, retained rows, intent, applied totals, booked rows and ledger (the status only if it is unchanged since staging), holds with `cost_provenance` and saves again at once; any other save error keeps the `needsDurable` retry, so a retry or restart never charges twice.
Paper members keep the modeled paper fees.

**Member cost bindings (#1887).** Each pair member has one immutable cost binding owned by its admission (`scheduler/pair_cost_policy.go`); no process-global map keyed by strategy id exists, and the legacy process policy (`bindFillCostPolicy`, `activeFillCostPolicy`) and the version-1 replay resolver, `fill_cost_policies` store and `verifyBasketReplayPolicy` are unchanged.

- **Transport declaration.** `pairVenue.ExecutionModel() string` declares the price algorithm: `virtual_ioc_adverse_max_v1` (`pairExecutionVirtualIOCAdverseMax`) only for a research admission's virtual transport (`Virtual()` true, `LiveShaped()` false), `venue_confirmed_v1` (`pairExecutionVenueConfirmed`) only for the controlled live-shaped transport; any other value is refused. `Virtual()` alone never proves the algorithm. The scripted research fixture declares `virtual_ioc_adverse_max_v1` and returns already priced IOC outcomes; #1880 supplies the algorithm itself.
- **Binding.** `loadPairResearchConfig` (shared by the research admission and the fixture) binds after the evidence source: for each contract member it checks that the transport provider equals the contract provider, that `AccountKey(partition)` equals the contract `account_key`, that the strategy is in the contract partition, and the declared model; the bindings are then frozen on the admission (`costs`, behind `costMu`), and a second load must rebuild identical bytes. `pairVenueFor` repeats the provider, account, partition and model check on every acquisition before dispatch or booking.
- **Canonical bytes and id.** `pairCostBinding` is exactly `{v: 2, contract_fingerprint, member_key (pairEvidenceKey), cost_policy_rates: {taker_fee_pct, slippage_pct (pairDecimal through pairDecimalFromFloat), fill_role, spread}, execution_model}`, written by `pairCanonicalJSON`; `PolicyID` = `pair-cost-` + the full lowercase SHA-256 of those bytes and is never part of them. Every member uses `ModelVersion` 1001 (`FillCostModelReplayCustomVersion`), rates equal to the legacy defaults included. Equal rates on different members have different ids; the contract bytes and fingerprint never include an id.
- **Durable evidence.** `pairProgress.CostEvidence` (`json.RawMessage`, JSON `cost_evidence`, inside `basket_contracts.progress_json`) holds the canonical `pairCostEvidence{v: 1, contract_fingerprint, members: [{member_id, binding, model_version, policy_id}]}` sorted by member key.
  A fresh run stages it at load and commits it in its first pair write (the slot reservation also writes it); until a commit succeeds `costDurable` is false, `stepPair` retries the commit and dispatches nothing, and every member policy resolution refuses.
  Load (`bindPairCostEvidence`) compares saved evidence with the rebuilt bindings by canonical bytes (missing, malformed, extra, changed rate, model, model version or id refuse with the saved state unchanged), even for a flat or zero-trade run. A run with saved pair state (a contract or progress row, a slot, an action, an expected quantity, a quote-event use, a pair-owned entry intent, or a member trade row or position) but no evidence is a pre-binding run that is refused and never stamped or converted. A run with no saved pair state, such as a resume after every first commit failed, stages the evidence the same way a fresh run does.
  The flat lifecycle reset keeps `CostEvidence`.
  Every pair write (`writePairTx`, `reserveBasketRebalanceRow`) checks the saved evidence before the contract upsert and refuses to add, remove or replace it.
- **Resolution.** `fillCostPolicyForStrategy(owner, id)`: a nil owner returns the legacy process policy; a released, unregistered, unbound or not yet durable owner, or an unknown member, refuses with `errPairCostOwnership`. A member book resolves through `StrategyState.Basket.Pair.admission` with its pair id, contract, partition and member checked (`strategyFillCostPolicy`); a pair member book (`pairCostPair`, set from `pair_id` and at the runtime link) that lost its link never falls back to the legacy policy, and pair-aware owners also check `sc.PairID` (`pairDispatchCostPolicy`).
- **Owners.** The checked binding is set as the book's cost scope before any cost or book change by `bookPairFill`, `executePerpsSignalWithLeverage`, `bookPerpsCloseWithFillFee`, `bookPerpsPartialCloseWithFillFee`, `applyPerpsScaleIn`, `executeHyperliquidResultDeferredOpen`, `executeOKXResult`, `closeVirtualPositionAtMark`, `closeVirtualPositionVerifiedFlat`, `applyHyperliquidCircuitCloseFill` (the model-only reconciliation included) and the manual queue; `paperStopBookPx(sc, s, side, px)` and the dynamic close regime check it before a trigger, regime or book change.
  Inside the scope the modeled fee is the member taker rate and paper slippage the member slippage on both venues; a confirmed IOC or venue fill books at its price with no second slippage, a confirmed fee of any sign (`pairConfirmedFee.Present`, #1874) replaces the modeled fee and keeps its order id, with the `userfills` source only when `Covered` is also set and `userfills_pending` otherwise, while ordinary callers keep the legacy positive-only selectors.
  A refusal changes nothing, logs CRITICAL and queues a fault that the next `stepPair` turns into a `cost_provenance` obligation, recovery with increases frozen and one CRITICAL alert; a confirmed live close that cannot be booked takes the same path and no later sync books it with another stamp.
- **Backstop.** `RecordTrade` stamps a pair member's execution or close row from the scope; outside a scope it records the row unchanged (version 0) and raises the fault, and a row whose stamp differs from the binding is recorded unchanged with the fault. Funding rows keep their type and the process stamp (#1888 owns pair funding). The ledger comparison still refuses model 1001.
- **Fee corrections.** Before any in-memory change the fee-only correction reads the target row from the owning partition file through `StateStore.PairTradeStamp` (also when it left the retained history) and requires exactly one row with the member stamp (and the same stamp on a retained copy); otherwise it holds with a `cost_provenance` obligation and increases frozen. The staged `pairFeeFix` carries `trade_row_id`, `cost_model_version` and `cost_policy_id`; `applyPairFeeFixTx` rechecks uniqueness and updates only that row id with that stamp, preserving it. A recheck failure (`errPairFeeFixProvenance` in `pairFeeFixError`) undoes the staged cash, daily PnL, retained row, intent and action changes, clears the pending fix, holds with the obligation and lets the partition save again; any other save error keeps the `needsDurable` retry. No correction row is created.

**Contracts for later children (#1880, #1881, #1888).**

| Name | Go signature or type | Rule |
|---|---|---|
| `pairCostBindings` | `func pairCostBindings(owner *pairResearchAdmission) map[string]fillCostPolicy` | fresh map keyed by member strategy id; each value has `ModelVersion` 1001, the full `PolicyID` and rates equal to `pairContractMember.CostPolicy`; nil for a nil, released, unbound or not yet durable owner; consumers refuse an absent or incomplete set |
| `fillCostPolicyForStrategy` | `func fillCostPolicyForStrategy(owner *pairResearchAdmission, strategyID string) (fillCostPolicy, error)` | nil owner = legacy; otherwise the durable member binding or an `errPairCostOwnership` error |
| `ExecutionModel` | `func (a *pairResearchAdmission) ExecutionModel(memberID string) (string, bool)` | the frozen model (`venue_confirmed_v1` or `virtual_ioc_adverse_max_v1`) once bound; #1880 declares `virtual_ioc_adverse_max_v1` through `pairVenue.ExecutionModel()` and reads rates from the contract member, which equal the binding |
| `pairCostBinding` | `V int "v"`, `ContractFingerprint string "contract_fingerprint"`, `MemberKey pairEvidenceKey "member_key"`, `CostPolicyRates pairCostPolicyRates "cost_policy_rates"`, `ExecutionModel string "execution_model"` | version `pairCostBindingVersion` 2; `pairCostPolicyRates` = `TakerFeePct pairDecimal "taker_fee_pct"`, `SlippagePct pairDecimal "slippage_pct"`, `FillRole string "fill_role"`, `Spread string "spread"` |
| `pairCostEvidence` | `V int "v"`, `ContractFingerprint string "contract_fingerprint"`, `Members []pairCostEvidenceMember "members"`; member = `MemberID string "member_id"`, `Binding pairCostBinding "binding"`, `ModelVersion int "model_version"`, `PolicyID string "policy_id"` | version `pairCostEvidenceVersion` 1, canonical bytes, members sorted by member key |
| `pairProgress.CostEvidence` | `json.RawMessage`, JSON `cost_evidence` | frozen at the first commit; later writes cannot change it; #1888 adds fields beside it and never rewrites it; `pairProgressVersion` stays 1 |
| `pairResearchManifest` | unchanged: `owner`, `run_id`, `pair_id`, `contract_fingerprint`, `pair_v`, `evidence_digest`, `uid` | carries no cost fields; the version-2 replay manifest (#1881) freezes `pairCostBindings` |

**Contracts for later children (#1874, for #1877, #1879 and later).**
Hyperliquid `tid` and OKX `billId` are the proposed venue fill ids; #1877 verifies and supplies them.

| Name | Go type or signature | JSON or column | Rule |
|---|---|---|---|
| `pairConfirmedFill` | `FillID string`, `VenueTimeMs int64`, `BaseQty string`, `NativeNotional string`, `EvidenceHash string` | `fill_id`, `venue_time_ms`, `base_qty`, `native_notional`, `evidence_hash` | canonical decimals above 0; `venue_time_ms` 0 means no venue time; `sealed()` sets the hash of the canonical JSON with `evidence_hash` empty |
| `pairFeeFill` | the five execution fields plus `FeeCurrency string`, `FeeAmount string` | adds `fee_currency`, `fee_amount` | signed canonical decimal (zero and rebates allowed); currency from the frozen member `fee_currencies`; execution fields equal the execution record |
| `basketVenueOutcome` | `FillCoverage []pairConfirmedFill`, `FeeCoverage []pairFeeFill` | `fill_coverage,omitempty`, `fee_coverage,omitempty` | `FeeEvidenceVersion` = `len(FeeCoverage)`, `FeeEvidenceID` = `pairFeeEvidenceID(action)`, `FeePresent` true; `FeeCurrency` and `FeeAmount` are optional summaries that must agree when `FeeCurrency` is set |
| `pairFeeEvidenceID` | `func pairFeeEvidenceID(a *basketAction) string` | | `pfe-` plus the first 32 hex of SHA-256 over partition, member, provider, account, instrument and client order id |
| `pairScopedFillID` | `func pairScopedFillID(a *basketAction, fillID string) string` | | the order scope, a `|`, then the venue fill id; sort and ledger key |
| `basketAction` | `AcceptedAtMs int64`, `FillCoverageJSON string`, `FeeCoverageJSON string`, `FeeFinal bool`, `SettlementJSON string` | `accepted_at_ms INTEGER 0`, `fill_coverage_json TEXT ''`, `fee_coverage_json TEXT ''`, `fee_final INTEGER 0`, `settlement_json TEXT ''` | guarded as in Storage Ownership; empty means unknown |
| `pairLedger` | `V int`, `Fills []pairLedgerFill`, `Fees []pairLedgerFee` | `v` (1), `fills`, `fees` | append-only per action |
| `pairLedgerFill` | `ScopedFillID`, `FillID`, `EvidenceHash`, `SelectedTimeMs`, `TimeSource` (`venue_time` or `accepted_at`), `SettlementAtMs`, `BaseQty`, `NativeNotional`, `PriceCurrency`, `Conversion *pairConversionEvidence`, `RateUSD`, `NotionalUSD float64`, `Booking pairLedgerRow` | snake_case of each name | `Conversion` is nil only for USD |
| `pairLedgerFee` | `ScopedFillID`, `FillID`, `EvidenceHash`, `SelectedTimeMs`, `SettlementAtMs`, `FeeCurrency`, `FeeAmount`, `Conversion`, `RateUSD`, `FeeUSD float64`, `Mode` (`with_quantity` or `correction`), `Target pairLedgerRow` | snake_case | `FeeUSD` is signed and never recomputed |
| `pairLedgerRow` | `TradeTsMs int64`, `TradeTs string`, `OrderID string`, `IsClose bool`, `ClosedPositionID string` | `trade_ts_ms`, `trade_ts`, `order_id`, `is_close`, `closed_position_id` | the saved booking row a fee correction targets |
| `pairConfirmedFee` | `Present bool`, `Covered bool`, `Currency string`, `Amount float64`, `Conversion pairConversionEvidence`, `EvidenceID string`, `EvidenceVersion int64`, `DeltaUSD float64`, `Parts []pairLedgerFee` | in-process only | `pairBookFill.Fee`; `Present` makes a zero or negative `DeltaUSD` the booked fee; `Covered` (fail-closed, set only when every fill booked in that row has a fee record) selects `userfills`, else the row saves `userfills_pending`; `Parts` keeps each fill's conversion when one booking spans rates |
| `withPairConfirmedFeeScope` | `func withPairConfirmedFeeScope(s *StrategyState, fee pairConfirmedFee, fn func()) error` | | the explicit-fee seam for native member closes (#1914); refuses a non-finite delta; `strategyExecutionFee` and `strategyExecutionFeeSource` read it; ordinary callers never set it |
| `SettlementConversion` | `func (s *pairFrozenEvidenceSource) SettlementConversion(ctx context.Context, m pairContractMember, currency string, fillAtMs, settlementAtMs int64) (pairConversionEvidence, error)` | | the two-time selection above; callers check the result with `pairCheckSettlementConversion`; it is not part of `pairEvidenceSource` |
| obligations | `fill_coverage`, `fee_unconfirmed`, `fee_invalid`, `fee_incomplete`, `fee_conflict`, `settlement_conversion`, `conversion_unavailable`, `legacy_settlement` | `progress_json` obligations | each counts as incomplete accounting (`pairSettlementObligationKind`), keeps a held action queried, and is recovery evidence under `pairRecoveryObligation` (the startup contract-change and wrong-file refusals) until its action ends |

**Pair funding (#1888).** Each research pair member is charged its own venue's funding by one pair lane under the coordinator and the partition save (`scheduler/pair_funding*.go`, Python mirror `shared_tools/pair_funding.py`).
The legacy Hyperliquid owner skips every pair member (`pairFundingOwned(sc)` = `isPairMember(sc)` in `syncPaperFundingEligibility`, `planPaperFunding`, `runPaperFundingAccounting` and `paperFundingCoins`), so a pair member has one funding owner and eligibility sync never creates, deletes or charges its state; `paperFundingEligible` and the quote owner are unchanged.
The controlled live-shaped fixture has no funding lane: a venue-confirmed charge never receives a modeled paper charge, and a fixture admission that supplies a funding source is refused because attributed live funding ingestion has no owner in this build.

- **Source.** `pairResearchDeps.Funding pairFundingSource` is required for a research admission (nil or any other type refuses before any write). Only `*pairFrozenFundingSource` (`newPairFrozenFundingSource(doc, wantDigest)`: digest, strict canonical decoding and every record checked) and `*pairRecordedFundingSource` (`newPairRecordedFundingSource(sourceID, anchorMs)` plus `Append(batch []byte) error`) are accepted. The lane never fetches: it calls `Collect` outside `mu` under one `collection_timeout_ms` context and rechecks the pair snapshot after relocking; a collection error alerts once and changes nothing.
- **Binding.** `pairFundingBinding{v: 1, contract_fingerprint, members (full keys sorted), source_kind, source_id, digest (frozen document digest, else ""), anchor_ms (recorded anchor, else 0), rules_digest, rules}` is built at admission (`bindFunding`), written as `funding_binding` into `pair-research.json` before any database write and into `pairProgress.FundingBinding` (JSON `funding_binding`, beside `cost_evidence`, frozen at the first commit; `pairProgressVersion` stays 1).
  Resume compares canonical bytes before any write and refuses a missing manifest binding (a pre-funding run needs a new run), a changed frozen digest, source, anchor, member set or venue rule table (`rules_digest`) with the files unchanged; storage load (`bindPairFundingBinding`) refuses saved pair state without a binding; every pair write (`writePairTx`, `reserveBasketRebalanceRow`) refuses to add, remove or replace it (`checkSavedPairFundingBindingTx`).
  A recorded source binds only its identity and anchor; its accepted batches are saved in `pair_funding_chain`, and resume reads them through an immutable snapshot open (`openPairFundingSnapshot`, before any writable open) and requires them to be a byte-identical prefix of the source's chain; a later batch may only append.
- **Coverage and visibility.** Funding windows are `(from_ms, through_ms]`. A coverage record proves that every settlement in each `proven` interval is among the rate records; a last returned record, an empty page, an exhausted request allowance or a timeout never proves anything alone. A read at cutoff C uses only records with `visible_ms <= C`; a coverage record whose proven interval contains a rate first visible after the coverage record is refused, and a recorded batch cannot add a rate inside earlier proven coverage. Held exposure (from the inventory history up to `min(C, inventory_through)`) that no proven interval covers is a `funding_gap` obligation per member; it never becomes a proven zero.
- **Venue rules** (`pairFundingRules`; a rule change needs a new rule version and refuses resume through `rules_digest`; an unknown rule leaves the event incomplete):

| `rule_version` | Venue | Rate | Price | Formula | Same-time order | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `hyperliquid_hourly_oracle_v1` | Hyperliquid, USDC | `fundingHistory.fundingRate` | `oracle` | `native_amount = -(signed_base_qty × native_mark × rate)` | inventory strictly before `event_ms`; assessment window 0 | Hyperliquid docs: "The funding rate on Hyperliquid is paid every hour"; "the funding payment at the end of the interval is position_size * oracle_price * funding_rate ... the spot oracle price is used ... not the mark price"; positive rate: "the long position will pay the short position" |
| `okx_swap_realized_mark_v1` | OKX, USDT | `funding-rate-history.realizedRate` ("Actual funding rate") | `mark` | the same, with `signed_base_qty` = contracts × contract size | inventory strictly before `event_ms`; an inventory change whose possible time lies in `[event_ms, event_ms + 60000)` holds the event (`assessment_window_fill`) | OKX help: "Funding fee = Position value × Funding rate", "Position value = Number of contracts × Contract size × Contract multiplier × Mark price", "When the funding rate is positive, traders with long positions pay", default every 8 hours "unless specified otherwise (i.e. every 1, 2 or 4 hours)", "The actual fee assessment may take up to a minute" |

  Neither venue states a rounding rule, so the exact decimal product is kept; no schedule is assumed (each settlement record is one event); another venue's rate never replaces a member's own.
- **Event-time inventory.** The perps book owner records every pair member book change: `mutatePaperPerpsBookCoins` calls `pairInventoryTrack` (via `StrategyState.pairFunding`, set at the runtime link) before the legacy helper, and the append-only `pair_funding_inventory` history keeps each signed base delta with `event_ms`, `event_latest_ms` (equal for an exact time), `visible_ms`, origin (`coordinator`, `native`, `unexplained`), action and fill identity, the position identity after the change, post quantity and contract size.
  A row whose post quantity keeps the sign of the book before the change keeps the earlier position identity; a row that opens or flips takes the new one, and a flat row has none.
  Coordinator settlements pass `pairFillContext` (`withPairFillContext`) from the outcome's `fills` (`basketVenueOutcome.Fills []pairVenueFill{FillID "fill_id", BaseQty "base_qty", EventMs "event_ms", VisibleMs "visible_ms"}`: unique printable ids, finite positive quantities summing to `cum_qty`, event at or after `accepted_at_ms`, visibility at or before `observed_at_ms`, earlier fills never changed or dropped); without fills the delta's time is the range `[accepted_at_ms or submitted_at_ms, observed_at_ms]`.
  Native changes take the book clock; a book change outside the helper is reconciled each step and in each saving write as an `unexplained` range since the last observation.
  Funding at T uses rows with `event_latest_ms < T`; a row with `event_ms < T <= event_latest_ms` makes the event `unresolvable` (`fill_time_unknown`).
  Inventory is complete through `min(last observation, for each unresolved member action: coverage_through_ms when everything confirmed is applied, else submitted_at_ms - 1)`; an event needs it through `max(T, T + window - 1)` (`inventory_incomplete` otherwise).
  A history row earlier than an applied event opens a durable `funding_integrity` obligation with recovery, frozen increases and a CRITICAL alert; the applied charge is never rewritten, and the obligation stays saved but does not hold lifecycle completion.
  Load refuses a history that does not sum to the saved book.
- **Settlement** (`stepPairFunding`, after reconciliation and exception detection, before lifecycle checks; no new charge while `SaveBlocked`, `needsDurable` or a staged application exists).
  An event settles once it is visible, its assessment window has passed, inventory is complete, the inventory before T is nonzero (proven zero writes nothing, or turns an earlier incomplete row `void`), the rule is known and serves the member's provider and settlement currency, coverage proves `(max(exposure stretch start, previous rate), T]`, one price of the rule's kind at T is visible, and a conversion from the bound frozen evidence document is valid at T and observed and received by the cutoff (`FundingConversion`: `conversion_invalid_at_event` and `conversion_unavailable_by_cutoff` are separate reasons; `pairEligibleAt` is not used).
  The USD amount is converted once; cash changes by it and one trade row is recorded (`Side`/`TradeType` `funding`, `PnLGross`, `RealizedPnL` = USD amount, `Timestamp` = T, `PositionID` = the identity of the last inventory row before T, never the position open at settle time, `ExchangeOrderID` `pair_funding:<rule_version>:<event_id>`, the process cost stamp); `RecordTradeResult`, daily PnL, consecutive losses and the circuit breaker are untouched.
  The member book save commits the cash, the row, the applied event, the inventory history and the obligation changes in one transaction; a failed save keeps `needsDurable` and retries the same staged application, and a restart re-evaluates from the saved state, so each event changes cash once.
  Missing evidence writes a durable `incomplete` row with its reason and a `funding_incomplete` obligation; lifecycle completion, `pairStartupErrors` (changed contract), ordinary-load refusal and removed-pair refusal treat funding obligations as open accounting.
  Funding gaps never reuse the `funding_venue_gaps` venue-absent rule.
  An event that no future evidence can settle, because the immutable inventory history is ambiguous at T (`fill_time_unknown`), changes inside the assessment window (`assessment_window_fill`) or names no position identity before T (`position_identity_unknown`), is saved `unresolvable` once inventory is complete through its need (or, since #1927, once its assessment interval intersects a native funding hold interval (open `[FromMs, infinity)`, bounded `[FromMs, ThroughMs]`, reason `native_decision_unproven`, one alert per hold)): no charge, no obligation, one CRITICAL alert, and the detail gives the possible signed base position range and, when the rule and price are known, the native charge range.
  An unresolvable event never holds lifecycle completion; holds that only wait for evidence (coverage, price, conversion, inventory, rule) keep their obligation.
  Source hashes are remembered per rate identity, keyed by the hashed event time and rate, and seeded at load from every applied event after its saved source hash is rechecked (a mismatch refuses resume), so a cycle with no new records computes no source hash for an applied event.
  Each inventory row keeps its parsed quantity, the member keeps its running total, and held exposure is built by one sorted sweep and cached until a row is appended (also a late earlier row), so a cycle with no new rows parses no earlier row and rebuilds nothing.
- **Negative cash.** `ValidateStateWithPairOwner(state, strategies, owner)` keeps negative cash only for members of a valid research admission's contract that has a funding binding and member cost bindings (paper partition, matching `pair_id`); `ValidateState` is the nil-owner form, so a `pair_id` alone grants nothing, ordinary OKX strategies keep their clamp and legacy Hyperliquid eligibility keeps its exemption. A pair host runs it after `LoadBaskets` with the admission.
- **OKX range reader.** `OKXExchangeAdapter.get_funding_history_range(symbol, start_ms, end_ms, page_limit=100, max_requests=16, deadline_ms=30000) -> dict` maps a coin or `<COIN>-USDT-SWAP` to the native instrument, validates integer arguments, `page_limit` in [1, 400] and `max_requests` in [1, 64] (both refused before any request), and calls `publicGetPublicFundingRateHistory({"instId", "after": cursor, "limit"})` directly (no market load, no ccxt filter).
  The cursor starts at `end_ms + 1` and moves to the oldest `fundingTime` of each page (OKX docs: `after` returns "records earlier than the requested fundingTime", `limit` "The maximum is 400", history "up to three months"); a row at or after the cursor, a conflicting duplicate, a non-text `realizedRate`, a foreign instrument or more rows than the limit stops the read.
  It returns `{provider, inst_id, rate_field: "realizedRate", requested: {from_ms, through_ms}, events: [{event_ms, inst_id, realized_rate, funding_rate, method, formula_type}] (raw text), proven, gaps, complete, requests, stop_reason, error}`: proven runs from `start_ms` (when a record at or before it was reached) or the oldest record minus one to the newest record; a short page (`history_exhausted`), the request bound (`request_limit`), the deadline, a transport (`transport_error`, ccxt network errors) or venue failure (`venue_error`) or malformed page leaves a gap; the interval after the newest record is always `after_last_record`; any error clears events and proves nothing.
  It differs from the Hyperliquid adapter's lenient same-name list method.
  `get_funding_history` is unchanged.
  `pair_funding.okx_range_records(key, result, visible_ms, provenance, evidence_id)` turns a result into rate records and one coverage record for a frozen document; Go `pairFundingFromHyperliquidCoverage` does the same for `hlFundingRecordsCoverage`, proving only through its last returned record.

**Research funding oracle (#1934).** An isolated paper run may opt in to a research Hyperliquid funding price with `scenario.funding_policy`; without a policy every legacy rule, serializer, digest and wire version stays byte-identical.
`scheduler/testdata/pair_funding_legacy/` pins the legacy rules digest, binding v1, document v1, batch v1 and event v2 bytes captured from the base serializers, and `TestBasketPairFundingPythonLegacyCanonicalGoldens` also settles, saves and reloads a legacy event through the real coordinator and compares its `event_json` byte for byte.

- **Policy.** `pairFundingPolicyV1{version, member_rules, window_ms, max_print_offset_ms, prepare_lead_ms, from_ms, drain_through_ms}` (JSON names as written) has every field required and these exact values: version 1, `window_ms` 5000, `max_print_offset_ms` 60000, `prepare_lead_ms` 30000, bounds equal to the collection bounds, the Hyperliquid member selecting `hyperliquid_hourly_oracle_receipt_research_v1` and the OKX member `okx_swap_realized_mark_v1`.
  A null policy, a null, float, string, missing or extra field, another member, another rule or other bounds is refused before the state directory is claimed (`validatePairPaperSpec`), and binding (`pairFundingValidatePolicyForContract`) checks the same mapping against the admitted contract.
  `policy_digest` is the SHA-256 of the canonical policy (`pairFundingPolicyDigest`, Python `policy_digest`).
  The timing values are fixed contract values without a mainnet measurement; another timing tuple needs another rule version.
- **Selection.** `pairFundingResearchRules` (Python `RESEARCH_RULES`) is a separate catalog; `pairFundingRules`, `pairFundingRulesDigest`, the legacy provider lookup and Python `RULES` are unchanged.
  With a policy, `pairFundingSelectedRule` returns the member's selected rule; a rate with another rule is kept as evidence and reaches `rule_unsupported` at the existing rule step.
  The research rule keeps the legacy formula and currency, price kind `oracle_receipt_research`, assessment window 0 and the single evidence statement "research receipt-time observation; timing bounds do not establish venue settlement equality or oracle-update age".
- **Hour mapping.** `T = event_ms - event_ms mod 3600000`; an event uses an observation for `T` only when `0 <= event_ms - T <= 60000` and `from_ms <= T <= drain_through_ms` (`pairFundingResearchTarget`), so a print 60001 ms or more after the hour stays `price_missing`.
  Event id, event time, position identity, inventory, conversion validity and coverage stay at the real print time; no rate is rounded or moved.
- **Observation.** An observation is valid only when `T <= request_ms <= receipt_ms <= D` with `D = min(T + 5000, drain_through_ms)`, `at_ms = visible_ms = receipt_ms` (the raw local receipt; there is no venue timestamp and no sample field), the price is a finite positive canonical decimal and the provenance is fixed.
  `evidence_id` is `hyperliquid-oracle:<instrument>:<T>:<receipt_ms>` and `attempt_id` is `hyperliquid-oracle-attempt:` plus the SHA-256 of canonical `{key, target_event_ms, policy_digest}`.
  Exactly one distinct eligible record hash for the full key and `T` visible by the cutoff selects it (`pairFundingObservationFor`); two distinct hashes, also at an equal price or another receipt time, are `conflicting_evidence`, and no nearest, earliest or best value is ever chosen.
  A price may arrive before its rate; an observation batch never proves or extends rate coverage.
- **After application.** Each policy step rechecks every applied and staged research event against the visible observations, also without a new rate and after a restart (`pairFundingAppliedRef` carries the policy digest, observation hash and target); a second hash opens the durable `funding_integrity` hold with recovery, frozen increases and a CRITICAL alert in that step and changes no prior cash, trade or event.
  `appliedVenueRules` and `pendingVenueRules` (keyed by full key and venue event id) refuse a second rule's charge for one venue event before staging and again in `stagePairFunding`: the collision adds the integrity hold, records no trade and changes no cash, and the database unique index stays the last guard.
  `pairFundingValidateSelectedEvent` checks a version 3 event against the admitted policy, the selected observation (by record hash and every copied field), the rate and the arithmetic at staging, inside the save transaction, at load and in the export checks; a legacy source keeps the legacy event checks only.
- **Reader.** `HyperliquidExchangeAdapter.public_oracle_observation(symbol)` needs the public-only mainnet adapter, makes one `metaAndAssetCtxs` read, requires exactly one universe entry for the symbol and the context of the same response, and returns the canonical `oraclePx` text, refusing a missing, duplicate, malformed, bool, float, NaN, infinite, padded, zero or negative value; it never uses cached metadata, the mark price, a private call or an exchange timestamp.
  `fetch_pair_history.py --kind=funding_oracle --provider=hyperliquid --instrument --member-key-json --target-event-ms --not-before-ms --deadline-ms --funding-policy-json --policy-digest` builds the adapter first, then waits in sleeps of at most 50 ms until `time.time_ns() // 1000000 >= T`, refuses a backward clock and a pass of `D`, sets `request_ms` right before and `receipt_ms` right after the one read, refuses times outside `[T, D]`, and prints `{ok, kind, provider, instrument, host, observation, request_ms, receipt_ms}`.
  `--kind=funding` takes `--funding-policy-json`, `--policy-digest` and `--rule-version` together or not at all and then seals a version 2 batch with no observation; the legacy funding and candle arguments are unchanged and refuse the new flags.
  `pairHistoryHLOracleProbeArgv`, `pairHistoryHLResearchFundingProbeArgv` and `pairHistoryOKXResearchFundingProbeArgv` are built at package init from a canonical probe policy and run in both probe vectors (`probeCheckScripts` and `paperProbes`).

| Wire object | Version | Fields (all required) |
|---|---|---|
| `pairFundingPolicyV1` | `version` 1 | `version`, `member_rules`, `window_ms`, `max_print_offset_ms`, `prepare_lead_ms`, `from_ms`, `drain_through_ms` |
| `pairFundingOracleObservation` | `v` 2 | `v`, `key`, `evidence_id`, `attempt_id`, `policy_digest`, `price_kind` (`oracle_receipt_research`), `target_event_ms`, `request_ms`, `receipt_ms`, `at_ms`, `visible_ms`, `time_basis` (`local_request_receipt`), `price`, `provenance` (`hyperliquid_info_metaAndAssetCtxs_oraclePx_receipt_research_v1@https://api.hyperliquid.xyz`), `record_hash` |
| `pairFundingDocumentV2` | `v` 2 | `v`, `source_id`, `policy`, `policy_digest`, `rates`, `prices`, `observations`, `coverage`; observations sort by key, target, receipt, evidence id, record hash |
| `pairFundingBatchV2` | `v` 2 | `v`, `source_id`, `seq`, `prev_hash`, `policy_digest`, `rates`, `prices`, `observations`, `coverage`, `batch_hash`; an oracle batch holds one observation and nothing else, a rate batch no observation; one source accepts one version family |
| `pairFundingEventV3` | `v` 3 | every v2 event field plus `policy_digest`, `observation_evidence_id`, `observation_record_hash`, `observation_target_event_ms`, `observation_request_ms`, `observation_receipt_ms`, `observation_time_basis`; `price_hash` = observation hash, `native_mark` = observation price; only the research rule writes it, OKX stays v2 |
| `pairFundingBindingV2` | `v` 2 | every v1 binding field plus `policy` and `policy_digest`; `rules` and `rules_digest` cover the sorted selected rule table |

  The in-memory `pairFunding*Value` envelopes (document, batch, event, binding) carry the legacy projection and the authoritative research value, refuse `json.Marshal` with an error that names the type, and are written only through `pairFunding*ValueBytes` or `pairFundingBindingCanonical`; decoders dispatch on the integer `v` and refuse unknown versions.

**Contracts for later children (#1881, #1882).**

| Name | Go type or signature (JSON) | Rule |
|---|---|---|
| `pairFundingSource` | `Kind() string`, `SourceID() string`, `Collect(ctx, pairFundingRequest) (pairFundingCollected, error)`, `NextVisibleMs(key pairEvidenceKey, afterMs int64) (int64, bool)` | `pairFundingRequest{Key pairEvidenceKey; FromMs, ThroughMs, CutoffMs int64}` returns records with `FromMs < event/at <= ThroughMs` (coverage overlapping the window) and `visible_ms <= CutoffMs`, sorted; `NextVisibleMs` is the earliest record visibility after `afterMs` for a schedule wake |
| `pairFundingRate` | `v int "v"` (1), `key pairEvidenceKey "key"`, `event_id string "event_id"` (`<provider>:<instrument>:<event_ms>`), `rule_version string`, `event_ms int64`, `visible_ms int64` (≥ event), `rate pairDecimal`, `provenance string`, `record_hash string` | source hash (`pairFundingSourceHash`, Python `source_hash`) = SHA-256 of canonical `{key, event_id, rule_version, event_ms, rate}` decides equal duplicate or conflict; `record_hash` covers the whole record |
| `pairFundingPrice` | `v`, `key`, `evidence_id`, `price_kind` (`mark`, `oracle`), `at_ms`, `visible_ms` (≥ at), `price pairDecimal` (> 0), `provenance`, `record_hash` | the event uses one price of the rule's kind with `at_ms = event_ms` |
| `pairFundingCoverageRecord` | `v`, `key`, `evidence_id`, `requested_from_ms`, `requested_through_ms`, `proven []pairFundingInterval{from_ms, through_ms}`, `gaps []pairCoverageGap{from_ms, through_ms, reason}`, `visible_ms`, `provenance`, `record_hash` | intervals sorted, disjoint, inside the request; proven and gaps never overlap |
| `pairFundingDocument` | `v` (1), `source_id`, `rates`, `prices`, `coverage` | canonical bytes (`pairFundingDocumentBytes`, Python `document_bytes`) sort by key then time; one identity per document; at most 65536 records per kind and 16 MiB |
| `pairFundingBatch` | `v` (1), `source_id`, `seq` (from 1), `prev_hash` ("" for seq 1), `rates`, `prices`, `coverage`, `batch_hash` | append-only chain; `Append` refuses a gap in `seq`, a wrong `prev_hash`, a conflicting identity, a repeated evidence id and a rate inside earlier proven coverage |
| `pairFundingEvent` (v2) | `v`, `key`, `event_id`, `rule_version`, `event_ms`, `visible_ms` (latest input visibility), `signed_base_qty`, `native_mark`, `rate`, `currency`, `native_amount`, `usd_amount` (all `pairDecimal`), `conversion pairConversionEvidence`, `coverage pairFundingCoverage`, `source_hash`, `price_hash`, `evidence_hash` | written once when applied and immutable; `pairDecodeFundingEvent` and Python `decode_event` recheck identity, rule, currency, amounts, conversion validity at T, coverage and hash |
| `pairFundingCoverage` | `v` (1), `key`, `requested_from_ms`, `requested_through_ms` (= event), `proven`, `gaps` (empty when applied), `inventory_through_ms`, `source_kind`, `source_id`, `cutoff_ms` | named `pairFundingCoverage` because #1871's quote `pairCoverage` already exists |
| `fundingSource` | `func (a *pairResearchAdmission) fundingSource() pairFundingSource` | the bound source beside `evidenceSource()` and `quoteSource()`; nil for the live-shaped fixture |
| `pairProgress.FundingBinding` | `json.RawMessage`, JSON `funding_binding` | frozen at the first commit beside `cost_evidence`; the manifest carries the same bytes |
| `ValidateStateWithPairOwner` | `func ValidateStateWithPairOwner(state *AppState, strategies []StrategyConfig, owner *pairResearchAdmission)` | the replay host (#1881) and paper host (#1882) call it after `LoadBaskets` instead of `ValidateState` |
| Funding rows | `TradeTypeFunding`, `Side` `funding`, `ExchangeOrderID` `pair_funding:<rule_version>:<event_id>`, process cost stamp | excluded from loss counters; a replay exports them from `trades` and the applied rows from `pair_funding_events` |

**Management-only members.** A member check runs with `--pair-member-json='{"pair_id","member_id","provider","instrument","mode":"management_only"}'` (Hyperliquid batch slot `pair_member`): native closes, ATR, indicators and protection run, no entry evaluator and no open-strategy close fallback run, and the output must echo the request exactly or Go records a script error and pair entry holds while trusted protection keeps its authority. Go drops every position-increasing member signal on the Hyperliquid step (after the check and again after admission), on both OKX routes and on batched results. `check_okx.py` accepts the request and, with `--management-stdin`, reads frozen management input (version 1: at least 30 candles and a mark) without constructing an exchange client (`runOKXPairManagementCheck`); its basket-member refusal is unchanged. Robinhood and TopStep accept the flag for probe shape and refuse it at run time.

**Interfaces for issues #1863 and #1864.** The venue seam is `pairVenue`: `basketVenue` plus `Virtual()`, `ExecutionModel()` (Member cost bindings); since #1876 the venue seam carries no valuation, and every valuation comes from the coordinator's published view (Pair valuation view below). `Submit` and `Query` take the saved action (purpose, base and native quantity, price bound, acceptance deadline, account, native instrument, client id); an outcome carries `cum_qty`, `cum_notional`, `accepted_at_ms`, `terminal`, coverage, optional per-fill `fills` (Pair funding below), the echoed identity, fee evidence (`fee_present`, `fee_currency`, signed `fee_amount`, `fee_evidence_id`, `fee_evidence_version`) and the #1874 `fill_coverage` and `fee_coverage` records, and `never_sent`, `absent` and `open` outcomes. A `Submit` at or after the saved deadline must send nothing and answer `never_sent`.
Since #1877 `pairVenue` also has `Position(ctx, member, nowMs)` for live-shaped venues (Pair bounds, transport and the position barrier below).

**Contract version 2 (#1869).** `pairContractVersion` is 2 and every field below is part of the contract fingerprint.
A saved version-1 pair contract is refused with an explicit unsupported-version error and is never converted: on resume the run manifest's `pair_v` (absent means 1) is checked before its fingerprint, and storage load (`parseBasketContract`) checks `pair_v` first and then refuses duplicate keys, invalid UTF-8 and unknown fields in saved pair-contract bytes (saved bytes need not be canonical).
Version-1 basket contracts keep their bytes and fingerprints.
Reload refuses any change, a pair member's leverage included, and startup refuses a changed contract while a position, an unapplied fill, a funding gap or recovery evidence remains.

| Owner | Go field | Go type | JSON | Rule |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `PairConfig`, `pairContractBody` | `BookNeed` | `pairBookNeed{Depth int; WindowMs int64; Capacity int}` | `book_need` {`depth`, `window_ms`, `capacity`} | depth [1, 20], window_ms [1, 900000], capacity [1, 4096], through `pairBookNeed.validate()` (the same bounds `contractBookNeed(window, capacity, depth).validate()` applies; only sealed members build that feed need, #1871) |
| `PairMemberConfig`, `pairContractMember` | `Source` | `string` | `source` | Hyperliquid `sealed_feed`, `archive` or `public_snapshot`; OKX `public_snapshot` |
| `PairMemberConfig`, `pairContractMember` | `ModeledDeliveryMs` | `int64` | `modeled_delivery_ms` | required; `archive` [1, 2147483647], every other source explicit 0 |
| `PairMemberConfig`, `pairContractMember` | `CostPolicy` | `fillCostPolicy` | `cost_policy` | raw keys exactly `taker_fee_pct`, `slippage_pct`, `fill_role`, `spread` (`model_version` and `policy_id` refused even at zero); `validateFillCostRates`; the contract freezes `rates()` (it serializes `model_version: 0` and no `policy_id`) and never binds the process-global cost policy; #1887 resolves and uses it (Member cost bindings) |
| `pairContractMember` | `Leverage` | `float64` | `leverage` | the member's `EffectiveExchangeLeverage`; no raw config field |
| `pairOperationalLimits` | `QuoteAgeMs`, `CollectionTimeoutMs`, `ReceiveSkewMs` | `int64` | `quote_age_ms`, `collection_timeout_ms`, `receive_skew_ms` | [1, 2147483647]; timeout and skew not above the quote age |
| `pairOperationalLimits` | `EnvelopeMaxBytes` | `int` | `envelope_max_bytes` | [1, 4194304] |
| `pairOperationalLimits` | `ProtectiveWidthBps` | `float64` | `protective_width_bps` | finite, above 0, below 10000 |
| `pairInstrumentMeta` | `PriceCurrency` | `string` | `price_currency` | Hyperliquid `USDC`; OKX `USDT` |
| `pairInstrumentMeta` | `PriceRule` | `string` | `price_rule` | Hyperliquid `hyperliquid_perps_v1`; OKX `fixed_tick_v1` |
| `pairInstrumentMeta` | `SzDecimals` | `int` | `sz_decimals` | Hyperliquid [0, 12] (`hlLotMetadataMaxSzDecimals`, zero valid); OKX explicit 0 |
| `pairInstrumentMeta` | `TickStepText`, `LotStepText` | `string` | `tick_step_text`, `lot_step_text` | OKX: positive plain decimals equal, as exact rationals, to the shortest decimal of `tick_step` and `lot_step`; Hyperliquid: empty |
| `pairInstrumentMeta` | `FeeCurrencies` | `[]string` | `fee_currencies` | nonempty, unique, upper-case; the contract serializes a sorted copy |

Raw kinds are checked at every nested level before typed decoding (`pairRawPairErrors`, called by `pairResearchRawPair` and `pairConfigErrors`).
Required fields refuse missing and `null`; numbers refuse booleans and strings and must be finite; integers also refuse fractions and exponents; booleans (`linear` included) and strings need their JSON kinds; `fee_currencies` needs an array of strings; unknown keys are refused at the pair, member, meta, cost-policy, book and limits levels.

**Currencies and units (#1869).** An OKX member needs `quote_currency`, `settlement_currency` and `price_currency` `USDT` and the instrument `<UNDERLYING>-USDT-SWAP`: every supported OKX order, close, query, candle, funding and balance route selects USDT swaps, and the legacy position reader keeps only the bare coin while the strict pair read (#1877) keys the exact instrument.
A Hyperliquid member is quoted, priced and settled in `USDC`.
No stablecoin is assumed equal to USD: the frozen evidence carries a conversion schedule for every required currency of a member (`pairRequiredConversionCurrencies`: the settlement, price and fee currencies), `USD` itself needs no record and a `USD` conversion record is refused.
Account collateral must be the settlement currency.
Hyperliquid native quantity is base units with contract size 1; OKX native quantity is contracts; base = contracts × contract_size, computed once in Go at the transport and position boundary (#1871, #1877 and the position and accounting children apply this rule).
Python scripts emit native contracts and native contract notional with an explicit unit and contract identity.

**Evidence records (#1869).** `scheduler/pair_evidence.go` owns these shapes; `shared_tools/pair_evidence.py` mirrors them (`SCHEMAS`).
Decimal fields use `pairDecimal` (a `string` type: `MarshalJSON` normalizes, `UnmarshalJSON` accepts canonical form only).

| Go type | JSON fields, all required | Rules |
|---|---|---|
| `pairEvidenceKey` | `partition`, `provider`, `account_key`, `instrument`, `member_id` (strings) | canonical risk partition (`live`, `paper`, `paper:<id>`), a pair provider, nonempty trimmed account, instrument and member; `pairMemberKey.evidenceKey()` and `pairEvidenceKey.memberKey()` convert and validate |
| `pairEvidenceStamp` (embedded anonymously, flattened) | `v` int, `key`, `evidence_id`, `evidence_hash`, `observed_ms`, `first_receipt_ms`, `valid_from_ms`, `valid_through_ms` (int64), `provenance` | `v` 2; id 1..128 and provenance 1..256 printable ASCII; no negative time; observed not after first receipt; `valid_from_ms` not after `valid_through_ms`; `evidence_hash` is the record hash |
| `pairCapabilityEvidence` | stamp, `meta` (`pairWireMeta`), `metadata_generation` (uint64) | provenance is the provider derivation id; generation at least 1, never decreasing within a key, and equal generations carry equal `meta` |
| `pairWireMeta` | `native_unit`, `base_asset`, `contract_size`*, `quote_currency`, `settlement_currency`, `price_currency`, `tick_step`*, `lot_step`*, `min_qty`*, `min_notional`*, `trading_status`, `linear` (bool), `max_leverage`*, `price_rule`, `sz_decimals` (int), `tick_step_text`, `lot_step_text`, `fee_currencies` (sorted []string); * = `pairDecimal` | public instrument rules only, per the derivation table below |
| `pairConversionEvidence` | stamp, `currency`, `rate_usd`* | upper-case currency other than `USD`; rate above zero |
| `pairAccountEvidence` | stamp, `position_mode`, `margin_mode`, `leverage`*, `collateral_currency`, `equity`*, `free_collateral`*, `margin_used`* | leverage above zero; free collateral and margin used not negative |
| `pairEvidenceDocument` | `v` (2), `capabilities`, `accounts`, `conversions` | one immutable research input; SHA-256 of its canonical bytes is the document digest |

**Field derivation table (#1869).** The provenance of a capability record is its versioned derivation id.
Go (`pairValidateWireMeta`, `pairExpectedWireMeta`, `pairCapabilityMatches`) and Python (`validate_wire_meta`, `hyperliquid_wire_meta`, `okx_wire_meta`, `expected_wire_meta`, `capability_matches`) apply the same table; the contract side is projected through the shortest decimal of each float and compared field for field.

| `pairWireMeta` field | Hyperliquid `hyperliquid_info_meta_v1`: POST `/info` `{"type":"meta"}`, entry resolved through `Info.name_to_asset` | OKX `okx_public_instrument_v1`: GET `/api/v5/public/instruments?instType=SWAP&instId=<instrument>` through the ccxt public client | Compared |
|---|---|---|---|
| `native_unit` | constant `base` | constant `contract` | yes |
| `base_asset` | entry `name` (the instrument) | `ctValCcy`, which must equal the `instFamily` base | yes |
| `contract_size` | constant `1` | `ctVal` | yes |
| `quote_currency` | constant `USDC` | `instFamily` quote | yes (OKX must be `USDT`) |
| `settlement_currency` | constant `USDC` | `settleCcy` | yes (OKX must be `USDT`) |
| `price_currency` | constant `USDC` | the quote currency | yes (OKX must be `USDT`) |
| `tick_step` | 10^-max(0, 6 − szDecimals): the finest increment; legal prices follow `price_rule` (at most 5 significant figures) | `tickSz` | yes |
| `lot_step` | 10^-szDecimals | `lotSz` | yes |
| `min_qty` | 10^-szDecimals (one lot) | `minSz` | yes |
| `min_notional` | named constant `10` (`MIN_ORDER_NOTIONAL_USD`, `pairHyperliquidMinNotional`) | constant `0` (no OKX minimum-notional rule) | yes |
| `trading_status` | `delisted` when `isDelisted` is true, else `live` | `live` when `state` is `live`, else the raw `state` | yes (the contract requires `live`) |
| `linear` | constant `true` | `ctType` is `linear` | yes |
| `max_leverage` | entry `maxLeverage` (a positive integer) | `lever` | yes |
| `price_rule` | constant `hyperliquid_perps_v1` | constant `fixed_tick_v1` | yes |
| `sz_decimals` | entry `szDecimals`: an integer equal to `asset_to_sz_decimals[asset]` for a main-universe perpetual; never the `_sz_decimals` fallback | constant `0` | yes |
| `tick_step_text`, `lot_step_text` | empty | `tickSz`, `lotSz` normalized | yes |
| `fee_currencies` | [`USDC`] | [`settleCcy`] | yes |
| contract-only | `position_mode`, `margin_mode` and `leverage` are checked against account evidence; `initial_margin_rate` and `maintenance_margin_rate` are conservative model rates with no public field (1/maxLeverage is not a finite decimal for every Hyperliquid leverage; OKX tiers come from a separate position-tiers endpoint) and are never compared | same | no |

**Canonical writer (#1869).** Go `pairCanonicalJSON(v interface{}) ([]byte, error)`; Python `canonical_bytes(obj) -> bytes`, `evidence_hash(record) -> str`, `seal_record`, `document_bytes` and `normalize_decimal`.
Output is compact UTF-8 with every object's keys sorted byte-wise (Go struct fields included); integers keep full precision (`-0` is written `0`); a non-integer number or a binary float is refused, so schema decimals are strings; booleans stay booleans.
Strings escape `"`, `\`, `\n`, `\r` and `\t` in short form, other control characters as `\u00xx`, `<`, `>` and `&` as `\u003c`, `\u003e` and `\u0026`, and U+2028 and U+2029 as `\u2028` and `\u2029`; all other characters stay raw UTF-8.
A canonical decimal matches `^-?(0|[1-9][0-9]*)(\.[0-9]*[1-9])?$` and is never `-0`; writers normalize schema decimals from plain or exponent input with redundant zeros, and only schema decimals are normalized (identifiers, provenance and step text keep their content).
A record hash is the lowercase SHA-256 of the canonical record with `evidence_hash` present as `""`; verification repeats it.
`pairEvidenceDocumentBytes` sorts fee currencies, capabilities and accounts by key (partition, provider, account_key, instrument, member_id, each compared byte-wise), first receipt, observation and evidence id, and conversions by key, currency, first receipt, observation and evidence id; other consumer schemas order their own arrays before calling the writer.
The strict readers (Go `pairStrictParse` with `pairDecodeEvidenceDocument`; Python `loads_strict`, `decode_record` and `decode_document`) refuse invalid UTF-8, duplicate keys (escaped spellings included), lone surrogate escapes, trailing values, wrong kinds, missing or unknown fields, unsupported versions and any bytes that differ from the canonical re-encoding, before typed decoding loses information.
Macro and basket hash formats are unchanged.

**Evidence source (#1869).** `pairEvidenceSource` has `Capability(ctx context.Context, m pairContractMember, refMs int64) (pairCapabilityEvidence, error)`, `Conversions(ctx, m, refMs) ([]pairConversionEvidence, error)` (one record per required currency, sorted by currency) and `Account(ctx, m, refMs) (pairAccountEvidence, error)`.
`pairResearchDeps.Evidence` is required; research admission accepts only `*pairFrozenEvidenceSource` from `newPairFrozenEvidenceSource(doc []byte, wantDigest string)`, which checks the digest, canonical bytes, versions, structure and every record hash.
`bind` runs inside `loadPairResearchConfig`, before the transport checks and before `checkStateDir` can write, and checks every record against the contract: partition and member identity, capability equality, account position mode and margin mode equal to the contract meta, account leverage equal to the frozen `leverage` as an exact rational, collateral equal to the settlement currency, and conversion currencies inside the required set; each member needs a capability, an account and a per-currency conversion schedule.
Because admission checks every record, a frozen document changes at run time only through time eligibility (gaps, expiry, future receipt); #1871 exercises its increase hold that way, and a mismatched-record hold needs another `pairEvidenceSource` implementation.
A read at `refMs` selects among records with `observed_ms`, `first_receipt_ms` and `valid_from_ms` at or before `refMs` and `valid_through_ms` at or after it, preferring the latest first receipt and then the latest observation; a tie with different content is an error (an equal-content tie returns the lowest evidence id); conversions are selected per currency; nothing substitutes current data, unit conversion or a private read; a cancelled context returns its error; records are returned as copies.
`Digest()` is saved as the manifest `evidence_digest`, and a resume needs the same digest before any write; the input document stays outside the run directory.
`(*pairResearchAdmission).evidenceSource()` exposes the bound source; consumers validate with `pairCapabilityMatches`, `pairAccountMatches`, `pairConversionMatches`, `pairRequiredConversionCurrencies` and `pairValidateWireMeta`.
#1871 collects outside `mu`, rechecks the pair snapshot and persists increase holds through pair progress; an evidence outage must not become a whole-step return before recovery queries and protective reductions, which keep their authority.

**Public capability reader (#1869).** `shared_scripts/fetch_pair_evidence.py --provider=hyperliquid|okx --instrument=<native> [--probe-only]` prints `{"ok":true,"provider","instrument","provenance","observed_ms","meta"}` and exits 0, or prints `{"ok":false,"error"}` and exits 1; `--probe-only` parses its arguments and exits before it loads an adapter or reaches the network.
It clears the Hyperliquid and OKX signing and account variables, loads each adapter by file path under its own module name and constructs it with `public_only=True` (Hyperliquid: no wallet, no metadata cache read or write, a failed read raises instead of the SDK fallback, strict `public_instrument_rules` through `name_to_asset`; OKX: no keys, `publicGetPublicInstruments`); egress pinning is unchanged.
Go `pairReadPublicCapability(ctx context.Context, key pairEvidenceKey, prev *pairCapabilityEvidence, validForMs int64) (pairCapabilityEvidence, error)` needs a caller deadline and spawns the reader through `spawnPythonProcessWithEnv` with those variables cleared and `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1`.
Its stamp: `observed_ms` is the reader clock right after the venue response (neither source carries a venue time), `first_receipt_ms` is the Go receive time and must not be earlier, `valid_from_ms` is `first_receipt_ms`, `valid_through_ms` is `first_receipt_ms + validForMs`, `provenance` is the derivation id, `evidence_id` is `<provenance>:<first_receipt_ms>:<first 16 hex of the SHA-256 of the canonical meta>`, and `metadata_generation` is 1 or the earlier record's generation, plus one when `meta` changed.
It checks the derivation table but not the contract; callers use `pairCapabilityMatches`.
It cannot read account position mode, selected leverage, margin mode or collateral; research account and conversion schedules come from the frozen document, and the controlled account reader is #1877.
`probeCheckScripts` registers `--provider=hyperliquid --instrument=BTC --probe-only` once when any `hyperliquid` `perps` strategy is configured and `--provider=okx --instrument=BTC-USDT-SWAP --probe-only` once for any OKX `perps` swap; startup and `go-trader probe` both reach it, and a pair-bearing ordinary config still fails load before dispatch.

**Bound rounding (#1869).** Go `pairRoundBound(m pairContractMember, cap pairCapabilityEvidence, side string, bound float64) (float64, error)`; Python `round_bound(member, capability, side, bound)` for #1875, where `member` is the contract member JSON.
It first requires `pairCapabilityMatches` (record hash, provider, account, instrument and member identity, derivation, metadata equal to the contract); partition and time eligibility belong to the evidence source and its consumer.
The input enters through its shortest decimal and every step uses exact rationals: `hyperliquid_perps_v1` calls the existing `quotePriceRule.roundQuotePx` with the verified `sz_decimals` (including its second precision step), and `fixed_tick_v1` uses the `tick_step_text` grid; a buy cap rounds down and a sell floor rounds up.
It refuses an invalid side, an unsupported rule, a nonpositive or non-finite input or output, and a result whose float projection, parsed again through its shortest decimal, is not exactly the rounded value, not legal on the venue grid or outside the directed bound; it never widens a bound.
