### Pair quote collection and envelopes (#1871)

Issue #1871 adds the depth sources, the bounded collector, the frozen two-venue envelope, its verifiers in Go and Python, the collection step and the version-2 policy and action requests.
It activates no pair in `main.go`: the research hosts are #1881 and #1882, wakes, per-read snapshots, clocks and the host order are #1873 (Pair cadence, clocks and host order below), the valuation view is #1876, rounded-bound and crossing enforcement is #1877 and required reduction references are #1879.

**Source contracts.** One compatibility table (Go `pairSourceSpecs`, `pairSourceSpecFor(provider, source)`, `pairSourceContractFor`, `pairSourceContractMap`; Python `pair_inputs.SOURCE_SPECS`) maps each frozen member source to its contract:

| Provider, source | Source contract | Time semantics | Identity semantics | Coverage |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Hyperliquid `sealed_feed` | `hyperliquid_ws_l2Book` | `source_event_ms+first_receipt_ms/1635-v1` | `sha256-lp(host,namespace,coin,event_ms,levels)/1635-v1` | the sealed window |
| Hyperliquid `archive` | `hyperliquid_archive_l2_v1` | `archiver_receipt_ms+modeled_delivery_ms/1871-v1` | as sealed | the archive window on the modeled delivery timeline |
| Hyperliquid `public_snapshot` | `hyperliquid_public_l2_snapshot_v1` | `hyperliquid_l2_time_ms+reader_receipt_ms/1871-v1` | `sha256(canonical_payload)/1871-v1` | one point |
| OKX `public_snapshot` | `okx_public_books_snapshot_v1` | `okx_books_ts_ms+reader_receipt_ms/1871-v1` | `sha256(canonical_payload)/1871-v1` | one point |

`pairBookNeed.validate()` checks the numeric bounds of the Contract version 2 table without building a feed need.
`pairFeedBookNeeds(rt *basketRuntime) map[feedObservationKey]feedBookNeed` calls `contractBookNeed(window_ms, capacity, depth)` only for sealed Hyperliquid members, keyed by `l2BookKeyFor(instrument)`; a host that seals a pair snapshot merges these entries through `feedRequirements.addBook` (the existing `addBookNeed` union and conflict list), so `feedBookNeed`, the shared feed and ordinary feed derivation stay Hyperliquid-only and unchanged.
Admission also refuses a configuration whose worst-case envelope (`pairEnvelopeWorstCaseBytes`: every payload, level, gap and evidence record at its bound, payloads counted as base64) exceeds `envelope_max_bytes`, because no slot could ever freeze; a sealed or archive member with capacity 4096 never fits, while a public member's payload does not depend on capacity.

**Quote source.** `type pairQuoteSource interface { Sources() map[string]string; Recorded() bool; Collect(ctx context.Context, req pairCollectRequest) ([]pairObservation, error); Reference(ctx context.Context, req pairReferenceRequest) (pairObservation, error) }`.
`pairCollectRequest{Partition RiskPartition; PairID string; ContractFingerprint string; SlotMs, StartMs, DeadlineMs int64; Members []pairContractMember; BookNeed pairBookNeed; SourceContracts map[string]string; Snapshot *marketSnapshot; SnapshotID string; Generation uint64}` and `pairReferenceRequest{Partition RiskPartition; PairID string; ContractFingerprint string; Member pairContractMember; Side string; BookNeed pairBookNeed; SourceContract string; StartMs, DeadlineMs int64; Snapshot *marketSnapshot; SnapshotID string; Generation uint64}`.
`Sources()` returns a copy of member id to frozen source label; `Recorded()` true means saved reads that keep their original provenance and receipts (never a network fallback or a new receipt stamp).
`pairResearchDeps.Quotes` is required: `newPairResearchAdmission` builds the contract and runs `pairQuoteSourceErrors(contract, quotes)` before the transport checks and before `checkStateDir` can write; a nil source, a missing, extra or provider-incompatible member, or a label other than the frozen source is refused.
`(*pairResearchAdmission).quoteSource()` exposes it beside `evidenceSource()`.
The production source is `newPairQuoteCollector(p PairConfig, limits pairOperationalLimits, pairQuoteCollectorOptions{Egress *EgressConfig; ArchiveDirs map[string]string})`: sealed members read the captured snapshot in process (`marketSnapshot.l2BookPayload` with the contract need), public members spawn the public reader, archive members spawn the archive reader on an absolute prepared archive directory; `Collect` runs the members concurrently and cancels the siblings when one fails.
Sources return native levels only; `Reference` returns both sides and `Side` (`buy` or `sell`) names the side that must reach the full depth (the other needs one level).

**Payloads.** `Payload` is bounded verified source bytes (standard padded base64 in JSON) and `PayloadHash` is the lowercase SHA-256 of those bytes; a verifier re-derives every observation field from the payload, so a matching hash alone proves nothing.
Hyperliquid public: the #1869 canonical bytes of `{"request":{"coin","type":"l2Book"},"response":{"coin","levels":[[{"n","px","sz"}],[...]],"time"}}`, each side cut to the requested depth.
OKX public: the canonical bytes of `{"request":{"instId","sz":"<depth>"},"response":{"code":"0","data":[{"asks","bids","ts"}],"msg"}}` from `GET /api/v5/market/books` with raw decimal text (docs/research/1871-okx-public-books-source.md).
Sealed: the canonical `marketL2BookPayload` for the member key and contract need.
Archive: the `backtest/book_replay.py` `payload_from_series` section for the source-native cutoff and as-of, with `time_semantics` `archiver_receipt_ns_floor_ms/1635-v1` and an empty snapshot id.

**Observation** (`pairObservation`, V=2; field list in the PR #1871 contracts section) derivation (Go `pairDerive`, Python `pair_inputs.derive`, one rule set):
- Every decimal is a plain positive decimal of at most 32 characters and 18 fractional digits, normalized exactly (`canonicalBookDecimal`); bids strictly descend, asks strictly ascend, the best bid is below the best ask and each side reaches the depth; the observation keeps the first `depth` levels.
- Venue time is required for all four contracts (a null venue time is refused); it is at most 250 ms after and at most 5000 ms before the receipt.
- Public: `FirstReceiptMs` is the reader clock right after the response (inside the Go launch window), `SourceCutoffMs` equals it, snapshot id empty and generation 0, and coverage is one point: `from_ms = through_ms = first_receipt_ms`, `required_depth = depth`, `required_window_ms = 0`, `required_capacity = 1`, prefix complete; it never claims websocket history, and a consumer that needs history refuses it.
- Sealed and archive: the latest observation in the window is the quote; every marker in the window and every receipt gap above 10000 ms (window start, between observations, to the cutoff) is a coverage gap, and coverage is complete only with a complete prefix and no gap; `provenance` is `<contract>@<owner_instance>`.
- Archive: the reader asks for the source-native cutoff and as-of `start - modeled_delivery_ms` (the replay owner's strict as-of boundary is kept), then receipt, coverage start and end and gaps add the modeled delivery once with checked arithmetic; venue times and `source_cutoff_ms` stay source-native, and a recorded replay never adds it again.
- A sealed observation carries the captured snapshot id and generation; a public or archive observation carries none.
- Normalization happens in the builder after evidence selection: base quantity = native quantity × the capability `contract_size` (exact), price stays native, `native_unit`, `contract_size` and `price_currency` come from the capability, and `normalization_id` is `<native_unit>*<contract_size>@<capability evidence_id>`.

**Envelope.** `pairQuoteEnvelope{V int "v"; Partition string "partition"; PairID string "pair_id"; ContractFingerprint string "contract_fingerprint"; SlotMs int64 "slot_ms"; StartMs int64 "start_ms"; DeadlineMs int64 "deadline_ms"; CutoffMs int64 "cutoff_ms"; SnapshotID string "snapshot_id"; Generation uint64 "generation"; Members []pairEnvelopeMember "members"}` with `pairEnvelopeMember{Key pairEvidenceKey "key"; Observation pairObservation "observation"; Capability pairCapabilityEvidence "capability"; Conversions []pairConversionEvidence "conversions"; Account pairAccountEvidence "account"}`, V=2.
`buildPairEnvelope(in pairEnvelopeInputs) (pairEnvelope, string)` (empty reason is success) normalizes, sorts members by full key and conversions by currency then evidence id, writes the #1869 canonical bytes and runs the verifier on them; `InputFP` is the lowercase SHA-256 of the complete canonical envelope bytes.
`verifyPairEnvelope(rt *basketRuntime, rb *basketRebalance) (pairQuoteEnvelope, string)` never refetches: it checks the saved bytes against `input_fingerprint`, the strict canonical decoding (`pairDecodeQuoteEnvelope`; the strict readers gained a nullable integer kind and a bytes kind in both languages), version, partition, pair, contract, slot, cutoff, snapshot identity and every rule below.
Rules: size within `envelope_max_bytes`; `slot_ms <= start_ms <= cutoff_ms < deadline_ms = start_ms + collection_timeout_ms`; exactly the contract members with their full keys; each observation verified against its payload with complete coverage; each receipt at or before the cutoff and within `quote_age_ms`; receipts within `receive_skew_ms` of each other; one capability (`pairCapabilityMatches`), one account (`pairAccountMatches`, account leverage equal to the frozen leverage) and exactly the required conversions (`pairConversionMatches`), each bound to the member key and partition and eligible at the cutoff (`pairEligibleAt`); no midpoint, last price or other-venue substitute.
`pairBoundCrosses(env pairQuoteEnvelope, memberID, side string, bound float64) bool`: a buy bound at or above the best native ask, or a sell bound at or below the best native bid, crosses (equality crosses); #1877 enforces it with the rounded bound.
Python `shared_tools/pair_inputs.py` (standard library and `pair_evidence.py` only) exposes `verify_envelope(request: dict) -> dict`, which takes exactly the version-2 policy request, re-canonicalizes the embedded envelope, requires its SHA-256 to equal `input_fingerprint`, checks the request's `source_contract` against the table and applies the same rules from the request's `book_need`, `limits` and members (`source`, `source_contract`, `modeled_delivery_ms`, `leverage`, `meta`); it also exposes `check_envelope(expect, raw)`, `derive` and `bound_crosses`, and `pair_evidence.py` gained `account_matches`, `conversion_matches`, `required_conversion_currencies` and `eligible_at`.

**Collection step.** `stepPair` runs reconciliation, exception detection, lifecycle checks and the single protective call first, then `collectPairSlot`, then `decidePendingPairSlots`, then elective execution; a collection failure never returns from the step before queries, settlement or protection.
`collectPairSlot` runs for `flat`, `opening` and `matched` pairs and skips while `SaveBlocked` or `needsDurable`.
It reads the coordinator clock (`basketCoordinator.pairClock`, `tradingNow` by default, never earlier than the step clock), takes slot = floor(now / cadence) × cadence and reserves only an absent slot (`errBasketDeadlineTaken` means no collection; no backfill); a nil or mismatched quote source or a missing evidence source holds with a logged reason and no reservation.
Under `mu` it captures immutable copies of the members, contract fingerprint, book need and source contracts; outside `mu` it reads the seal for a sealed member through `in.PairSnapshotFor(rt, start)` (#1873; never `in.Snapshot` or `in.SnapshotFor`), then collects and reads the evidence under one context of `collection_timeout_ms` measured as a duration (a historical logical time never becomes an operating-system deadline); evidence is read at the final cutoff, which is the clock when the reads return.
After reacquiring `mu` it holds the slot when the pair records changed, the read failed, the context ended, the wall budget was used up, the cutoff reached the logical deadline, the contract changed, `recheckPairSlot` failed, the seal was missing or invalid, or the host feed generation moved during the read; otherwise it builds and freezes through `StateStore.FreezePairEnvelope` in the pair's partition file before any policy runs.
A hold is written through pair progress; if that save fails the slot stays reserved on disk, `needsDurable` is set and no slot is collected until the retry saves, and a restart turns the reservation into the existing durable hold.
The step refreshes `in.Now` and the runtime clock after every unlocked operation (valuations, queries, collection, the policy run and each submit) before any time-sensitive decision.

**Use of saved evidence.** `decidePairSlot` verifies the saved envelope before the policy; damaged, changed or missing evidence holds the slot and the policy never runs.
An increase verifies the selected quote event's envelope (`pairBindIncrease`; the event may be newer than the origin decision), requires that member's receipt within `quote_age_ms` of the dispatch time (a first dispatch that fails ends the decision held), and copies the member conversions, `capability.evidence_hash` and the envelope fingerprint into the immutable request.

**Version-2 requests.** `pairPolicyRequestVersion` and policy results are 2; the request adds `BookNeed pairBookNeed "book_need"` and each `pairPolicyMember` adds `CostPolicy fillCostPolicy "cost_policy"`, `Source string "source"`, `SourceContract string "source_contract"`, `ModeledDeliveryMs int64 "modeled_delivery_ms"` and `Leverage float64 "leverage"`; `envelope` is the saved bytes unchanged.
`pairActionRequestVersion` is 2 and adds `ContractFingerprint string "contract_fingerprint"`, `Conversions []pairConversionEvidence "conversions,omitempty"`, `CapabilityHash string "capability_hash,omitempty"`, `EvidenceHash string "evidence_hash,omitempty"`, `Reference *pairObservation "reference,omitempty"` and `NativeBooked bool "native_booked,omitempty"`.
`StateStore.ReservePairAction` validates every new request (`pairValidateActionRequest`) and, for an increase, rechecks inside the reservation transaction that the selected slot row, its envelope bytes and fingerprint, the quote event, the capability hash and the conversions agree (`checkPairIncreaseBindingTx`), then saves the request, entry intent and quote use atomically:

| Request kind | Required | May be absent |
|---|---|---|
| Increase (`pair_entry`: first leg or hedge) | `quote_event_id`, `contract_fingerprint`, `conversions`, `capability_hash`, `evidence_hash`, all checked in the reservation transaction against the selected event's saved envelope | none |
| Coordinator reduction (`pair_exit`, `pair_protective`, protective retry) | `contract_fingerprint`, `reduce_only` | evidence fields (refused when present); `reference` optional and verified when present (source, payload, identity, depth on the order side, receipt within `quote_age_ms`) |
| Native-booked (`pair_kill_switch`, `pair_operator`) | `contract_fingerprint`; `native_booked: true` only with a matching recovery note and `reduce_only` | all other evidence fields, the quote event and the price bound |

A saved version-1 request keeps its bytes (the immutable `request_json` trigger) and stays usable for query, settlement and native recovery; it is never reserved again or resent as version-2 authority.

**Readers.** `shared_scripts/fetch_pair_quotes.py --provider=hyperliquid|okx --source=public_snapshot|archive --instrument=<native> --depth=<1..20> [--window-ms --capacity --archive-dir --cutoff-ms --as-of-ms] [--probe-only]` prints `{"ok":true,"provider","source","instrument",...,"payload"}` (public adds `host`, `provenance`, `request_ms`, `receipt_ms`) and exits 0, or prints `{"ok":false,"error"}` and exits 1; `--probe-only` validates the arguments (archive flags are required for the archive source and refused for public) and exits before any adapter or archive load.
It refuses nonempty `HYPERLIQUID_SECRET_KEY`, `HYPERLIQUID_ACCOUNT_ADDRESS`, `OKX_API_KEY`, `OKX_API_SECRET`, `OKX_PASSPHRASE`, `HYPERLIQUID_TESTNET` and `OKX_SANDBOX`; Go passes them as empty overrides with `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1`.
Public reads use `HyperliquidExchangeAdapter(public_only=True).public_l2_snapshot(symbol, depth)` (strict `public_instrument_rules` resolution, then `Info.l2_snapshot`) and `OKXExchangeAdapter(public_only=True).public_order_book(inst_id, depth)` (`publicGetMarketBooks`, no market load, no float conversion); each adapter's `public_host()` must be the mainnet host, which goes into the provenance.
The archive reader loads `backtest/pair_archive_book.py`: `prepare_archive(archive_dir, out_dir, coins, days, manifest)` runs `book_archive.import_window` (which verifies each object through `archive_cache.verified_object`) and writes `import_report.json` with the day-file digests and per-file `day_bounds` (row count, open rows, first and last row time, last eviction time, first coverage time), and `archive_payload(import_dir, coin, cutoff_ms, as_of_ms, need)` opens only the tail, the last file with coverage before the as-of time and each file whose bounds can hold a row present in the window at the as-of time, verifies each opened file against its digest and bounds, and returns the same `payload_from_series` section as a full load; archive preparation and host orchestration stay outside collection.
A row received in the window can sit in a later day file because a day file holds the rows evicted on that day, so the reader selects by bounds and never by calendar day.
`probeCheckScripts` registers `pairQuoteHLPublicProbeArgv` and `pairQuoteHLArchiveProbeArgv` when any Hyperliquid `perps` strategy is configured and `pairQuoteOKXPublicProbeArgv` for any OKX `perps` swap, each carrying every runtime flag; startup and `go-trader probe` reach the same owner.

**Launcher and egress.** `spawnPythonProcessWithEnvLimited` and `spawnPythonProcessRawLimited` take opt-in `pythonOutputLimits{Stdout, Stderr int}`; an overflow cancels the process group and returns `*pythonOutputOverflowError` after the existing bounded pipe cleanup, and the zero value keeps every existing caller unbounded.
The quote readers cap each stream at `envelope_max_bytes` and enter through `acquirePythonSlot(ctx)`, the shared `pythonSemaphore` with a cancellable wait, holding the permit until the spawn and its cleanup return.
A public read first calls `pairPublicEgressReady(ctx, cfg)`: `establishProcessEgress(ctx, cfg, true)`, then, when a proxy is configured or required or the runtime is active, it requires under the runtime lock an active runtime with an attestation and no lost attestation; there is no direct fallback, and a stderr overflow is a child failure.

**Storage growth.** Each collected slot writes its envelope (up to `envelope_max_bytes`, at most 4 MiB) once into `basket_rebalances.evidence_json` and, since #1875, one decision record of at most 98304 bytes (`pairTargetsMaxBytes`) into `targets_json`.
`prunePair` prunes memory only, so a research run's state file grows by at most `envelope_max_bytes` plus 98304 bytes per cadence slot, and runs are sized as duration ÷ `decision_cadence_ms` × (`envelope_max_bytes` + 98304) (research-only; no production loader hosts pairs).

### Pair policy `cross_exchange_perp_arbitrage` (#1875)

Issue #1875 adds the first research pair policy beside the test-only fixture policy, its evaluator process and its parameter validation; it stays research-only, and no ordinary route, deployed configuration, default, promotion or live transport admits a pair.
#1877 adds authoritative Go bound rounding, post-round validation, saved version-2 bound evidence and dispatch crossing checks (Pair bounds, transport and the position barrier below); #1878 replaced the temporary runner table with the canonical pair accessor and registered the policy as production-refused (Registration below); replay latency, polling and recording stay with #1881 and #1882.

**Parameters** (Go `pairPolicyCrossExchangePerpArbitrage`, `pairPolicyParamKeys`, `pairPolicyParamValidators`, `pairCrossExchangeParamErrors`; Python `cross_exchange_perp_arbitrage.param_errors`).
`params` needs exactly `entry_threshold_bps`, `exit_threshold_bps`, `adverse_band_bps` and `funding_allowance_bps` as finite JSON numbers; there are no defaults, and missing keys, unknown keys, `null`, booleans, strings, arrays, objects and numbers that overflow a float64 are refused.
Rules: `0 <= exit_threshold_bps < entry_threshold_bps <= 10000`, `0 <= adverse_band_bps < 10000` and `funding_allowance_bps >= 0`.
Go checks the rules on the float64 values it later sends, and Python checks the exact decimal of those same tokens, so both languages accept the same values.
`pairRawPairErrors` JSON-decodes and trims the raw `policy` string, selects `pairPolicyParamValidators[name]` and runs it on the raw `params` (an absent `params` reports all four keys missing); it runs in `pairResearchRawPair`, which research admission and the controlled fixture call before the first typed decode, evidence bind or state-directory write, and in `pairConfigErrors`.
Errors are sorted and deduplicated; a policy without a validator (the test-only fixture policy) keeps the typed unknown-key check (`pairParamErrors`), and the add-strategy command refuses the name through `productionRefusedStrategies` before its pair-token check (#1878).

**Evaluator and runner.**
`shared_strategies/open/cross_exchange_perp_arbitrage.py` exposes `evaluate_pair(request: dict) -> dict`, where `request` is the strict tree from `pair_evidence.loads_strict` (numbers stay `JSONNumber` tokens).
It imports only the standard library and loads `shared_tools/pair_evidence.py` and `shared_tools/pair_inputs.py` by file path under those names (a module of the same name loaded from another file is refused); it reads no clock, configuration, state, network or adapter, and no single-symbol registry, shim or composer import loads it.
`shared_scripts/run_pair_policy.py [--probe-only]` sets `sys.dont_write_bytecode`, loads the canonical `shared_strategies/open/pair_registry.py` by file path under `go_trader_pair_registry` (never `registry.py`, NumPy, pandas, adapters, configuration or state), checks membership against `pair_policies()` and resolves the evaluator only through `get_pair_evaluator(name)`; it reads one request from stdin and prints exactly one finite JSON object (sorted keys, no NaN) with exit 0 for `enter`, `exit`, `hold` and `invalid`.
Non-strict request bytes (invalid UTF-8, duplicate keys, a trailing value), a non-object, a request version other than 2 and an unknown policy are `invalid`; an unexpected exception, a missing or failing canonical registry or evaluator import, or an output failure writes a traceback to stderr and exits 1, which never authorizes a decision (the runner catches no exception from `get_pair_evaluator` or `evaluate_pair`).
`--probe-only` loads the canonical registry and every evaluator in `pair_policies()` order without reading stdin and prints `{"ok":true,"probe":true,"policies":[...]}`; a failed load exits 1.
Go `runPairPolicyProcess(ctx context.Context, stdin []byte, timeout time.Duration) ([]byte, []byte, error)` is `pairPolicyRunFn`: one deadline of `timeout` covers `acquirePythonSlot` and `spawnPythonProcessWithEnvLimited(pairPolicyScript, ...)` with `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1`, a 262144-byte stdout ceiling (`pairPolicyMaxStdoutBytes`) and a 65536-byte stderr ceiling (`pairPolicyMaxStderrBytes`).
The shared permit is released after process cleanup, and an overflow, a timeout, a cancelled or saturated admission or a nonzero exit is a runner error that holds the slot.

**Evaluation order.** Version and policy, then parameters (`invalid` at stage `params`), then `pair_inputs.verify_envelope(request)` (`invalid` at stage `evidence`), then the member fields.
Economic inputs come from the verified envelope (capability meta, account, conversions and base-quantity levels with native prices), the request members (`cost_policy` `taker_fee_pct` and `slippage_pct` as fractions, contract `initial_margin_rate` and `maintenance_margin_rate`, the frozen `leverage`, `booked_base_qty` and `booked_avg_px`) and the request `limits`.
The fee rate is the member `taker_fee_pct`, which is the #1887 binding rate, so the request stays version 2.

**Entry economics** (lifecycle `flat`; a booked quantity on either member holds).
All arithmetic uses exact rationals; every amount is USD through the member's saved cutoff conversion for its price currency (account amounts through its settlement currency), and no stablecoin is assumed equal to USD.
Per leg `r = max(adverse_band_bps / 10000, slippage_pct)`; asks become `P × (1 + r_buy)` and bids `P × (1 − r_sell)` in native prices before conversion and the depth walk.
For base quantity `q`: `B` is the walked buy cost, `S` the walked sell proceeds, entry fees `E = fee_buy × B + fee_sell × S`, the exit-fee reserve is `E` again, the exit adverse reserve is `r_buy × B + r_sell × S`, the funding reserve is `B × funding_allowance_bps / 10000` (once, with no credit for funding receipts), and `capture = S − B − 2E − exit adverse reserve − funding reserve`.
Entry needs `capture > 0` and `10000 × capture > entry_threshold_bps × B`; equality holds.
Proposed native bounds are the worst consumed ask × `(1 + r_buy)` and the worst consumed bid × `(1 − r_sell)`.
The exact proposed bound is projected to the safe-side float (a buy cap to the largest float whose value and shortest decimal are at or below it, a sell floor to the smallest at or above it); that float is the target `price_bound`, `pair_evidence.round_bound` gives the sizing-rounded bound, and a rounded bound beyond the exact bound is refused.
Minimum notional is checked in native price-currency units at `min(proposed, rounded)` (the rounded buy cap, the proposed sell floor), because the target carries the proposed bound and `pairLegalLot` checks the minimum there.
Reachability: `pair_inputs.bound_crosses` must hold for the rounded bound, and the saved depth at or inside it must cover `q` on both sides.
Caps and margin use `max(proposed, rounded)` × the price conversion (the proposed buy cap, the rounded sell floor): gross and per-venue notional within `gross_budget_usd` and the venue cap (equality passes; equal opposite targets leave no unmatched notional).
Per leg with notional `N`, `N × max(initial_margin_rate, 1 / leverage) + taker_fee_pct × N` must not exceed the account `free_collateral`, and `equity − taker_fee_pct × N` must not fall below `N × maintenance_margin_rate` (account amounts converted, margin used kept as evidence only); these mirror `pairPlan.reading`, and Go stays the final admission and execution authority.
Candidates are common multiples of both base lots (`lot_step × contract_size`, exact least common multiple) that meet both native minimums (`min_qty × contract_size`).

**Size search.**
The quantity range is cut at the union of both walked sides' cumulative depth; on each interval `(lo, hi]` (a breakpoint belongs to the level that ends there) both walks are affine and the worst levels, bounds and reachable depth are fixed, so every condition is an exact linear bound on `q`.
The search solves the intervals from the deepest down, takes the largest grid multiple inside the first nonempty intersection, recomputes that candidate directly (`entry_candidate`) and fails the run if the two disagree; a failing deeper interval never hides a smaller passing one, and the work is bounded by the saved levels, never by the number of lots.
Both orientations are searched independently; the larger quantity wins, and an equal quantity falls back to the buy member's, then the sell member's, full evidence key `(partition, provider, account_key, instrument, member_id)` in byte order (with uncrossed books both orientations cannot pass at once).
With no passing quantity the result is a hold naming each orientation's deepest candidate failures.
Targets are `+q` for the buy member and `−q` for the sell member, each with its safe-side proposed native bound, and `orientation` is `long_hyperliquid_short_okx` or `long_okx_short_hyperliquid`.

**Matched exit.**
The booked signs identify the long and the short; both must be finite, nonzero and opposite, otherwise the policy holds.
The quantities may differ (a hedge short of one lot still matches the pair), so each leg walks its own booked size: the long sells into its bids and the short covers from its asks, each with its closing adverse rate.
Remaining spread is per base unit, `10000 × (cover_cost / q_short − sale_proceeds / q_long) / (sale_proceeds / q_long)`; at or below `exit_threshold_bps` (equality exits) the result is `exit` with zero targets and the safe-side closing bounds, and above it the policy holds.
Closing fees, net liquidation and an estimated round trip from `booked_avg_px` are recorded separately; a convergence exit can lose money.
Missing closing depth, an unroundable bound or a rounded bound that cannot reach the depth holds the policy; coordinator protection, the hold-age exit, queries and recovery keep their authority.

**Results and the Go boundary.**
Every result carries `v` 2, the request identity (`pair_id`, `slot_ms`, `contract_fingerprint`, `input_fingerprint`), `status` and a `decision` object; reasons are cut to 1024 bytes.
The decision records the policy, stage and reason, the effective parameters, the evidence hashes and receipts, each member's rules, conversions, rates and account readings, up to 16 interval summaries and the deepest candidate per orientation, and either the selected entry candidate (grid, native quantities, walked amounts, fees, reserves, capture, proposed, rounded and emitted bounds, notionals and margin readings) or the `exit` block with both booked quantities and their difference.
Exact values are decimal strings, and a non-terminating value is written `n/d`.
`decidePairSlot` keeps the failed-process check before parsing; `pairDecodePolicyResult` then requires one strict object (`pairStrictParse`: no duplicate keys at any depth, no trailing value) with only the known fields, integer `v` and `slot_ms`, string identity and status, a `decision` object of at most 65536 bytes (`pairPolicyMaxDecisionBytes`) and strings of at most 2048 bytes (`pairPolicyMaxReasonBytes`).
Per status, `enter` needs an orientation and targets, `exit` needs targets and no orientation, `hold` and `invalid` carry no targets or orientation, a hold carries no error and an invalid result no hold reason; each target is exactly `{target_base_qty, price_bound}` with finite numbers.
`validatePairPolicyResult` and the lifecycle, legal-lot and full-fill cap checks follow; `pairFullFillCapCheck(rt, env, targets)` converts each bound with the verified envelope's price-currency conversion, and missing conversion evidence holds.
A semantic `invalid` result is frozen as a hold that keeps its decision evidence; a schema failure is frozen as a hold without it.
`freezePairTargets` replaces a record above 98304 bytes (`pairTargetsMaxBytes`) by a hold whose decision is `{"oversize_bytes":N}`.

**Registration (#1878).** `shared_strategies/open/pair_registry.py` is the only pair authority: `PAIR_CONTRACT` (`{"input": "pair_policy_request_v2", "output": "pair_policy_result_v2"}`), `pair_policies()` (sorted names), `pair_descriptor(name)` (a copy with `contract`), `get_pair_evaluator(name)` and `UnknownPairPolicy` (a `LookupError` subclass raised only for an unregistered name).
Enumeration never loads an evaluator; `get_pair_evaluator` loads it by repository-relative path under its descriptor module name with bytecode writes suppressed, refuses a cached module from another file, drops a failed import from `sys.modules` and propagates every import or execution error.
`registry.py` loads the same module by path, re-exports `PAIR_CONTRACT`, `UnknownPairPolicy` and `get_pair_evaluator`, adds `pair_evaluators()` and projects each descriptor into `STRATEGIES` (`pair`, `pair_fn` as a lazy evaluator, `pair_param_keys`, an empty `default_params` and a `fn` that refuses one-symbol evaluation); `cross_exchange_perp_arbitrage` is `no_edge` with `edge_source="unvalidated"`, evidence `backtest/candidates/cross_exchange_perp_arbitrage_1655/REPORT.md` and `production_refused=True`.
Ordinary admission: `strategy_composition` resolves a name that the injected single-symbol lookup does not know through a cached canonical registry load (`_go_trader_composer_open_registry`) and refuses a production-refused name at the positional, open, close-fallback and positional-under-override references; unknown names keep their errors, so spot-registry check scripts now report the production refusal for futures-only refused names.
Offline: `refuse_pair_evaluator` (`backtest/registry_loader.py`, also reached through `refuse_quote_capability` and `refuse_basket_evaluator`) is the first statement of `run_single_backtest` and `walk_forward_optimize`, runs in `run_walk_forward`, `compute_parity_frame` and `extract_fills_report` before their frames are used, and in `parity_diff.main` before `load_cached_data`.
Research admission is unchanged: `pairPolicyStrategies`, `pairPolicyParamKeys` and `pairPolicyParamValidators` stay the Go authority and never consult `productionRefusedStrategies`.

**Probe.** `probeCheckScripts` registers `run_pair_policy.py --probe-only` (`pairPolicyScript`, `pairPolicyProbeArgv`) once whenever any strategy names a check script, beside the shared-script probes; startup and `go-trader probe` both reach it, and a deploy tree without the script fails the probe (exit 78).

**Tests.** `TestBasketPairPolicyReal*` (`pyintegration`, local only; CI excludes Python-backed basket tests) runs real admission, the evaluator process, the coordinator and SQLite together, compares the bounded search with an exhaustive common-lot enumeration and covers the boundary, exit, process-failure, determinism and import-isolation cases, frozen-request replay after later quote and account inputs change (`TestBasketPairPolicyRealFrozenRequestIgnoresLaterInputs`) and canonical load failures in isolated runner copies (`TestBasketPairPolicyRealCanonicalLoadFailuresHold`); `TestBasketPairPolicyParams*` covers the raw parameter refusals in the default Go suite.

### Pair cadence, clocks and host order (#1873)

Issue #1873 gives research pair hosts one coordinator contract for wakes, sealed reads, clocks, the policy budget and the timeout marker, and adds the integration host `pairTestHost` (`scheduler/basket_pair_helpers_test.go`).
It activates no pair: the replay and paper hosts are #1881 and #1882, which call the steps below in this order and consume the incomplete marker; #1876 adds `bc.preparePairRisk(in)` and its line in the order; #1872's children (#1888) own pair funding; #1876 and #1879 adopt `readPairReference` for their consumers.

**Pair host order.** One host cycle at logical time `now`:
1. Deliver the evidence visible at `now` (the seals `PairSnapshotFor` returns, recorded or archive reads, frozen evidence).
2. Apply funding due at `now` through its owning funding contract with the same-time inventory and visibility rules of #1888. This is a documented downstream insertion: no pair funding exists until that contract lands, and #1873 claims no funding parity.
3. Call `bc.preparePairRisk(in)` (#1876) with `Now`, `PairSnapshotFor` and `PairGeneration`; it runs on every cycle, wake-only and save-blocked cycles included, and publishes the valuation view that step 4 and step 5 read.
4. When every active partition is save-blocked (`allPartitionsSaveBlocked`), skip risk and management and call `bc.step` with `SaveBlocked: true`. Otherwise measure each `activePartitions` partition with `measureScopeCycleRiskMarked` and the lock-held `bc.pairMarksLocked()` (under one `RLock`, never a second read lock), apply it with `applyScopeCycleRisk` and the paper kill switch `applyPaperKillSwitchCycle` (under `Lock`), and on a management cycle call `bc.beginCycle()` and run native member management (the Hyperliquid perps step and the shared OKX step) for the pair members.
5. Call `bc.step(in)` with `ScopeRisk`, `PairSnapshotFor` and `PairGeneration`.
6. Save every partition (`store.SaveAll` under `Lock`).
7. Read fresh time and schedule the next cycle at the minimum of the host schedule and a nonnegative `bc.wakeDelay(now)`. A sub-second host never uses `adjustDelay`, which clamps every wake to at least one second.

**Management freshness.** `bc.beginCycle()` runs only on a host cycle that runs native member management; a wake-only step (no member due) calls neither.
A pair increase needs, for each member, a validated management echo from the current or the previous management cycle, observed (`basketMemberObservation.AtMs`, the coordinator clock at `observePair`) no more than `pairManagementMaxAge(cfg)` (2 × `interval_seconds`) before the increase time, with ATR above zero; otherwise increases hold and exits, queries and protection continue.
Injected observations prove nothing about host order; the Python-backed host check runs the real member steps.

**Wakes.** `(bc *basketCoordinator) pairWakeDelay(now time.Time) time.Duration` takes the read lock; `wakeDelay` folds `pairWakeDelayLocked(now)` in under its existing lock (never a recursive read lock) and ordinary basket timing is unchanged.
Boundaries per admitted pair runtime, all from saved records and checked arithmetic (`pairAddMs`): the current cadence slot start and the next one; a frozen undecided slot's useful decision-window end; each pending non-native action's acceptance deadline and its strict absence boundary (deadline + `entryRecoveryGrace` + 1 ms); a native-booked action's inclusive boundary (deadline + grace); an eligible protective retry (`recovery` or `operator_held`, nonzero exposure, no pending protective reduction); the maximum hold age (`opening`, `matched`); the recovery age (`recovery`).
A future boundary contributes its delay; a passed boundary wakes now only when it passed after the last step's start (`basketRuntime.pairLastStepMs`), so a step always settles a passed boundary and repeated calls never form a zero-delay loop.
Held, reserved, terminal, blocked and durability-pending work therefore waits for the next cadence boundary, which is also the retry interval of a failed save (at most `decision_cadence_ms`); queries of unresolved actions run on every cadence step and no query deadline is persisted.
It returns -1 when no pair deadline exists. A restart starts with `pairLastStepMs` zero, steps once, and then rebuilds the same schedule from the owning partition's saved slots, actions and progress.

**Sealed reads.** `basketCycleInputs` adds `PairSnapshotFor func(rt *basketRuntime, at time.Time) *marketSnapshot` and `PairGeneration func() uint64`; ordinary `Snapshot` and `SnapshotFor` keep their basket behavior and are never a pair fallback.
`PairSnapshotFor` contract: the coordinator calls it only for an operation that reads a `sealed_feed` member, once per collection or reference read, at that operation's read-start time `at`, through `bc.unlocked` (without `mu`; it may block, and it may take `mu`); it may read only the immutable `rt.Contract` and `rt.Partition`.
It returns the immutable seal valid for `at`, covering `pairFeedBookNeeds(rt)` merged through `feedRequirements.addBook`, with a printable `EvaluationID` of at most 256 bytes, `ConfigGeneration` at least 1 and equal to `PairGeneration()`, and a seal time (`Deadline`, else `SealedAt`) not after `at`.
Because it is never called twice for one operation, a host may return its newest seal; a newer seal that arrives during the read never invalidates the captured one, and each later operation obtains its own.
`PairGeneration` returns the host's current feed configuration generation without blocking; the coordinator reads it before the read and again after it, and a move holds.
A missing callback, a nil seal, a missing generation callback, a missing identity, a seal after `at`, a generation mismatch, an unavailable section and stale observations hold the sealed read with no public, cycle-snapshot or private substitute. Public, archive and recorded members that need no seal need no callback.

**Reference reads.** `(bc *basketCoordinator) readPairReference(ctx context.Context, rt *basketRuntime, in basketCycleInputs, member pairContractMember, side string) (pairObservation, error)` takes the coordinator lock; a caller already holding it uses `readPairReferenceLocked` with the same arguments.
It checks runtime ownership, the admitted member (provider, account, instrument, source), the side (`buy` or `sell`) and the quote source, fills `pairReferenceRequest`, reads the seal by the rule above and calls the source's `Reference` outside `mu`, bounded by the caller context and `collection_timeout_ms`.
On return it refuses changed pair records, a changed contract, a read error, an ended context, a used-up wall budget, a completion at or after the logical deadline, a moved generation and a snapshot identity that does not match the captured seal, then validates the observation with `pairCheckReference` at the fresh completion time; any failure returns an error and no substitute observation.

**Policy budget.** `(bc *basketCoordinator) pairPolicyBudget(rt *basketRuntime, rb *basketRebalance, now time.Time) (time.Duration, bool)` verifies the saved envelope; `decidePairSlot` uses `pairPolicyBudgetFor(rt, rb, env, now)` and `pairPolicyWindowFor(rt, rb, env)`.
Real mode: the minimum of `pairPolicyMaxTimeout`, the next slot minus now, the envelope cutoff plus `quote_age_ms` minus now, and the earliest verified member `first_receipt_ms` plus `quote_age_ms` minus now (a member receipt before the cutoff bounds first); a nonpositive or overflowing bound holds without a policy call.
Simulated mode (`tradingClockBound()`): a fixed wall budget of `pairPolicyMaxTimeout`, with logical eligibility checked before launch; wall time never advances the simulated clock.
In both modes `decidePairSlot` reads fresh time after the runner returns and holds an `enter` or `exit` result outside its useful window: the next slot is strict, the earliest receipt age is inclusive at `quote_age_ms` and the cutoff age is inclusive; a zero launch budget still prevents a launch.

**Action clocks.** Each `executePair` and protective iteration reads fresh time. An increase rechecks, immediately before `ReservePairAction`, the selected quote event's saved envelope, member receipt age and binding (`pairBindIncrease`) and both members' view validity at that fresh time (mark, conversions, capability and account); a failed first dispatch ends the decision held. Reductions gain no new evidence gate: an exit checks mark validity, and plans its tranche, at the time `executePair` started, never a time refreshed between its legs.
The view is prepared once per host cycle: an increase whose view evidence aged during collection or the policy run holds (a first dispatch ends held), so a host's quote age must cover collection and policy time.
After `MarkPairActionSubmitting` the coordinator reads fresh time again and checks the acceptance deadline and, for an increase, the same evidence; inside the unlocked transport boundary it checks an immutable proof (deadline, selected receipt, and for both members the earliest end of the mark, conversion, capability and account validity) against the clock right before `Submit`.
An expiry after reservation sends nothing and ends through `finishPairNoFill` (intent `no_fill`, quote use released, the saved request and client identity unchanged). After a query or submit returns, settlement and the next leg use fresh time; a confirmed fill is always settled through its owner even when the entry evidence aged during transport.

**Timeout marker.** Any end of the policy context (a deadline from any budget bound, or a parent cancellation) is an infrastructure hold: the slot is held with a coordinator decision and pair progress gains one `policy_infrastructure_timeout` obligation (`pairObligationPolicyTimeout`, slot and cause in its detail, first record kept through `addObligationOnce`), saved together through `persistPair` (the `writePairTx` progress transaction).
A failed save sets `needsDurable`: elective work holds, the retry `SavePartition` writes both, and the slot is never decided again; when the pair state changed during a timed-out run the decision is discarded and the marker is still saved.
A runner error without a context end, invalid output, a missing launch budget and a late result are ordinary coordinator holds; a policy `hold` is economic. Neither gets the marker.
The marker survives saves, lifecycle resets, later successful slots, pruning and restart, and it is not recovery evidence (`pairRecoveryObligation` excludes it from the startup, `storage-inspect` pending and wrong-file refusals).
`pairIncompleteEvidence(rt *basketRuntime) (pairObligation, bool)` reports it, a pending unsaved marker included; #1881 and #1882 mark their run results incomplete from it. No table or second state writer is added.

### Pair valuation view (#1876)

Issue #1876 gives research pairs one coordinator-owned valuation view; risk, wallet, sizing and report owners read each member at its own venue price and checked evidence, never at a shared symbol price; a member with no current checked mark is valued conservatively only for a bounded time (Incomplete valuation below).
It activates no pair in `main.go`, adds no table and enables no pair reload; ordinary, read-only, probe and reload loads still refuse pairs.

**Producer and lifetime.** `preparePairRisk` runs as host order step 3 on every cycle, before risk.
Under `mu` it captures the contract, records snapshot (`pairSnapshot`: slots, actions with confirmed, applied quantity, notional, fee and fee version, member books with cash and average cost, lifecycle and progress sequence) and a preparation sequence; the reference read (`readPairReferenceLocked`, the once-per-read `PairSnapshotFor` and generation checks) and the frozen evidence reads (`Capability`, `Account`, `Conversions` at the fresh read time) run outside `mu`.
It then rechecks the sequence, runtime, records, contract and context and publishes both members in one write: a failed, cancelled, late or changed read publishes explicit invalidity with its reason, and an older successful view never stays as authority.
A mark read failure keeps the independently read conversion evidence, so settlement with a valid conversion continues.
The view lives in process memory on the pair runtime; a restart or a new coordinator starts with none, and increases hold until a preparation succeeds.
Preparation sends no order, reserves nothing, runs no policy and never writes progress, so it also runs on save-blocked cycles.
A host that calls it without `PairSnapshotFor` or `PairGeneration` while a pair has a sealed member gets one CRITICAL per pair (`notePairPrepHostInputs`, kept in process memory) and the sealed member holds; `main.go` admits no pair today, so its bare call raises nothing.

**Reader validity.** Publication flags never extend evidence: every reader checks at its own time (`usdMarkAt`, `conversionAt`, `capabilityReasonAt`, `accountAt`).
A saved envelope stays the authority for its own decision and request binding (`verifyPairEnvelope`, `(pairQuoteEnvelope).member`); current view evidence never replaces saved bytes, hashes, conversions or bindings.
Prices convert with `PriceCurrency` (planning upper valuation, partition gates, booked fill price) and fees and collateral with the settlement or collateral currency; base quantity is already normalized, so contract size applies only at the native boundary.
The full-target check at decision time uses the verified envelope's conversions and account record with current books.

**Collateral rule.** Accounts group by `(partition, provider, account_key)`; members sharing a resource must carry the same account record, else increases hold.
Book free collateral = cash − posted initial margin (`|booked| × avg cost × max(initial_margin_rate, 1/leverage)`) − margin and fee of confirmed unapplied increase fills; posted margin already sits in perpetual cash and is never added to equity.
Venue free collateral = account free collateral (USD) − the same book commitments − booked costs (`max(0, initial_capital − cash)`), because account evidence has no inclusion watermark and a later receipt never proves a fill, fee or margin is reflected.
Effective free collateral is the smaller of the two; equity is the smaller of book cash and account equity less booked costs, plus the adverse unrealized result of booked, unapplied and endpoint fills, less fee reserves.
Every endpoint of every unresolved request reserves its added initial margin and fee once and checks maintenance margin against that equity; holds come only from conflicting records, ownership mismatch, invalid evidence or insufficient adjusted collateral.

**Incomplete valuation (risk rule).** A pair member with exposure but no current mark is `stale`, `substituted` or `unavailable`, and every aggregate that includes it is incomplete.
Risk values it conservatively (the worse of the last checked mark and its average cost, average cost with no mark) and passes the partition reading as untrusted: no peak raise, no peak rebaseline, the drawdown substitution label, and an over-limit reading defers through `untrustedEquityLatchDeferral` and then latches; it never vetoes the latch.
Paper `EquityTrusted` stays true; the pair state is the separate `PairValuation` signal.
An incomplete partition also holds new opens through the notional cap (when configured) and both exposure-cap directions, and the member circuit-breaker drawdown uses the same conservative value without a peak raise.
Increases and leaderboard scoring hold; queries, settlement, protection, recovery and native closes continue.
**Time limit.** Each published view carries `MarkLostAtMs`: 0 while the mark is valid; the first invalid publication takes the previous valid view's evidence expiry (`EvidenceMs + MaxAgeMs`, capped at the publication time), or the publication time after a restart; later invalid publications keep it; a valid publication clears it, and a valid view that ages at the reader counts from its expiry.
The partition also saves `PortfolioRiskState.PairValuationIncompleteSince` (`portfolio_risk.pair_valuation_incomplete_since`, written by `applyScopeCycleRisk` through `recordPairValuationIncompleteSince`): the earliest start among its incomplete members, kept across restarts and cleared by the first complete reading; `pairMarksLocked` passes it to every member, but a member's own start (`sinceFor`) takes it only when the member's in-memory start has no earlier knowledge (no view, or `MarkLostAtRestart`: the first invalid publication after a restart), so restarts never move a start forward and a member that goes stale later keeps its own start.
When the partition valuation stays incomplete without a break for `untrustedEquityLatchDeferral` from the earliest of its members' own starts and the saved start (`pairMarks.partitionOverdue`, `scopeCycleRisk.PairValuationOverdue`, `PairValuationOverdueMs`), the reading counts as over the limit and latches when `max_drawdown_pct` is set (event source `pair_valuation`); when a member that holds a position stays incomplete for that long from its own start (`pairMarks.overdue`), `checkPairMemberRisk` trips the enabled member circuit breaker with `RiskReasonPairValuationStale`; the breaker books no close without a checked price.

**Wallets and reports.** `walletKeyFor` and `configuredWalletKeyFor` never group a pair member; pair totals stay book-derived, and account evidence only bounds collateral and appears once per resource in `pair_accounts`.
Status totals and rows, strategy overview and equity views, warning contributors and position text, Discord status, positions, P&L, strategy summaries and leaderboard, and the exposure-status note read the view through `state.pairMarksLocked()` captured under the state lock and label incomplete values; an incomplete leaderboard entry is unranked and outside totals.
The quote admission gate values pair members through the same accessor; the basket gate never sees pair members because pair research configurations refuse baskets.

**Contracts for later children (#1879, #1881, #1882).**

| Name | Go signature or type | Rule |
|---|---|---|
| `pairMemberValuation` | `Key pairMemberKey`, `Symbol string`, `ContractFingerprint string`, `EvidenceHash string`, `Generation uint64`, `Mark`, `UpperPx`, `LowerPx float64` (native mid, best ask, best bid), `EvidenceMs`, `MaxAgeMs int64` (reference receipt, `quote_age_ms`), `ContractSize float64`, `Conversions map[string]float64`, `FeeRate float64`, `PriceCurrency`, `SettlementCurrency string`, `MarkUSD`, `LowerUSD`, `UpperUSD float64`, `Observation pairObservation`, `Capability pairCapabilityEvidence`, `ConversionEvidence []pairConversionEvidence`, `Account pairAccountEvidence`, `MarkValid`, `ConversionValid`, `CapabilityValid`, `CollateralValid`, `Substituted bool`, `MissingReason string` | one record per member per publication; `FeeRate` is the member binding `CostPolicy.TakerFeePct`; `Conversions` holds `USD`=1 plus one rate per checked record; `Substituted` means `MarkUSD`/`LowerUSD`/`UpperUSD` come from the member's last checked mark and grant no authority |
| `pairViewSet` | `map[string]pairMemberValuation` | keyed by runtime member strategy id, which equals the contract member id and the in-memory book id; each entry carries the full partition, provider, account, instrument and member key; stored ids translate only inside the storage owner |
| `usdMarkAt` | `func (v pairMemberValuation) usdMarkAt(nowMs int64) (px float64, valid bool, reason string)` | valid only when the view mark is valid, the receipt is not after `nowMs` and within `quote_age_ms` of it, and the `PriceCurrency` conversion record is eligible at `nowMs`; `px` = native mid times that rate |
| `usdBoundsAt`, `conversionAt`, `capabilityReasonAt`, `accountAt`, `increaseReasonAt`, `evidenceLimitMs`, `depthBound` | methods on `pairMemberValuation` | each rechecks its own record's eligibility at the reader time; `accountAt` returns `pairAccountUSD{Resource, EvidenceHash, Equity, Free, MarginUsed}` converted once with the collateral currency; `increaseReasonAt` is empty only when mark, every conversion, capability and account are valid; `evidenceLimitMs` is the earliest end of those intervals |
| `preparePairRisk` | `func (bc *basketCoordinator) preparePairRisk(in basketCycleInputs)` | takes the coordinator lock; per pair one `readPairReferenceLocked` read per member (side `sell` for a long book, else `buy`) and the frozen evidence reads outside `mu`, all under one context of `collection_timeout_ms` from `bc.parent`; publishes both members atomically or discards its result when a newer preparation started |
| `pairViews`, `pairViewsLocked` | `func (bc *basketCoordinator) pairViews(now time.Time) pairViewSet`, `func (bc *basketCoordinator) pairViewsLocked(now time.Time) pairViewSet` | detached deep copies (maps, slices, observation payload and levels, capability metadata) with the validity flags re-evaluated at `now`; `pairViews` takes the read lock, the locked form is for callers that already hold `mu` |
| `pairMarksLocked` | `func (bc *basketCoordinator) pairMarksLocked() *pairMarks`; `func (s *AppState) pairMarksLocked() *pairMarks` | caller holds `mu`; detached copies stamped with the coordinator clock; `AppState.pairViews` is set by `newBasketCoordinator` only when it admits a pair, so ordinary state returns nil |
| Marked readers | `portfolioValueMarked`, `portfolioNotionalMarked`, `perpsMarginDrawdownInputsMarked`, `aggregatePerpsMarginInputsMarked`, `computeAssetDeltasMarked`, `computeCorrelationMarked`, `evaluateExposureCapMarked`, `evaluateNotionalCapHoldMarked`, `computeSubsetPortfolioValueMarked`, `computeTotalPortfolioValueMarked`, `displayStrategyValueMarked`, `latestDisplayTotalMarked`, `latestDisplayTotalForPartitionMarked`, `computeSubsetDisplayValueMarked`, `measureScopeCycleRiskMarked`, `checkPairMemberRisk` | each takes `pm *pairMarks` and returns `pairValueStatus`; the legacy names that remain are nil-view wrappers and keep their results for ordinary strategies (Nil-view rule) |
| Nil-view rule | `CheckRisk`, `checkStrategyRiskWithView(sc, s, pv, prices, pm, logger, assist)` | notional and margin readers have no wrapper, so no caller can drop their status; `CheckRisk` without a view holds a pair member with a position (an active circuit breaker keeps its own reason) and changes no peak or drawdown; `main.go` and `basket_replay.go` call `checkStrategyRiskWithView` with `state.pairMarksLocked()`, which runs `checkPairMemberRisk` for members; manual-open notional and exposure gates and the basket partition gate read the same view and hold only while it is incomplete |
| `MarkLostAtMs`, `MarkLostAtRestart`, `incompleteSinceMs`, `sinceFor` | `pairMemberValuation.MarkLostAtMs int64`, `pairMemberValuation.MarkLostAtRestart bool`; `func (pm *pairMarks) incompleteSinceMs(id string) (since int64, known, priorUnknown bool)`; `func (pm *pairMarks) sinceFor(id string) int64` | `MarkLostAtMs` is process memory; `MarkLostAtRestart` is true when the first invalid publication had no earlier view (a restart or a new coordinator), and later invalid publications keep it; `priorUnknown` is true when the member's start has no earlier knowledge (no marks, no view, `MarkLostAtRestart`, or no recorded start); `sinceFor` takes the saved partition `PairValuationIncompleteSince` only when `priorUnknown` is true and the saved start is earlier or no start is known, else the member's own start, else the reader time |
| `overdue`, `partitionOverdue` | `func (pm *pairMarks) overdue(st pairValueStatus) map[string]int64`; `func (pm *pairMarks) partitionOverdue(st pairValueStatus) (int64, map[string]int64)` | `overdue` lists members whose own start (`sinceFor`) is at least `untrustedEquityLatchDeferral` old; `partitionOverdue` returns the partition start (the earliest of the members' own starts and the saved start) and every incomplete member once that start is that old |
| `pairValueStatus` | `Members map[string]string`, `Reasons map[string]string`; `complete() bool`, `label() string` | member state `stale`, `substituted` or `unavailable`; any entry makes an aggregate incomplete |
| `scopeCycleRisk.PairValuation`, `ExposureCapStatus.ValuationIncomplete`, `LeaderboardEntry.Incomplete` | `pairValueStatus`, `string`, `string` | carry the incomplete state to the risk owner, the exposure gates and the reports |
| Status JSON | `pair_valuation`, `pair_valuation_by_scope`, `pair_accounts` (`pairAccountDisplay`), member row `pair_valuation`, UI overview `pair_valuation`, equity view `pair_valuation` | present only when a pair is admitted or incomplete; ordinary output is unchanged |

Boundaries: native kill-switch and circuit-breaker close booking prices stay with #1914 and required reduction references with #1879; the version-2 replay host and its output proof stay with #1881 and #1882, which call `preparePairRisk` at host step 3, measure risk with `measureScopeCycleRiskMarked` and the lock-held marks, and run `checkPairMemberRisk` for members.

### Pair bounds, transport and the position barrier (#1877)

Issue #1877 freezes the rounded policy bounds, enforces crossing and full depth at dispatch, adds the strict pair order, query and position routes on both venues, the fixture-only live-shaped venues and the generation-checked venue position barrier.
Production pair activation stays refused, research admission stays synthetic and virtual, and no deployed configuration, default, credential or promotion changes.
Reduction reference bounds and the reduction policy are #1879 (Coordinator reduction references below).

**Saved bounds.** `decidePairSlot` validates a policy result in three stages: identity, schema, finite values and lifecycle (`validatePairPolicyResult`, which checks lots without minimum notional); rounding (`pairRoundTargets`); and economics (`pairRoundedEconomics`).
Each side comes from the target change against the member exposure; a zero-target exit closes the nonzero exposure.
`pairRoundBound` (now `pairCapabilityMatches` plus `pairRoundBoundRule`) rounds with the verified envelope capability: a buy cap down, a sell floor up, never wider; the policy's rounded diagnostic fields are not authority.
`pairTargetsVersion` is 2: `pairTargets.BoundEvidence map[string]pairBoundEvidence` (`bound_evidence`) with `pairBoundEvidence{ProposedBound "proposed_bound", RoundedBound "rounded_bound", Side "side", CapabilityHash "capability_hash"}`, and `Members` holds the rounded bounds.
Minimum notional is checked at `min(proposed, rounded)` for the entry target and for an exit's nonzero close tranche; caps and margin use `max(proposed, rounded)` with the saved conversions (`pairFullFillCapCheck` on a conservative copy).
Tranche planning and every unresolved increase keep the same conservative bound (`pairCandidate.riskBound`, `pairRiskBound`), and `legalTranche` checks the venue minimum for reductions too.
A failure at any stage freezes a version-2 hold without bound evidence.
Policy, action-request and contract versions are unchanged.

**Legacy records.** `parsePairTargets` reads version 1 and 2; a version-1 record keeps its bytes and never receives bound evidence.
An active version-1 `enter` or `exit` decision authorizes no order: with no unresolved action and no exposure its slot ends held, otherwise recovery opens (`holdPairLegacyOrigin`); saved actions keep their queries and settlement.
A version-2 actionable record must pass `pairCheckSavedTargets` (every member, side, hash, positive bounds, safe-side rounding, saved bound equal to the rounded bound, on the venue grid) before dispatch.

**Dispatch checks.** An increase rechecks, against the selected event's verified envelope, that the saved bound is a legal price under that event's capability, that it crosses the best level (`pairBoundCrosses`, equality crosses), and that the saved depth at or inside the bound covers the full exact tranche in base units (`pairDispatchBookCheck`, `pairDepthWithin`).
A failing first leg ends its slot held with no reservation; a failing hedge after a first-leg fill opens recovery at once; a failing later first-leg tranche only holds.
Order quantities are exact: `pairExactOrderQty` computes native = lots × lot step and base = native × contract size as rationals and refuses unless each float's shortest decimal is exactly the grid value; the action's requested quantity and both request quantities use these values.
A live-shaped venue needs a finite positive bound on the venue grid before reservation (`pairBoundOnGrid`); a hold-age exit rounds its fallback bound safe-side or holds.
Protective and hold-age reductions take the #1879 reference width bound; a reduction without a fresh saved reference holds (Coordinator reduction references below), and no market or uncapped route exists.
A nonzero exit remainder below the venue minimum after rounding keeps a visible `reduction_below_minimum` obligation and one CRITICAL alert; the flat check clears both obligations.
For a live-shaped admission the reservation transaction (`pairCheckLiveRequest` in `pairValidateActionRequest`) also requires the bound on the grid and equal to the action's, exact quantities, and, for increases and decision exits, the bound and side of the saved bound evidence.
The transport proof keeps the deadline, the selected receipt within `quote_age_ms` and the per-member view evidence limit (`evidenceLimitMs`, #1876) and adds the saved request deadline the child checks, the crossing and depth result and the barrier freshness limit.

**Order and query routes.** `check_hyperliquid.py` and `check_okx.py` accept exactly one of `--pair-order-json=<pairActionRequest>` and `--pair-query-json=<pairActionRequest> --since-ms=<ms>`, plus `--mode=live`; `check_hyperliquid.py` and `fetch_okx_positions.py` accept `--pair-position-json=<{v:1, provider, account_key, instrument, member_id, partition}>`.
`shared_tools/pair_orders.py` parses requests strictly (`loads_strict`, exact decimals, known fields only, version 2, provider, purpose `pair_entry`, `pair_exit` or `pair_protective` with the matching `reduce_only`, side and position side, base = native × contract size, instrument, account and client id formats); native-booked purposes never use the route.
`--probe-only` returns after the strict parse and before any clock, adapter or venue call; malformed input, a past deadline, a mismatched credential account, an off-grid size or price (Hyperliquid `check_quote_request`, OKX instrument grid and SDK serialization through `pair_order_check`) and a deadline reached immediately before the order call all answer `never_sent` with no order call.
Hyperliquid sends `limit_open(tif="Ioc", cloid, reduce_only by purpose, require_exact=True)` after `set_order_expiry(deadline)`; OKX sends `pair_limit_ioc` (`create_order` limit, `timeInForce` IOC, `tdMode` cross, `clOrdId`, `reduceOnly`, `expTime` header restored on every exit).
After the order call the child reads the order record by client id and scans fills (`scan_recovery_fills` with attribution, `scan_okx_client_fills` keeping rows) and answers once terminal, with the per-fill records in the same submit answer, so a fully covered first leg commits without waiting for a query.
Go sends with `runPythonSideEffectWithTimeout` after its own deadline check; queries and position reads enter through `acquirePythonSlot(ctx)` and `spawnPythonProcess` within the caller context.

**Outcome mapping** (`pairMapScriptOutcome`). Venue-observed fields (credential account, order instrument, client id, side, original size, limit price, time in force, reduce-only flag, contract size, price currency, native unit) are kept apart from the request echo; any missing or different field makes the outcome `unknown` and queryable.
Terminal nonzero execution is `filled` or terminal `partial`, terminal zero execution `rejected`, a live order `open`, a proven unsent request `never_sent`, an unknown client id `absent` with the fill-scan coverage and contrary evidence; fills visible below the executed size stay non-terminal.
`accepted_at_ms` comes only from the venue record (Hyperliquid order `timestamp`, OKX `cTime`).
Go normalizes once: base quantity and base-unit notional are native values × contract size; it seals `pairConfirmedFill` and `pairFeeFill` records (`fill_id` Hyperliquid `tid`, OKX `billId`), sets `FeeEvidenceVersion` to the fee record count and `FeeEvidenceID` to `pairFeeEvidenceID(action)`, and sets `FeeFinal` only for a terminal answer with complete coverage, execution records summing to the cumulative totals and a fee record for every fill.
Fee signs: Hyperliquid `fee` is the total fee charged ("the total fee, inclusive of builderFee", info endpoint; maker rebates are negative rates, fees page), kept as is; OKX fill `fee` is negative when charged ("Negative number represents the user transaction fee charged by the platform"), negated once; a fill without fee amount or currency has no fee record.

**Live-shaped venues.** `hyperliquidPairVenue` and `okxPairVenue` (`newHyperliquidPairVenue`, `newOKXPairVenue`) are built only for a valid live-shaped fixture admission, one contract member each, with substituted driver scripts (production scripts refused), a clock and the live partition; they read no private valuation.
Under `liveShapedFixture` only, a member `account_key` may use `hyperliquid:<network>:<address>` or `okx:swap:<network>:key-<16 hex>` (`pairAccountKeyError`); research admission keeps the synthetic and virtual checks and refuses `pairResearchDeps.Account`.
The fixture may inject `pairResearchDeps.Account pairAccountReader`; `evidenceSource()` then returns `pairLiveShapedEvidenceSource`, which keeps the frozen capability and conversion selection and the document digest and accepts an account record only with the member key, a valid hash, `pairAccountMatches` and eligibility at the read time.
`OKXPositionJSON`, `OKXPosition` and the legacy positions output gain `inst_id`, `contracts`, `contract_size`, `margin_mode` and `account`; legacy fields and closes are unchanged.

**Position reads.** `pairVenue.Position(ctx, m, nowMs) (pairVenuePosition, error)`; `pairVenuePosition{V 2 "v", Key "key", SignedNativeQty "signed_native_qty", ContractSize "contract_size", PositionMode "position_mode", MarginMode "margin_mode", ObservedMs "observed_ms", FirstReceiptMs "first_receipt_ms", EvidenceID "evidence_id", EvidenceHash "evidence_hash"}`, sealed over its canonical JSON.
Hyperliquid `strict_pair_position` and OKX `fetch_pair_position` refuse a malformed row, a row with no instrument, a duplicate instrument, a foreign instrument (OKX), a non-`oneWay` or non-`net` row, a missing list or a venue error; only a successful complete read proves an absent instrument flat.
Virtual venues make no private read and are never asked.

**Barrier** (`scheduler/pair_position.go`). `pairProgress.PositionMatch` (`position_match`: `generation`, `status` unchecked, matched, mismatch or failed, `reason`, `checked_at_ms`, `episode_ms`, `held_since_ms`) is saved through the progress writer; missing loads as unchecked and the flat reset keeps it.
The generation hashes the partition, contract, member identities and books and every action's status, quantities, terminal flag and native ownership; the lifecycle and barrier fields are not part of it, because neither moves a venue position or the reachable range, so the flat-to-opening flip of a first leg keeps the step's matching read.
`stepPairPositions` runs for live-shaped members after the lifecycle checks unless the partition is latched or the step is save-blocked: at startup, when the generation changed and at each decision cadence, never in a busy loop.
It runs inside the pair step, so its reads always follow `preparePairRisk` (host step 3); live-shaped venues take every mark and exit depth bound from that view.
`pairReadPositions` captures the generation, reads both venues outside `mu` under one `collection_timeout_ms` context, rereads time, discards a changed generation, and requires `observed_ms <= first_receipt_ms <= now` with both within `quote_age_ms`.
A member matches when the venue base quantity lies in `[low, high]` from the book, confirmed unapplied quantity and each possibly sent action's zero-to-remaining fill (endpoints by `basketQtyEqual`); nothing is adopted into the book.
A mismatch opens recovery and sends one CRITICAL alert per episode (`position_match|<episode_ms>`), and the recovery cannot reset to flat while the barrier is a mismatch; a failed, stale, future, malformed or timed-out read holds increases with the saved reason; a later matching read clears only the barrier.
An increase needs an in-memory match for the current generation within `quote_age_ms`: before reservation (reading again if needed), after the submitting mark with the dispatched action excluded as proven unsent, and in the transport proof; a restart starts with no authority.
A one-sided opening held by the barrier past the acceptance deadline plus the absence grace opens recovery.
Queries, settlement, closes and protection run before and regardless of the barrier; a save failure keeps the hold and sets `needsDurable`; the startup line and `storage-inspect` show a mismatch or failure.

**Contracts for later children (#1879, #1880, #1881, #1882, #1863).**

| Name | Go signature or type | Rule |
|---|---|---|
| `pairBoundEvidence` | `ProposedBound`, `RoundedBound float64`, `Side`, `CapabilityHash string` (`proposed_bound`, `rounded_bound`, `side`, `capability_hash`) | saved in version-2 `pairTargets.BoundEvidence`; `Members[id].PriceBound == RoundedBound`; dispatch reuses it |
| `pairRoundBoundRule` | `func pairRoundBoundRule(instrument string, meta pairWireMeta, side string, bound float64) (float64, error)` | the grid rounding behind `pairRoundBound`; #1879 rounds reference bounds with it |
| `pairBoundOnGrid` | `func pairBoundOnGrid(m pairContractMember, side string, bound float64) error` | a live-shaped request bound must pass it |
| `pairExactOrderQty` | `func pairExactOrderQty(m pairContractMember, baseQty float64) (pairOrderQty, error)` | exact native and base quantities for every request |
| `pairVenue.Position` | `Position(ctx context.Context, m pairContractMember, nowMs int64) (pairVenuePosition, error)` | live-shaped venues only; replay and paper hosts never read private positions |
| `pairPositionMatch` | `Generation`, `Status`, `Reason`, `CheckedAtMs`, `EpisodeMs`, `HeldSinceMs` | `pairProgress.PositionMatch`, preserved by flat resets |
| `pairPositionAuthorized` | `func (bc *basketCoordinator) pairPositionAuthorized(rt *basketRuntime, exclude string, nowMs int64) string` | empty only for a fresh in-memory match of the current generation; #1879 reductions do not need it |
| `pairAccountReader` | `Account(ctx, m pairContractMember, refMs int64) (pairAccountEvidence, error)` | fixture-only `pairResearchDeps.Account` |
| `pairLiveTransport` | `Script`, `PositionScript string`, `Partition RiskPartition`, `Clock func() time.Time`, `Substituted bool` | the only way to build a live-shaped pair venue |
| Script flags | `--pair-order-json`, `--pair-query-json` with `--since-ms`, `--pair-position-json` | each probed in both probe paths (`pairOrderHLProbeArgvs`, `pairOrderOKXProbeArgvs`, `pairPositionOKXProbeArgv`) |
| Obligations | `protective_bound_unavailable` (member), `reduction_below_minimum` | recovery evidence; cleared when the exposure is flat |

**Tests.** `TestBasketPairBound*` and `TestBasketPairPosition*` run locally (CI skips `^TestBasket`); `TestBasketPairVenueReal*` (`pyintegration`, local only) runs the real scripts and adapters with substituted SDK clients, a driver clock and network and signing guards.
`TestBasketPairVenueRealCoordinatorRunsBothVenuesAndRecoversBySavedIdentity` drives the coordinator through both real scripts: the first leg commits from its submit answer, the hedge follows at the next slot, an exit crashes after venue acceptance, and after a restart the saved client id query settles it with no second order.

### Coordinator reduction references (#1879)

Issue #1879 gives every coordinator reduction (`pair_exit`, `pair_protective`) a saved fresh reference and a bounded reduce-only IOC order, and sizes live-shaped Hyperliquid reductions through the existing close size owner.
Native emergency closes (`pair_kill_switch`, `pair_operator`) keep their separate route: no missing reference, bound, accounting record or pair reservation blocks them, and native evidence, units, fee settlement and paper emergency prices stay with #1914.
No deployed configuration, default, credential, promotion or live activation changes.

**Reference source.** A reduction consumes the reference that `preparePairRisk` published for the member (side `sell` for a long exposure, else `buy`); no consumer reads its own quote.
`pairMemberValuation.ReferenceValid` is true only for a reference read that succeeded with an ordered best bid and ask in a preparation that did not change, cancel or time out; conversion, capability, account and position-barrier evidence are not reduction gates.
`pairReductionReference` binds a detached copy with the view `Generation`, `EvidenceHash`, side and exposure (`pairReductionRef`); `pairReductionStillValid` rechecks the publication, side, exposure, `pairCheckReference` (identity, source, payload, native levels, `quote_age_ms` at fresh coordinator time) and crossing.
The view is process memory, so a restart needs a new publication before any reduction.
Exit valuation and tranche proofs keep the step-start time (#1876); reference age and crossing use fresh coordinator time.

**Bounds.** `pairReductionBound(m, side, ref, nowMs, lim)` refuses an invalid width or a stale reference, takes best bid × (1 − `protective_width_bps`/10000) for a sell floor or best ask × (1 + `protective_width_bps`/10000) for a buy cap, rounds safe-side with `pairRoundBoundRule` (sell up, buy down) and requires the rounded bound to cross the reference level (equality crosses).
Protective and hold-age reductions use it; a policy exit keeps its saved rounded bound evidence (`pairPolicyExitEvidence`) and holds while that bound does not cross the current reference.
The virtual residual rounding exception and exact live-shaped quantities are unchanged.

**Saved request.** A new reduction request saves `Reference` (the detached observation), `ReferenceMaxAgeMs` (`reference_max_age_ms`, the contract `quote_age_ms`), `ReferenceReceiptMs` (`reference_receipt_ms`) and `ReferencePx` (`reference_px`, the native best bid for a sell or best ask for a buy).
`pairCheckReductionAuthority` (inside `pairValidateActionRequest`, at the reservation transaction with the refreshed coordinator time) requires all of them, the committed published reference of that member, the legal bound (saved policy evidence or the recomputed width bound, equal to the action) and crossing; increases and native requests refuse every reference field.
The coordinator rechecks the reference before reservation and after the submitting mark (a failure ends the action never sent), and the transport proof and `pairLiveBase.submit` check `pairReductionAgeRefusal` with fresh time; an expired reference sends nothing even before the acceptance deadline.
Older saved requests stay readable for queries and settlement; missing authority never permits a send.

**Transport projection.** `pairTransportRequestJSON` removes `reference` from the order and query argument, so the argument stays small for any admitted window (Linux refuses one argument above 131072 bytes); the full request stays in the state file.
`shared_tools/pair_orders.py` accepts `reference_receipt_ms`, `reference_max_age_ms` and `reference_px` only on reductions, all together, with a canonical positive level that the bound crosses; the order route (`parse_request(raw, provider, route="order")`) refuses a reduction without them, and the query route accepts it.
`reference_refusal(req, now)` runs after `--probe-only` returns: both check scripts call it before the adapter is built and again immediately before the venue order call, so time spent waiting for a process slot or in adapter setup counts.
The Hyperliquid probe vector (shared by the order and query argvs) carries the three fields.

**Hyperliquid sizing.** A live-shaped Hyperliquid reduction calls `pairSizeHLReduction`: it releases `mu`, takes `lockHyperliquidProtectionSync(symbol)`, captures the exposure (`pairExposure`), peers (`pairHLPeerBooks`: books on the same account and coin, member excluded) and records, refetches through `pairVenue.Position` outside `mu`, rechecks the records, contract and publication, and calls `resolveHLCloseOrder` (`planHLCloseOrder` stays the sole size and mode owner).
Skip, Defer, a netted Cross and a changed record send nothing and cancel nothing; a capped size floors to lots, repeats the exit tranche proof and the venue minimum; the uncapped reduce-only fallback after a failed refetch keeps the saved price bound.
The symbol lock is released after reservation and before transport, so native closes and protection never wait for the order script.
The pair IOC route never cancels protection, so `rearmAfterSizedClose` is not used; paper and virtual reductions call no planner and read no private position.
