**Holds.** `holdPairReduction` saves one `reduction_reference_unavailable` member obligation (detail updated, episode kept), sends one CRITICAL alert per episode (`reduction_reference|<member>|<episode>`, the recovery start for protective reductions) and, for protective reductions, the retry backoff. Each hold saves the obligation `retry_at_ms` (the backoff time for a protective reduction, the hold time plus `retry_initial_ms` for an exit). While the member has exposure, `pairWakeBoundaries` wakes the coordinator at the later of that time and the last step plus `retry_initial_ms`, so a held exit retries soon and never more often than `retry_initial_ms`.
A reservation clears it and the legacy `protective_bound_unavailable`, and so does a flat member; recovery-age escalation, `reduction_below_minimum`, queries, settlement and native protection continue.

**Contracts for later children (#1914, #1880, #1881, #1882, #1863).**

| Name | Go signature or type | Rule |
|---|---|---|
| `pairReductionBound` | `func pairReductionBound(m pairContractMember, side string, ref pairObservation, nowMs int64, lim pairOperationalLimits) (float64, string)` | the only width bound for protective and hold-age reductions |
| `pairReductionRef` | `Ref pairObservation`, `Side`, `Px string`, `Generation uint64`, `EvidenceHash string`, `Exposure float64` | `pairCandidate.Ref`; built by `pairReductionReference`, rechecked by `pairReductionStillValid` |
| `pairMemberValuation.ReferenceValid` | `bool` | the only reduction authority flag of a published view |
| Request fields | `reference`, `reference_max_age_ms`, `reference_receipt_ms`, `reference_px` | required on new coordinator reductions; refused on increases and native requests |
| `pairCheckReductionAuthority` | `func pairCheckReductionAuthority(rt *basketRuntime, m pairContractMember, a *basketAction, req pairActionRequest, nowMs int64) error` | reservation rule |
| `pairTransportRequestJSON`, `pairReductionAgeRefusal` | `func pairTransportRequestJSON(raw string) (string, error)`; `func pairReductionAgeRefusal(raw string, nowMs int64) string` | every order and query argument; the Go transport age check |
| `pair_orders.reference_refusal` | `reference_refusal(req, now) -> str` | child age check before the adapter and before the order call |
| `pairSizeHLReduction` | `func (bc *basketCoordinator) pairSizeHLReduction(rt *basketRuntime, c pairCandidate, qty float64, v pairVenue, in *basketCycleInputs) (float64, func(), string)` | live-shaped Hyperliquid only; returns the symbol unlock for the caller to release before transport |
| Obligation | `reduction_reference_unavailable` (member) | recovery evidence; cleared by a reservation or a flat member |

Rules: #1880's virtual venue keeps the reference and width bound and never calls `pairSizeHLReduction` or `Position`; #1881 and #1882 publish the view at host step 3 before any reduction; #1914 keeps native requests free of reference fields.

**Tests.** `TestBasketPairReduction*` (untagged, local; CI skips `^TestBasket`) covers fresh and missing references, backoff and alerts, hold-age width bounds, policy-bound crossing holds, the transport-boundary expiry, Hyperliquid capped, uncapped, fallback, skip and changed-record sizing with lock release and no protection call, native closes without a reference, virtual routes, restart and the sealed-window argument size; `TestBasketPairVenueRealReferenceAgeChecksInTheChild` (`pyintegration`) runs both real scripts.

### Virtual pair venue (#1880)

Issue #1880 adds the deterministic virtual immediate-or-cancel venue that research pairs execute against: `pairVirtualVenue` matches price-limited orders against recorded order books, uses each recorded level once and returns the same execution after a restart.
It changes no deployed configuration, default, credential, promotion or live trading behaviour; production pair activation stays refused.

**Construction and attachment.** `newPairVirtualVenue(pairVirtualVenueConfig{Partition, RunTag, MemberID, Provider, AccountKey, Instrument, Market, AdverseBandBps, SubmitLatencyMs, ResponseLatencyMs})` validates a research partition (never live), a synthetic account, the run tag pattern, a market, `0 <= adverse_band_bps < 10000` and latencies in `[0, 86400000]` ms.
`Name`, `Provider`, `AccountKey`, `Virtual` (true), `LiveShaped` (false) and `ExecutionModel` (`virtual_ioc_adverse_max_v1`) are pure, so admission and cost binding call the host factory before any store exists.
After the store opens and `LoadBaskets` builds the runtime, the host calls `bc.attachPairVirtualVenues()` once, before `startupCheck`; it attaches every member venue with the store, the runtime, the member configuration and the coordinator clock (`bc.pairClock`), which is the only venue clock; a nil `bc.pairClock` refuses attachment and attaches nothing.
Attachment checks the run tag against the admission run id, the partition, the frozen contract member, `checkTransport`, the durable cost binding (model version 1001, `pair-cost-` id), the policy `adverse_band_bps` when the policy declares it, and the saved binding record; any difference refuses and writes nothing.
An unattached venue refuses `NewClientOrderID`, `Submit` and `Query`; `pairVenueFor` and `startupCheck` refuse an unattached virtual venue, and nothing executes before the member cost binding is durable.

**Market window.** `pairVirtualMarket.Events(ctx, key pairMemberKey, afterMs, throughMs int64) (pairVirtualMarketWindow, error)` returns `pairVirtualMarketWindow{Events []pairObservation, Coverage pairCoverage}`: every record with `afterMs < venue time <= throughMs` and first receipt `<= throughMs`, in recorded source order (point sources by receipt, windows by source cutoff).
The venue re-reads from the saved acceptance time, validates every event with the strict parser (`pairCheckObservationEvent`, the quote check without the coverage-complete gate), refuses a foreign key, malformed payload, future receipt or broken order, and extracts books: one per public snapshot (identity = payload hash) and one per window record (identity = the record identity, rehashed with `bookIdentity` when the record is not truncated).
A repeated identity with different levels or event time refuses the window; a copy keeps one identity and its earliest receipt.
A book's availability is max(event time, record receipt, receipt of the first window carrying it).
Coverage proves continuity only for window sources, only when `complete`, prefix complete and gap free, and only inside one merged span of the carried windows' own complete coverage; point snapshots, empty answers, timeouts and the request bound prove nothing.
The venue saves a `query` record (through time and digest of available books) when it sees new input; a later read whose books available by that time differ is a changed committed prefix and supplies no fill or absence proof.
#1881 must expose only the frozen prefix at the simulated clock and #1882 must commit each poll and its coverage before returning it; the contract above is the one both hosts implement.

**Identifiers.** `NewClientOrderID()` returns the newest allocation for the run and full member key when it has neither an action binding (provider, account, client id, member and partition checked) nor a venue record (earlier allocations are spent, because a new id is committed only after the newest is spent), else commits `vp-<run_tag>-<sha256(member key)[:8]>-<seq>` before returning it; a collision with any other allocation or action refuses.
Both coordinator sites (`runPairAction`, `beginPairNativeReduction`) allocate and reserve under `bc.mu`; the unique action client index stays the backstop.

**Acceptance.** `Submit` checks identity, the durable cost binding and the deadline (a submission at or after it saves a `deadline` record and answers `never_sent`), then validates the saved request: version 2, identity and contract, purpose and reduce-only direction, side, exact lot quantities (or, for a reduction only, an off-lot quantity equal to the member's full known inventory), a finite positive bound on the venue grid, and a durable submitting action row with the same request and allocation.
When the origin slot holds an actionable version-2 decision of the action's kind (entry for entries, exit for exits) the bound must equal its saved rounded bound evidence and side (`bound_source` `decision_evidence`); a version-1 decision authorizes nothing; an entry needs its decision.
Every reduction also needs its saved #1879 reference authority: `reference` passes `pairCheckReference` at the acceptance time, `reference_receipt_ms` is its first receipt, `reference_max_age_ms` is `quote_age_ms`, `reference_px` is its best level on the order side, and the bound crosses that level; a protective or hold-age reduction's bound must equal `pairReductionBound` of that reference (`bound_source` `reduction_reference`), and a missing or failing authority is a durable `refusal` answered `never_sent`.
A refusal saves a `refusal` record and answers `never_sent`; the first valid submission saves the request, its hash and acceptance time s and answers `open`.
`Query` for an unaccepted client answers `unknown` before the deadline and saves a `deadline` record and answers `never_sent` from the deadline on, so no later submission can accept it; a changed request under a saved identity answers `unknown`.

**Matching.** An eligible book has event time t with `t > s`, `t >= s + submit latency`, `t < deadline`, availability `<= now` and a complete side (`min(declared depth, book depth)` levels, strictly ordered, uncrossed).
The decision uses the eligible book with the smallest availability, then event time, then recorded order, so query timing never changes it; earlier accepted orders of the member decide first.
With r = max(adverse_band_bps / 10000, member `slippage_pct`), a buy walks asks at native price × (1 + r) and a sell walks bids at native price × (1 − r) while the adjusted price is within the saved bound, using only remaining level capacity (capacity key: run tag, member key, source contract, source identity, side, level index).
A reduction is contained to the member inventory at t: earlier virtual executions plus native and unexplained journal rows before t, with an ambiguous row taken at the less permissive endpoint.
Any short fill is floored to the lot; the result is `filled`, terminal `partial` or terminal `rejected` and never waits for another book.
Base quantity is native × contract size once; `CumNotional` sums base quantity × the adjusted native price; `FeePresent` stays false so the member cost binding charges its fee once with no second slippage.
The `match` record (full outcome, book levels, fills, digest) and its `consume` records commit in one transaction that rechecks capacity and saved level content; a failed commit exposes nothing.

**Visibility and absence.** Visibility is availability + response latency, checked for overflow; before it the venue answers `open` with no fill totals, then terminal with immutable fills (`<client>-f<n>`, event t, visible at the visibility time) and a `visible` record.
`NextVisibleMs() (int64, bool)` returns the earliest saved visibility after the clock, and `pairWakeBoundaries` adds it; hosts still schedule market deliveries and deadlines.
With no saved decision, absence needs continuous window coverage from at or before s through the deadline plus the recovery grace and `pairAbsenceReleasable` (strict `now > deadline + grace`); the venue then saves an `absence` record and answers `absent` with complete coverage; anything else stays `open`.
A clock earlier than the latest saved venue record answers `unknown`.

**Coordinator boundary.** The venue never reads a private position (`Position` refuses and counts the call) and the barrier never selects it.
Before each `Submit` or `Query` the coordinator hands the venue the member's native and unexplained journal rows (`noteNativeInventory`), and refuses an answer observed or visible after its own time.
A virtual protective or hold-age reduction takes the #1879 saved reference and width bound like every coordinator reduction; a missing or stale reference holds before reservation with `reduction_reference_unavailable`.
`pairVirtualConservation` compares, per member, virtual executions (hidden included) plus native journal rows with the book plus each action's executed minus applied quantity, and refuses an action that confirms more than the venue executed; a disagreement holds increases (`pairIncreaseHold`) with one alert while queries, settlement and native protection continue.
Native closes enter only through their journal rows; a native-booked action's applied quantity is never added.

**Contracts for later children (#1879, #1881, #1882, #1914, #1863).**

| Name | Go signature or type | Rule |
|---|---|---|
| `pairVirtualVenueConfig` | `Partition RiskPartition`, `RunTag`, `MemberID`, `Provider`, `AccountKey`, `Instrument string`, `Market pairVirtualMarket`, `AdverseBandBps float64`, `SubmitLatencyMs`, `ResponseLatencyMs int64` | built before admission; identity fields only |
| `newPairVirtualVenue` | `func newPairVirtualVenue(c pairVirtualVenueConfig) (*pairVirtualVenue, error)` | the only constructor |
| `attachPairVirtualVenues` | `func (bc *basketCoordinator) attachPairVirtualVenues() error` | once, after `LoadBaskets` and the clock, before `startupCheck`; the factory must return the same instances |
| `pairVirtualMarket` | `Events(ctx context.Context, key pairMemberKey, afterMs, throughMs int64) (pairVirtualMarketWindow, error)` | full available window, recorded order, no future receipt |
| `pairVirtualMarketWindow` | `Events []pairObservation`, `Coverage pairCoverage` | window coverage proves continuity only inside the carried windows' complete spans |
| `NextVisibleMs` | `func (v *pairVirtualVenue) NextVisibleMs() (int64, bool)` | earliest saved visibility after the clock |
| Client ids | `vp-<run_tag>-<member hash8>-<seq>` | allocation-only state is research state |
| Bound sources | `decision_evidence`, `reduction_reference` | an entry or a policy exit takes its slot's saved rounded bound evidence; protective and hold-age reductions take the #1879 width bound of their saved reference |
| Conservation | `pairVirtualConservation(rt) string` | native journal rows only; #1914 may change native applied quantities without changing it |

**Tests.** `TestBasketPairVirtualVenue*` are store-backed Go integration tests with a scripted recorded market; they spawn no Python and run locally (CI skips `^TestBasket`).

### Native emergency evidence and accounting (#1914)

Issue #1914 books a pair member's kill-switch and circuit-breaker closes only from confirmed evidence: a live-shaped member from the native close's venue fill records, a paper member at its own checked mark.
Native sends keep their authority; no missing reference, bound, reservation, receipt save or accounting record blocks one, and native requests carry no reference field.
It changes no deployed configuration, default, credential, promotion or live trading behaviour; production pair activation stays refused.

**Native evidence.** `close_hyperliquid_position.py` and `close_okx_position.py` add `close.evidence` (`v` 1, `venue_order_ids`, `accepted_at_ms`, `executed_native_qty`, `fills` in the `pair_orders.fill_record` shape, `complete`, `detail`) built by `shared_tools/native_close_evidence.py`; the ordinary `fill` fields keep their meaning.
Hyperliquid reuses the single user-fill read of the close order; OKX reads each close order (`fetch_order_info`) and its trades and retries a few times while an order is not terminal or its fills are short; `market_close_orders` returns every order a close sent, and `market_close` returns the first as before.
`pairNativeEvidenceResult` maps evidence once: base = native × the admitted contract size, notional = native notional × contract size, explicit fees (zero and rebates included) become fee records, and the accepted time is a venue time (the order time, else the first fill time), never a local time.
A requested size, an order price, an exit code or a reported fill without fill records books nothing and is `unknown`; evidence with no fill records is `no_fill` only when it is complete (every order terminal, zero executed), else `unknown`; a remainder the venue reports executed without records adds `native_fill_unconfirmed`; a transport error that still returns fill records keeps them.
`OKXCloseFill.BaseSz` is set only by the pair-aware closer; `parseOKXCloseOutput` zeroes a script value, and `TotalSz` and `--sz` stay in contracts.

**Native actions.** A native action's purpose (`pair_kill_switch`, or `pair_operator` with recovery-note trigger `circuit_breaker`) alone makes it native (`basketAction.pairNative`); reservation also needs a strictly decoded note with `native_booked`.
The wrappers match only live-partition pair runtimes, the circuit router also matches the strategy id, and a peer strategy holding the coin in the partition makes the fill `native_receipt_ambiguous` and books nothing for the member.
A confirmed fill moves the action to `settling`, opens recovery explicitly and is booked by `settlePairActionLive` with the scoped fill identity `partition|member|provider|account|instrument|native|fill id`, the frozen settlement conversion at the venue fill time and the confirmed fee seam; book, trade, ledger, applied totals and progress save together, and a progress-only write never saves `committed` for any action.
Status `held` (added by a one-time table rebuild of `basket_member_actions`) ends a native action whose fill is unknown or whose fee evidence stays incomplete after booking; it is terminal, never pending and never counts as booked.
`idx_pair_actions_one_protective` counts native rows only while `reserved`, `submitting` or `unresolved`, so a settling native accounting row never blocks a later native close or a coordinator reduction.

**Receipts.** When no action can be reserved or saved (busy entry lock, pending protective reduction, failed write), a confirmed fill becomes a sealed `pairNativeReceipt` in `pairProgress.NativeReceipts` with obligation `native_receipt_unattached`; a receipt kept only in memory raises one CRITICAL alert and every later progress write retries its save.
`attachPairNativeReceipts` runs on every non-save-blocked step: one transaction (`StateStore.AttachPairNativeReceipt`) refuses a fill already saved on a native row of the member (an identical repeat drops the receipt, a different one holds as `native_receipt_conflict`), inserts a `settling` accounting action and removes the receipt; no order is sent.
A process loss before a receipt save leaves a visible held result (the unrecorded-close recovery and the venue position barrier); no unsaved record is described as durable.
Committed native rows from before #1914 with confirmed fills and no ledger load with `native_legacy_unverified` and are never booked again.

**Paper and generic closes.** `closeVirtualPositionInner` never books a pair member: it sets the strategy's pending close under `PlatformPendingClosePair` (`"pair"`), which survives the kill-switch pending reset, cooldown expiry and restart.
`stepPairEmergency` books a paper member only at its own `usdMarkAt` value: it saves a staged `pairPaperClose` (member key, close time, USD price, native mark, price currency, conversion record, view generation and evidence hash) before the book change, books through `bookVirtualClose` inside the paper book owner (native-origin inventory rows, so `pairVirtualConservation` is unchanged), and a failed save retries the same staged price: the record stays `staged` in memory and in every progress write until a book save commits (each member's commit, pending-close clear and obligation clear is applied only after the member loop, just before the book save, and a failed save restores them; the flat-book commit waits while `needsDurable` is set); a missing, stale or substituted mark keeps the book with `emergency_close_pending`.
The kill-switch owners (`forceCloseKillSwitchPositions`, `forceCloseSettledPositions`, `forceClosePaperScopePositions`) pass trigger `kill_switch` explicitly (`forceCloseAllPositionsFor`, `closeVirtualPositionAtMarkFor`); breaker closes pass `circuit_breaker`; ordinary close reasons are unchanged.
A live-shaped member waits for native evidence, and its pending close clears only when its book is flat and no native accounting is open.
`applyHyperliquidCircuitCloseFill` refuses a pair member, and Hyperliquid account sync skips pair members.
`RiskReasonPairValuationStale` now also sets the native circuit pending and the pair pending close; it still books nothing without a checked price.

**Completion.** The paper kill switch sets `KillSwitchCloseApplied` and sends its close-complete message only when `pairEmergencyPendingFor` reports no member; the live venue-flat branch suppresses the owner-less auto-reset and appends `pairKillSwitchAccountingNote` while it reports any.
`ResetPortfolioKillSwitchManual` stays the operator's terminal path and is not gated; it clears the latch and leaves every pair obligation, so the research result stays incomplete. Since #1927 these five kinds end only through a saved venue-history conclusion or an audited operator decision (§ Native fill resolution (#1927) below), before #1863 activates a live pair.
`pairIncompleteEvidence` reports, after the policy timeout, native and emergency obligations, settlement and fee obligations of native actions, unattached receipts and staged paper closes.
Funding inventory treats native actions like every action and, through `from_ms` on native unknown, unconfirmed and ambiguous obligations and unattached receipts, never advances past an unknown native fill time.
Pair progress is version 2 (version 1 loads; an older binary refuses version 2), and `storage-inspect` and startup report receipts and staged closes.

**Contracts for later children (#1881, #1882, #1863).**

| Name | Go signature or type | Rule |
|---|---|---|
| `nativeCloseEvidence` | `V int`, `VenueOrderIDs []string`, `AcceptedAtMs int64`, `ExecutedNativeQty string`, `Fills []pairScriptFill`, `Complete bool`, `Detail string` (`close.evidence`) | the only native fill authority; native units |
| `pairNativeEvidenceResult` | `func pairNativeEvidenceResult(m pairContractMember, ev *nativeCloseEvidence, reportedFill, alreadyFlat bool, closeErr error, nowMs int64) pairNativeResult` | base and notional × contract size once; no records = `unknown` |
| `pairKillSwitchHLCloser`, `pairKillSwitchOKXCloser`, `pairCircuitHLCloser`, `pairCircuitOKXCloser` | `func (bc *basketCoordinator) pairCircuitOKXCloser(stratID string, inner OKXLiveCloser) OKXLiveCloser` (same shape) | live partition only; pass through without a pair runtime |
| `pairCircuitHLCloserFor`, `pairCircuitOKXCloserFor` | `func pairCircuitOKXCloserFor(state *AppState, stratID string, inner OKXLiveCloser) OKXLiveCloser` | the circuit owners route each job through `AppState.pairViews` |
| `pairNativeReceipt` | `ID`, `Key`, `MemberID`, `Purpose`, `Trigger`, `OrderSide`, `PositionSide`, `ExecutedQty`, `Unconfirmed`, `Outcome`, `CreatedAtMs`, `Hash` (snake_case) | saved in `pairProgress.NativeReceipts`; attach only through `AttachPairNativeReceipt` |
| `pairPaperClose` | `MemberID`, `Key`, `Symbol`, `PositionID`, `Side`, `Qty`, `Trigger`, `CloseAtMs`, `PriceUSD`, `NativeMark`, `PriceCurrency`, `Source`, `Generation`, `EvidenceHash`, `Conversion`, `Status` (`staged`, `committed`), `CommittedAtMs` | saved in `pairProgress.PaperCloses` before the book change; retried unchanged |
| `PlatformPendingClosePair` | `"pair"` key of `RiskState.PendingCircuitCloses` | set by every generic virtual close of a pair member; cleared only by a committed flat member |
| `pairEmergencyPendingFor` | `func pairEmergencyPendingFor(state *AppState, part RiskPartition) []string` | hosts gate close-complete messages and owner-less resets on an empty result |
| `basketActionHeld` | `"held"` | terminal, not pending, never booked |
| Obligations | `native_fill_unknown`, `native_fill_unconfirmed`, `native_receipt_unattached`, `native_receipt_conflict`, `native_receipt_ambiguous`, `native_legacy_unverified`, `emergency_close_pending` | keep accounting incomplete; only a committed booking or attachment clears the receipt and emergency kinds |

Rules: #1881 and #1882 call `preparePairRisk` before risk so a paper emergency close has a fresh own mark, run `bc.step` after the paper kill switch, and report `pairIncompleteEvidence`; they never book a pair member through a generic close owner.
#1863 keeps the coordinator as the only pair book writer.

**Tests.** `TestBasketPairNativeEmergency*` and `TestBasketPairPaperEmergency*` (untagged, local; CI skips `^TestBasket`), the extended `TestBasketPairKillSwitch*`, `TestBasketMemberViewIncompleteValuationEscalatesAfterTheDeferral` and `TestBasketPairVirtualVenueNativeBookChangesCountOnce`, and `TestBasketPairEmergencyReal*` (`pyintegration`: the real close scripts with substituted venue responses and refused network and signing).

### Native fill resolution (#1927)

Issue #1927 ends the five native obligations of #1914 through a saved venue-history conclusion or an audited operator decision, and books newly confirmed fills once through the existing settlement ledger.
It changes no deployed configuration, default, credential, promotion or live trading behaviour; production pair dispatch stays refused, and a refused pair runs no recovery read and applies no decision.

**Attempts.** `finishPairNative` saves one `pairNativeAttempt` per native send in `pairProgress.NativeAttempts`: a deterministic id `pna-…` (pair, member key, purpose, trigger, send time and `NativeAttemptSeq`), the member key, provider, account, instrument, contract fingerprint, purpose, trigger, sides, the linked action or receipt, the ambiguity reason, the send, end and acceptance-deadline times, every valid venue order id (sorted, at most 8), `orders_complete`, the raw close evidence, the close error, the provenance (`close_evidence`, `close_error`, `close_no_evidence`, `operator_orders`), the evidence revision and per-attempt retry state (`retry_count`, `retry_at_ms`, `last_error`, `last_read_at_ms`; never `pairProgress.Retries`).
An attempt is kept only while a native obligation or a receipt names it, so progress stays bounded; resolution audits live in their own table.
`close.evidence` adds `orders_complete`: Hyperliquid sets it once the close order id is known; OKX sets it when every close order returned, and `market_close_orders` raises `OKXCloseOrdersError` carrying the orders already sent when a later order fails, so `close_okx_position.py` reports their ids with `orders_complete` false.
A missing field means complete only for a close that returned without an error.
Unknown and ambiguous outcomes keep the order ids; an ambiguous close keeps its whole receipt in the attempt.
A new action-less `native_fill_unknown` and every new `native_receipt_ambiguous` obligation is keyed by its attempt id, so two closes never share one obligation or alert.
An action-linked obligation keeps the action id; an unattached confirmed receipt keeps its fill-derived `pnr-…` id.
An obligation saved before #1927 with no attempt has no provable identity: a `pnr-…` action-less or ambiguous one may cover several closes and only a write-off or acknowledgment ends it, and an action-linked one needs operator order ids; no order id is ever inferred from time, size or the unused reserved client id.

**History route.** `--pair-native-history-json` on `check_hyperliquid.py` and `check_okx.py` takes a strict version-1 request (`pair_id`, `partition`, `member_id`, `provider`, `account_key`, `instrument`, `attempt_id` `pn[ao]-` plus 32 hex, `order_side`, 1..8 `venue_order_ids`, `from_ms`, `through_ms`, `revision`; unknown fields, duplicate keys and malformed ids are refused) and prints `native_history` (version 1: echo, `status` `complete` or `incomplete`, `detail`, per-order state, terminal flag, side, instrument, executed native quantity and acceptance time, fill-history coverage and version-1 evidence).
Probe mode exits before any adapter, signing or read; the route places, cancels or changes nothing, and the ordinary pair order and query routes still refuse native purposes.
Hyperliquid reads `query_order_by_oid` for each order and one complete `scan_recovery_fills` window from `from_ms` (truncated, capped or stalled history is incomplete) and matches fills by oid; OKX reads `fetch_order_info` and `fetch_order_trades` per `ordId` and treats a full page of 100 trades as clipped.
Both require the credentials to answer for the request account, every order terminal and the fill records to sum exactly to the executed size; `pairCheckNativeHistory` then rechecks the echo, the order set, each order's side, instrument and acceptance inside the window (send time minus 10 s through the acceptance deadline plus 10 s), the executed sum and each fill's order, side and time.
An empty or incomplete answer never proves a zero fill: a zero fill needs every order terminal with zero executed in a complete answer.

**Recovery reads.** `stepPairNativeRecovery` runs inside `stepPair` after the durable-settlement retry and before receipt attachment, and only when the step is not save-blocked and no settlement waits to become durable.
It applies staged operator decisions first, then reads at most one due attempt per pair per step: the attempt must name every order (`orders_complete`), be unambiguous, back a `native_fill_unknown` or `native_fill_unconfirmed` obligation, belong to a member with no native send, read or staged decision in flight, and its action must be `held` or `unresolved`, or `settling` only while its attempt holds a staged resolution for it and the action is not complete (a recovered booking whose quantity waits for fee evidence).
Under `mu` it reserves a member read token and captures the obligation revision and the member's native-send generation, reads through `bc.unlocked` with a 20 s timeout, and discards the answer when the token, the generation, the obligation or its revision changed.
The reader is `basketCoordinator.pairNativeHistory` when a test sets it, else the live-shaped venue's `NativeHistory`; paper, replay and virtual venues have none and never read private history.
A failed, incomplete, conflicting or stale answer books nothing, clears nothing and sends nothing; it saves the diagnostic and the next retry (`retry_initial_ms` doubling up to `retry_max_ms`), and wake boundaries include each due retry.

**Accounting.** A complete answer maps through `pairNativeEvidenceResult` once.
For an action it keeps each already accepted fill's saved `pairVenueFill` (so `pairFillsImmutable` passes), runs `pairOutcomeError`, the cross-action fill check, the decided-fill check, `pairEvaluateExecution` and `pairEvaluateFees` on a copy, and refuses any change to accepted fills, a fee conflict, new fills after final fees, an overfill or a legacy action.
`StateStore.CommitPairNative` then moves the row from its saved status (`held`, `unresolved`, or the waiting `settling` above) to `settling` with a dedicated guarded update (exact action id, member, native purpose, request, client id and saved fill set, plus a cross-row native fill-id check) and saves the new evidence and a staged resolution (`pairNativeAttempt.Pending`) in one transaction; generic terminal-row guards are unchanged and a failed transaction restores memory.
The settlement owner books only the ledger delta (new fills, or a fee-only correction); the resolution row and the obligation removal are written only inside a save that writes the member book and leaves the action `committed` and complete (`writePairProgressFinalizingTx`), and memory changes only after that commit (`applyPairNativeFinalized`).
A progress-only or subset save keeps the durable obligation and publishes no resolution, so a failed book save keeps every completion gate closed.
A proven zero fill commits `no_fill` (dedicated update requiring zero confirmed and applied quantity) with its resolution and no book change, and is refused for `native_fill_unconfirmed` or any action holding confirmed or booked fills.
An action-less attempt with confirmed fills becomes a sealed receipt whose `native_receipt_unattached` obligation replaces the unknown one in the same transaction; the receipt owner books it.

**Operator decisions.** `/go-trader-resolve-pair-native` (§ Discord Slash Commands) reaches the coordinator through `pairNativeOperatorRouter` on `AppState.pairViews`.
The handler checks owner and DM scope, then under `mu` the partition, member, kind, decision, current revision (`pairNativeObligationRevision`: SHA-256 prefix of the kind, member, id, start time, linked action status, quantities, fill, fee and ledger records, request hash, attempt id, revision and orders and receipt seal; detail rewrites and retry diagnostics never change it), a printable reason of at most 500 characters, at most 8 order ids, no in-flight native send or read, no committed resolution, no other staged decision and, for a write-off or acknowledgment, no staged recovered booking for the obligation (it ends the obligation through the ledger; a venue read can add its missing evidence, and `show` lists it); a DM `confirm` is required, and staging saves a `pairNativeOperatorRequest` (`pnq-…`) in progress or reports that nothing is staged.
The coordinator step is the only writer: it voids a request whose obligation ended, whose revision changed or after a newer native send for the member (the send keeps its authority), commits write-offs and acknowledgments, and runs at most one operator venue read per step through the same checks as recovery.
A newly staged request (or one loaded after a restart) wakes the coordinator once; a request that cannot apply yet (save blocked, settlement not durable, member busy or a failed commit) waits for the ordinary decision cadence instead of the 1-second floor.
`venue-read` is allowed for `native_fill_unknown` and `native_fill_unconfirmed`; the read covers the operator order ids together with every order id the attempt or action already saved (its order list, its raw close evidence and the action's order id), so a subset never proves a zero fill or settles the close; the 8-order limit applies to that merged set, an unreadable saved id refuses the read, operator order ids already listed by another attempt or saved as the venue order id of another pair action of the member are refused, a read whose fills another pair action of the member already accounts books nothing, and an action without an attempt gets an `operator_orders` attempt for its staged resolution.
The recovery and receipt-conversion transactions and receipt attachment check fill ids against every saved pair action row of the member, whatever its purpose and whether `prunePair` already dropped it from memory; the order-id refusal reads only in-memory actions, so the saved-fill check is the durable guard.
`native_receipt_conflict` and `native_receipt_ambiguous` take only a write-off or an acknowledgment, because no venue record can attribute a whole-coin fill shared with a peer; `native_legacy_unverified` takes only an acknowledgment or a write-off and is never booked again.
A write-off or acknowledgment changes no book, never asserts venue flatness and never changes the kill switch; an `unresolved` action becomes `held`, a conflicting receipt is dropped unbooked, and an obligation with a start time on a horizon kind adds `pairNativeFundingHold` (`NativeFundingHolds`), which `pairNativeUnknownFromMs` reads only while the hold is open, so funding inside its unknown interval stays incomplete.
A later exact venue position read can bound a hold: `pairReadPositions` with proofs enabled captures a token (mutation epoch, coin epoch, native-send generation, contract fingerprint and eligible open holds) before the unlocked read, then per member requires every `pairCheckVenuePosition` check, exact rational equality of venue base quantity (native times contract size) with the canonical book quantity, no unapplied fill, pending action, native or settlement obligation, unattached receipt, staged close or decision, pending recovered booking, native send or history read, no save block, no peer holding or configured for the coin, and the same token, eligibility and journal prefix after the read.
The proof (`pairNativeFundingProof`, canonical bytes with a hash, the sealed position, the contract bytes and fingerprint, exact quantities, the journal row count at acceptance and the ordered host-local read times) and the bound `ThroughMs` (the first receipt time) commit in the one progress transaction through `PersistBasketProgress`; a failed commit restores memory, reads the saved progress back, and keeps the hold open (with a volatile read-back retry when the read-back itself fails), and `checkSavedPairNativeBoundsTx` refuses any later progress write that removes, replaces or moves a saved bound or proof.
Load checks that journal anchor only through `journal_rows`, the prefix present when the proof was accepted; a later append, including one whose event time is at or before the bound, never refuses the load, and a prefix whose quantity differs from `BookBaseQty` still does.
Each funding event whose assessment interval intersects any hold interval is saved `unresolvable` (`native_decision_unproven`) with no charge and no funding obligation, with one alert per hold and no CRITICAL alert per event; an event wholly after a bound uses the ordinary inventory, coverage and settlement checks, coverage stretches and gap obligations subtract the native-unproven intervals, a hold never holds the lifecycle flat reset or the contract-change check, and previously saved unresolvable rows never change.
While any member has an open hold, `pairIncreaseHold` holds every pair increase until an exact venue position read bounds the hold; a bounded hold alone never blocks an entry, exits, queries, protection and emergency closes continue, and the write-off and acknowledgment confirmation states the bound release.
A live member whose book stays open keeps `emergency_close_pending` after a write-off; only booked fills (a venue read) flatten it.

**Audit and markers.** Every resolution is one immutable row in `pair_native_resolutions` (§ Storage Ownership) with mode `venue_history`, `zero_fill`, `operator_venue_read`, `write_off` or `acknowledge`, decided by `coordinator` or `operator`, the operator, reason, request id, revision, decision and commit times, the prior obligation, the attempt snapshot, the evidence, the booked quantity, fill ids, the funding hold start and `alert_status`.
The rows load into the runtime (never into progress), so the flat reset cannot drop them; `recoverPairNativeLegacy` and `endPairNativeAction` skip a resolved item, and receipt attachment holds a receipt carrying a written-off or acknowledged fill as `native_receipt_conflict` for review.
Each committed resolution is alerted once (operator decisions CRITICAL) and marked `dispatched`; a `queued` row after a restart is an uncertain delivery and is never resent.
`pairEmergencyPendingFor`, `pairIncompleteEvidence` (adds `native_funding_uncertain` per hold) and the lifecycle `accounted` test (staged decisions and staged resolutions, never holds) read the remaining state; another member's obligation, risk latches, venue-flat checks and pending actions are untouched.
Pair progress is version 3 only while `native_attempts`, `native_attempt_seq`, `native_funding_holds` or `native_requests` is present (else version 2 bytes are unchanged; versions 1 and 2 load), the flat reset carries all four, and they are recovery evidence for the wrong-file and removed-pair refusals; staged decisions and staged resolutions also refuse a contract change, a funding hold does not.
The offline pair replay exports `pair_native_resolutions`, reports each committed decision in `pair.native_decisions`, lists every hold in the ordered `pair.native_funding_holds`, and keeps a result with a write-off incomplete, using a saved proof offline with no private position read.

**Contracts for later children (#1882, #1883, #1863).**

| Name | Go signature or type | Rule |
|---|---|---|
| `pairNativeAttempt` | `ID`, `MemberID`, `Key`, `Provider`, `AccountKey`, `Instrument`, `ContractFP`, `Purpose`, `Trigger`, `OrderSide`, `PositionSide`, `ActionID`, `ReceiptID`, `Ambiguous`, `SentAtMs`, `EndedAtMs`, `DeadlineMs`, `VenueOrderIDs`, `OrdersComplete`, `Outcome`, `Evidence`, `CloseError`, `Provenance`, `Rev`, `RetryCount`, `RetryAtMs`, `LastError`, `LastReadAtMs`, `Pending` (snake_case) | in `pairProgress.NativeAttempts`; kept only while an obligation or receipt names it |
| `pairNativeResolution` | `ID` (`pnz-…`), `Partition`, `PairID`, `MemberID`, `Kind`, `ObligationID`, `AttemptID`, `ActionID`, `ReceiptID`, `Mode`, `DecidedBy`, `Operator`, `RequestID`, `Reason`, `Revision`, `DecidedAtMs`, `CommittedAtMs`, `Prior`, `Attempt`, `Evidence`, `FillIDs`, `BookedQty`, `FundingHoldFromMs` | one row per obligation in `pair_native_resolutions`; immutable |
| `pairNativeHistoryRequest` / `pairNativeHistoryResult` | request fields above; `Complete`, `Detail`, `ObservedAtMs`, `Orders []pairNativeHistoryOrder`, `Evidence nativeCloseEvidence` | `--pair-native-history-json`; `pairCheckNativeHistory` rechecks every script answer |
| `pairNativeHistoryReader` | `func(ctx context.Context, m pairContractMember, req pairNativeHistoryRequest) (pairNativeHistoryResult, error)` on `basketCoordinator.pairNativeHistory` | nil in production (the live-shaped venue serves); never set on paper or replay hosts |
| `pairNativeOperatorRouter` | `pairNativeOperator(cmd pairNativeOperatorCommand, stage bool) (string, bool)` on `AppState.pairViews` | the only operator entry; stages, the step applies |
| `pairNativeObligationRevision` | `func pairNativeObligationRevision(rt *basketRuntime, ob pairObligation) string` | 16 hex; the operator's expected version |
| `pairNativeUnknownFromMs` | unchanged signature; adds open `NativeFundingHolds` only | funding consumers keep calling it; bounded holds act only through their interval |
| `pairNativeFundingProof` | `V`, hold identity, `ContractFP`, `ContractJSON`, sealed `Position`, `VenueBaseQty`, `BookBaseQty`, `JournalRows`, `RequestStartMs`, `AcceptedAtMs`, `TimeSource`, `ProofHash` | canonical proof bytes stored in the hold; `ThroughMs` is the position first receipt time; `JournalRows` is the inventory prefix checked at load |
| `pairCheckNativeFundingProof` | `func pairCheckNativeFundingProof(hold pairNativeFundingHold, proof pairNativeFundingProof) error` | validates hash, saved contract, position, exact quantities, identity and ordered times without a runtime |
| `checkSavedPairNativeBoundsTx` | `func checkSavedPairNativeBoundsTx(tx *sql.Tx, rt *basketRuntime, next pairProgress) error` | every progress writer refuses to remove, replace or move a saved bound or proof |
| `pairProgressVersionNative` | `3` | written only with native recovery fields |
| `nativeCloseEvidence.OrdersComplete` | `*bool` `orders_complete` | missing = complete only without a close error |

Rules: #1882 keeps paper and replay hosts without a history reader, reports `pair.native_decisions`, treats a write-off as incomplete, and carries the four native progress fields through any new progress literal.
#1883 never resolves a native obligation itself and reads the audit table only.
#1863 enables live pairs only with this resolution path in place and keeps the coordinator as the only pair book writer.

**Tests.** `TestBasketPairNativeRecovery*` and `TestBasketPairNativeOperator*` (untagged, local, CI skips `^TestBasket`: real coordinator and SQLite with a scripted history reader; rate limit then recovery for both venues, confirmed partial then later fills, refused answers, zero fill, identity across restart, action-less receipts, a failed book save, operator write-off, refusals, operator venue read, legacy acknowledgment across the flat reset, and staging racing a step) and `TestBasketPairNativeRecoveryReal*` (`pyintegration`: the real close and check scripts with substituted venue responses, refused network and signing, and a reader that maps the fixture's synthetic accounts to the venue account format).

### Offline basket replay (#1791)

`go-trader basket-replay --spec <step-script|-> [--max-steps N] [--fault exit-after-submit:N]` runs the real coordinator above offline (a version-2 specification runs a research pair instead, Offline pair replay below); `backtest/basket_replay.py` is its driver (docs/backtesting-registry.md).
It is research-only, live use stays refused, and it adds no second coordinator, ranker, member-order authority or storage owner.

**Contract.** Input is one versioned step script (`go-trader.basket-replay` v1, unknown fields refused): `mode` `new` or `resume`, `run` (`run_id`, an absolute real `state_dir`, `source` provenance), the `basket` block, `members` (`id`, `coin`, `timeframe` and an allowlisted `config`: capital, leverage, drawdown and circuit-breaker settings, close strategy, stops, margin mode, pause, ATR method), optional `portfolio_risk`, `target_input` (`ranker` or `baseline` with frozen weights per deadline), optional `cost_policy`, `lot_metadata`, gap-free closed `candles` per `COIN|timeframe`, optional `funding` coverage per coin, and ordered `steps` (`at`, `marks`, scripted `venue` and `queries` outcomes, `save_failure`).
Output is one JSON document on standard output (logs go to standard error): status `complete`, `incomplete`, `stopped`, `refused` or `error`, run fingerprints and cursor, the capital, clock and event-order models, the applied and default parity cost policies, one record per applied step from the start of the run (touched rebalances with targets, holds, exceptions and deviations; touched actions, including holding-horizon closes, with both endpoint bound readings; member lifecycle (entry, expiry, state, attempt, reopen suppression), cash, books, stops, risk state, entry allowance and funding progress; partition latch and daily-loss state; exposure and basket equity), every booked trade, coverage gaps and metadata-fetch refusals.
The output also carries the run-wide venue event log (`venue_events`, #1790): every submit and query call in database event order with its step, client order id, action id, scripted flag, script index and outcome, so a caller can compare the chronological call sequence (the sorted step records do not prove submission order).
Member records also carry the lifecycle failure count, retry time and retained alert keys.
Each step record reads the basket rebalance and action rows saved at that step from the run state file as well, so a rebalance completion or an action that a durable save pruned within the same step (for example a holding-horizon close sent at a new rank deadline) still appears; rows still in memory keep their in-memory state.
A resumed run reads the earlier steps' records and lot refusal counts back from the run state file, so its steps, gaps and refusal count cover every applied step, whichever process applied it.
Exit codes: 0 run applied, 1 error, 2 usage, 3 refused, 75 injected fault.

**Isolation.** The adapter dispatches before any daemon setup, builds its own paper config, opens no notifier, feed or deployed config, and unsets credential environment variables.
Before any state is opened it refuses a step script that is malformed or invalid (including a step earlier than the one before it, a member field that sets routing, mode or storage, and an unknown protocol version), a state directory under a deployed path or behind a symbolic link, new-run mode on any existing path, and resume of a directory the adapter did not create for this run (manifest, single replay run record, owner, entries and every fingerprint must match).
It takes the state file lock, and the scheduler's own storage inspection refuses a file that holds a replay run.

**Clock and inputs.** One simulated clock (`tradingNow`) reaches the booking, funding, risk, decision, action and lifecycle owners; ordinary callers keep the system clock.
The coordinator's holding-horizon expiry, lifecycle, reopen suppression and basket row times take the step time, and the per-book clock (`strategyBookNow`) falls back to the same clock, so a basket with `holding_horizon_seconds` expires unchanged on simulated time, also between rank deadlines and across a resume.
Rank admission and collection use simulated time (a step at or after the window end starts no job, a result collected at or after it holds), while the ranker context and process timeout use the remaining simulated window measured on the system clock, and output from a cancelled or timed-out run never freezes targets.
Frames are cut at the step time with the forming bar open-only; marks are fresh at their sealed time.
Frozen lot metadata serves both target freezing and paper booking and never fetches; a coin absent from it holds and counts as a fetch refusal.
Funding settles through the paper funding owner against the frozen records up to the step time; a gap, held record or unsettled exposure leaves the run incomplete.

**Cycle order.** Each step runs funding accounting, partition risk with the paper kill switch (kept latched, as with an owner configured), the per-member circuit-breaker check and the shared Hyperliquid perps management step (`runHyperliquidPerpsStep`, the same function the scheduler runs for every Hyperliquid perps strategy), then the coordinator step, then one full save that also commits the cursor.
After each durable save the step records built since the previous durable save are written to the run state file.
A step whose save failed with no later durable save, a stored cursor behind the applied steps, or an applied step with no stored record (a stop between the save and the record write) leaves the run incomplete.
A step between deadlines still runs management, reconciliation and frozen-target execution.

**Costs.** Default runs book the frozen paper model (version 1, taker fees on paper take-profit tiers).
A `cost_policy` (`taker_fee_pct`, `slippage_pct`, `fill_role: taker_all`, `spread: inside_slippage`) that differs from it is passed into the same open, add, partial-close, full-close, stop and kill-switch booking owners and the prospective margin gate, once per confirmed fill, and stamps version 1001 with the run's policy id.
A changed policy refuses resume.

**Baseline.** `target_input.mode: baseline` freezes declared weights per deadline through the same target freezing, transition, risk, settlement and recovery owners; listing eligibility, finite weights, gross, the declared net bound (`max_transition_net_usd`) and the per-coin cap are checked, and only the long and short side requirement of production ranker admission is dropped.
Each weight key must be a rebalance deadline that some step reaches inside the evaluation window, and two keys must not name one instant; otherwise the step script is refused.
The path runs only inside the adapter.

**Limits.** Fill role is taker for every fill; spread is inside slippage; regime labels are not frozen, so regime-keyed stops and unified regime closes are refused; venue-absent funding hours inside the window leave the run incomplete; `exit-after-submit` is a controlled restart fault for tests.
The adapter builds each step's market snapshot in process and never writes or reads a sealed feed document, so the seal version (including version 4 trade-flow, version 5 macro-event, version 6 volume-profile, version 7 l2_book and version 8 premium seals) does not apply; it freezes no open-interest, trade-flow, l2_book or premium observations and no macro calendar or quote records, and a configuration that needs any of them is refused before any state is opened.

### Offline pair replay (#1881)

`go-trader basket-replay --spec <spec|-> [--max-steps N] [--fault <kind>:N]` dispatches on the specification `version`: version 1 is the basket replay above, unchanged in input, output and exit meanings, and version 2 runs one research pair (Research pairs above) offline through the real coordinator, policy runner, member checks, virtual venues, funding, risk and booking owners (`scheduler/basket_replay_pair*.go`).
It adds no second coordinator, decision writer, policy caller, book writer or storage owner, and it changes no deployed configuration, default, credential, promotion or live trading behaviour.

**Specification (version 2).** Fields, unknown fields refused: `protocol` (`go-trader.basket-replay`), `version` 2, `mode` (`new` or `resume`), `run` (`run_id`, an absolute clean `state_dir`, `source` provenance), `research_config` (one research configuration object with the sole `pairs` entry and its two member strategies), `input_manifest` (`root`, an absolute directory outside the run directory, and `objects`), `scenario` and `bounds`.
`research_config` must not set `db_file`, `log_dir`, `paper_db_file`, `paper_sources`, `market_feed`, `egress` or `baskets`; the adapter sets `db_file` to `<state_dir>/replay-state.db`, `log_dir` to `<state_dir>/logs` and `market_feed` to `websocket` (management frames reach the Hyperliquid check on stdin), so every storage file lies inside the claimed directory.
Each object is `{kind, member_id, path, sha256, size_bytes}` with kind `evidence` (the canonical `pairEvidenceDocument`), `funding` (the canonical `pairFundingDocument` v1, or since #1934 a `pairFundingDocumentV2` whose policy supplies the research rule and whose `from_ms` and `drain_through_ms` must equal the lifetime start and end; no recording or reader is needed, and the selected evidence and funding amount equal the recorded run while only a sealed recording playback claims the full comparison hash), `quotes` (a public member's recorded snapshots `{v: 1, member_id, records: [{receipt_ms, payload}]}`, payload as base64 source bytes in receipt order), `books` (a sealed Hyperliquid member's recorded book stream `{v: 1, member_id, instance, coverage_from_ms, coverage_through_ms, records: [{event_ms, recv_ms, bids, asks}], markers}`) or `candles` (`{v: 1, member_id, timeframe, rows}` gap-free closed bars).
`scenario` holds `submit_latency_ms`, `response_latency_ms` (the virtual venue latencies), `receipt_model` (`recorded_first_receipt`: every record keeps its recorded first receipt) and `venue_failures` (a list, empty for none): `submit_transport_error` (`purpose`, `nth`), `policy_process_failure` (`at`), `management_check_failure` (`member_id`, `at`) and `save_failure` (`at`); each applies at most once per run and is recorded in `basket_replay_failures` so a resume never repeats it; a finished run with a declared failure that never fired (an `at` that is no step or management time, an `nth` past the submits of that purpose) lists it in `incomplete_reasons` and is not mechanically complete.
`bounds` holds the inclusive `start` and `end` and a finite `max_steps`; the lifetime string `<start_ms>..<end_ms>/<max_steps>` is frozen in the run record and the manifest.
The input fingerprint is the SHA-256 of the specification without `mode`, `run.state_dir` and `input_manifest.root`; the run fingerprint binds protocol 2, run id, source (provenance, adapter `Version` and `SourceCommit`), input and the portable configuration; the manifest also binds the contract fingerprint, the evidence and funding digests, the funding binding, the object set, the lifetime, the scenario, the event order and the administrative field list.

**Validation and claim.** Every object is read, sized and hashed, and every structural check runs before any directory exists; the recorded quote source validates its records against the admitted contract through `pairQuoteSourceErrors` (`pairContractBinder.bindContract`, called once per contract and cached), so a payload that does not derive refuses inside admission before the claim.
Research admission (`newPairResearchAdmission`) is the only directory claim: a new run calls `os.Mkdir(state_dir, 0700)`, creates `pair-research.json` with `O_CREATE|O_EXCL` and syncs the file and the directory, so an existing path (an empty directory, a file or a symbolic link), a missing parent or a deployed prefix refuses and a losing concurrent claim writes nothing into the winner's directory.
The replay then creates `logs`, opens the store, inserts its run record, loads the state and commits the startup bindings, and only then writes `replay-manifest.json` (`O_EXCL`, synced); a directory without that manifest is an unfinished initialization that resume refuses.
Resume accepts only the replay-created entries (`replay-state.db` and its companions, `replay-manifest.json`, `pair-research.json`, `logs`), compares the manifest before admission and the contract, funding binding and run record read-only after it, and only then opens the store writable.

**Startup.** Each member venue is built with `newPairVirtualVenue` before admission (the member partition, its synthetic account, run tag = run id, the policy `adverse_band_bps` when declared, the scenario latencies) and the venue factory returns those instances throughout.
After the store opens: `LoadStateWithStore`, `LoadEntryGuards`, `LoadBaskets`, `ValidateStateWithPairOwner`, the coordinator with `bc.pairClock` = the simulated clock, the canonical policy runner wrapper and the replay hooks, `attachPairVirtualVenues`, `startupCheck`.
A new run then commits the books, contract, `CostEvidence` and `FundingBinding` through `SavePartition`, reads `pairCostBindings`, refuses an absent or incomplete map (model version 1001, a full `pair-cost-` id, rates equal to the contract member cost policy) and freezes it into `basket_replay_runs.member_costs_json` and the manifest before any management, allocation or execution.
A resume compares the loaded bindings and the saved cost evidence with the manifest and checks every member trade stamp (execution rows carry the member binding, funding rows the process stamp) before trading.
The startup refuses (exit 3) when a runtime file is missing or a probe fails: the Hyperliquid market-stdin pair argv (`hyperliquidMarketCheckProbeArgv`), the OKX pair management argv (`okxPairManagementProbeArgv`, with `--pair-member-json` and `--management-stdin`) and `run_pair_policy.py --probe-only`; `probeCheckScripts` also registers the OKX vector for every `check_okx.py`, so startup and `go-trader probe` reach it.

**Sources.** Public members read the newest recorded snapshot whose first receipt is at or before the read time, re-derived from its payload (`pairObservationFromPayload`) with its recorded receipt; nothing substitutes current data.
A sealed Hyperliquid member's seal is rebuilt for each `PairSnapshotFor` call from the recorded book stream received by the read time through `freezeBook` (readiness included) for every `pairFeedBookNeeds` key, with a printable `EvaluationID` `replay/<run>/seal/<at>`, generation 1 equal to `PairGeneration()` and seal time equal to the read time; `archive` members are refused in version 2.
The virtual market (`pairVirtualMarket`) returns, clamped to the simulated clock, every recorded record with `afterMs < venue time <= throughMs` and first receipt `<= throughMs` in recorded order; point sources carry incomplete coverage, and a book stream's window coverage is complete only from its declared start, through its declared end, without markers or receipt gaps above 10000 ms, so end of input never proves absence.
Management frames are the frozen closed candles cut at the cycle time; the Hyperliquid frame adds one open-only row, the OKX input (`pairManagementInput`) carries closed candles only and the member's own visible native mid.

**Host.** One cycle at logical time T: evidence delivery (the visible prefix), `stepPairFundingDue` (the coordinator entry point that takes `mu` and calls `stepPairFunding` with its inventory, visibility, save-block and pending checks; `stepPair` keeps its later call), `preparePairRisk`, partition risk through `measureScopeCycleRiskMarked` with `pairMarksLocked` under one `RLock`, `applyScopeCycleRisk` and `applyPaperKillSwitchCycle` under `Lock`, then on a management cycle `beginCycle` and one risk check plus one native management run per member (Hyperliquid through `runHyperliquidPerpsStep`, OKX through `checkStrategyRiskWithView` and `runOKXStep` with `okxPairManagementCheck`), then `bc.step`, then the final `SaveAll`.
A save-blocked cycle skips risk and management and steps the coordinator with `SaveBlocked`.
The next wake is the earliest of the management deadline (every `interval_seconds` from the start), record deliveries, evidence eligibility boundaries, funding visibility (`NextVisibleMs`), venue visibility, candle closes and the coordinator `wakeDelay` from fresh time; a zero delay runs again at T at most three times in a row.
The run finishes when the next wake is after `end`; exhausting `max_steps` or `--max-steps` stops it unfinished.

**Checkpoints.** Before any work the cycle saves a pending `basket_replay_steps` row (step id, time, ordinal, visible-prefix hash, the host state before the cycle) and points `basket_replay_runs.pending_*` at it.
After `buildPairEnvelope` succeeds the coordinator hook writes the checked collection (run, pair, contract, slot, original start, cutoff and deadline, envelope bytes and fingerprint, seal identity, generation, input object hashes) into the pending row before `freezePairEnvelope`.
After risk and management a `SaveAll` commits the books, risk rows and the coordinator checkpoint (host state after management, the management flag and the partition risk summary) in one transaction (phase `coordinator`).
The final `SaveAll` commits the books, the completed step record, its result hash and the cursor advance (`steps_applied`, `cursor_*`, the virtual ledger count as `cursor_binding_seq`) in one transaction.
The save hook writes only these two markers: internal coordinator saves inside `bc.step` write no replay row, so they never advance the cursor or the phase.
The host state is the management cycle, the next management deadline, the last pair step time, the valuation publication counters (`pairViewGen`, `pairPrepSeq`), the management observations and the echo failures.

**Resume table.**

| Saved state at restart | Restored | Rerun at the pending time | Skipped |
|---|---|---|---|
| cursor complete, no pending row | host state of the last step record, next wake from it | the next cycle | nothing |
| pending, phase `pending` | host state before the cycle | the whole cycle (funding is idempotent by event identity) | nothing |
| pending, phase `coordinator` | the coordinator checkpoint (cycle, observations, deadline, risk summary); the counters before preparation | `preparePairRisk`, `bc.step`, final save | funding, risk measurement and application, the kill switch, `beginCycle`, member management |
| pending with a saved collection and the slot still `reserved` | before `recoverInterrupted`: verified with `verifyPairEnvelope` on the saved bytes and original times, then `recheckPairSlot` and `freezePairEnvelope` under `bc.mu` after attachment and `startupCheck` | as the pending phase says; the slot is decided once from its frozen envelope | collection and reservation of that slot |

A saved collection for another run, contract, slot or input object set, a slot frozen with other evidence or a slot the file never reserved refuses the resume; ordinary recovery keeps its hold rule for every other reserved slot.
The valuation view starts empty and is rebuilt through `preparePairRisk`; the restored counters keep the publication sequence, and a first resumed view without a valid mark for a member removes the equality claim of that run (its last-mark substitution and `MarkLostAtMs` are process memory).

**Faults.** `--fault` accepts `exit-after-submit:N` only for version 1 and `exit-after-collection`, `exit-after-management`, `exit-after-envelope-freeze`, `exit-after-acceptance`, `exit-after-book-write` and `exit-after-book-commit` only for version 2; a wrong pairing or an unknown kind is a usage error (exit 2).
N counts occurrences in the current process: collections recorded, member management passes (`exit-after-management` fires after the pass and before the coordinator checkpoint save, so the resume reruns the whole cycle), envelopes frozen, submits answered with a venue acceptance, and the cycle's final save of the run file (`exit-after-book-write` fires inside `storeCommitHook` after the writes and before the commit; `exit-after-book-commit` after the commit returns).
Each prints `fault <kind>:<N>` on standard error and exits 75.

**Process failures.** The policy runner wrapper records a runner error, an ended context and unreadable output, and every member echo failure after management is recorded, each in `basket_replay_failures` immediately (stage, kind, step, slot or member, cause), before the coordinator freezes its hold; those rows keep the run mechanically incomplete after any resume.
A valid economic hold is a complete policy outcome; scenario rows for transport and save failures are markers only.

**Completion.** `mechanically_complete` needs a finished bounded run, the durable cursor equal to the completed steps with no pending row, no persisted policy or native-check failure, no declared scenario failure left unapplied, passing raw checks (step result hashes, virtual ledger chains, applied funding event decoding, frozen slot envelopes, member trade stamps), no `pairIncompleteEvidence`, no open pair obligation, no pending action, no confirmed but unapplied fill (`held` actions are terminal), no reserved or undecided slot, no non-durable settlement, no `recovery` or `operator_held` lifecycle, a passing `pairVirtualConservation`, no `pairEmergencyPendingFor` member, complete member valuation at the end and no incomplete funding event; an open position at the declared end is permitted when its valuation and accounting are complete.
Exit codes: 0 mechanically complete, 1 incomplete or process error, 2 usage, 3 refused, 75 injected fault; `scored` and `promotion_eligible` are always false for a pair result.

**Output.** One JSON document on standard output: status (`complete`, `incomplete`, `stopped`, `refused`, `error`), run info (fingerprints, lifetime, cursor, resume point, stop reason), member costs, `mechanically_complete` with sorted `incomplete_reasons`, every completed step record (host order trace, host state, partition risk summary, members with their own view marks and contract sizes, the owner readings `portfolioValueMarked`, `portfolioNotionalMarked`, `aggregatePerpsMarginInputsMarked`, `computeAssetDeltasMarked`, `evaluateExposureCapMarked`, `evaluateNotionalCapHoldMarked` and the member risk input status, pair lifecycle and obligations), failures, the `server.go` status fields read through `handleStatus` with live mark fetches disabled, the export and the comparison.
Step records keep every reading exactly as its owner returned it: the replay sums the per-member notional and margin readings in member order, `computeAssetDeltasMarked` walks sorted ids, and stored values are exported exactly.
The export reads the run file tables (strategies, positions, closed positions, trades, portfolio risk, kill switch events, basket contracts, member state, slots, actions, entry intents, quote uses, funding events and inventory, virtual allocations and execution events without `query` records).
The comparison hash is the SHA-256 of that export after the administrative projection: entry intent ids, trade position ids and trade row ids become first-appearance ordinals per kind in every foreign reference, and the progress `seq`, `progress_seq`, the step trace, the partition `total_pv` and `total_notional` of the step risk summary (the coordinator measurement sums them in map order; the per-member values, the owner readings and every risk outcome stay), the virtual ledger sequence and hashes, the failure rows and the storage audit columns are excluded; the raw hashes are verified before the projection.
`comparison.eligible` is true only for a mechanically complete run with no equality reason; an uninterrupted run and a run resumed after any fault with the same run id and inputs share the hash.

**Egress.** `basket-replay` keeps its local classification and joins the configless branch of `gateSubcommandEgress` (`egressConfiglessSubcommands`, shared with the offline `quote-replay` branch): in proxy mode it attests with no configuration and never reads `scheduler/config.json`, for version 1 as well; the version-2 replay builds no public source, account reader or exchange adapter.

**Limits.** Collection and valuation budgets are wall-clock checks of `collection_timeout_ms`, so a host far slower than that budget holds slots; fixtures use 2000 ms for in-memory reads.
A frozen book is used only through the recorded payloads; a policy bound rounded inside the adverse price of the worst consumed level leaves an IOC without a fill.
Pair research configurations refuse higher-timeframe filters, so no higher-timeframe series is cut.

**Tests.** `TestBasketReplayPairOffline*` (`pyintegration`, local only; CI excludes Python-backed basket tests) run the real binary, policy child and member checks: a complete enter, match, funding and exit run with owner proofs, every fault kind resumed to the uninterrupted hash, a sealed source resumed from a saved collection, an economic hold, process failures kept incomplete after resume, a scripted management failure that fires once across a restart, scripted failures that never fire reported as unapplied, missing quotes, an exhausted budget and a failed save, future-record independence, usage and refusal exits, a run with every exchange route blocked, the kill switch closing each member at its own mark, the plan-version-2 driver and its version-2 refusal records.
`TestBasketPairAdmissionExclusiveClaimHasOneWinner`, `TestBasketReplayConfiglessEgressBranchReadsNoDeployedConfig` and `TestBasketReplayPairStorageMigrationKeepsVersionOneRows` run untagged in a local basket test run (CI skips `^TestBasket`).

**Contracts for later children (#1882, #1883).**

| Name | Go signature or type | Rule |
|---|---|---|
| `pairReplayHooks` | `collected func(rt *basketRuntime, c pairCollectedSlot) error`, `frozen func(rt *basketRuntime, slotMs int64)`, `beforeSubmit func(a basketAction) error`, `submitted func(a basketAction, out basketVenueOutcome, err error)` on `basketCoordinator.pairReplay` | nil in production; `collected` runs after `buildPairEnvelope` and before `freezePairEnvelope` (an error holds the slot), `submitted` runs outside `mu` after `Submit` |
| `pairCollectedSlot` | `SlotMs`, `StartMs`, `DeadlineMs int64`, `Envelope pairEnvelope` | the checked collection a host saves before the freeze |
| `stepPairFundingDue` | `func (bc *basketCoordinator) stepPairFundingDue(in basketCycleInputs)` | host step 2; takes `mu`, skips refused or not cost-durable pairs |
| `pairContractBinder` | `bindContract(c basketContract) error` | an optional quote source method `pairQuoteSourceErrors` calls before the claim |
| `pairReplayHostState` | `Cycle`, `NextManageMs`, `LastStepMs`, `ViewGen`, `PrepSeq`, `Observations`, `EchoFailures` (snake_case) | the process memory a restart must restore before the first preparation |
| Phases | `pending`, `coordinator`, `complete` in `basket_replay_steps.phase` | the resume table above; internal saves write no phase |
| `basket_replay_failures` | `stage` (`policy`, `management`, `scenario`), `kind`, `step_index`, `slot_ms`, `member_id`, `cause` | policy and management rows keep a run incomplete; #1882 records outcome classes for paper |
| `mechanically_complete` | output field, exit 0 only when true | `scored` and `promotion_eligible` stay false for every pair result |
| `comparison` | `hash`, `eligible`, `reasons`, `raw_checks` | projection of `pairReplayAdministrativeFields`; equality claims need `eligible` |
| `okxPairManagementProbeArgv` | `[]string` | the OKX management vector both probe paths run |
| Driver plan v2 | `go-trader.basket-replay-plan` v2: `run_id`, `state_dir`, `source`, `research_config {path, sha256}`, `objects [{kind, member_id, path, sha256}]`, `scenario`, `bounds` | the driver hashes and sizes every file and exits 0 only for adapter exit 0 with `mechanically_complete` |

### Isolated paper recording (#1882)

`go-trader basket-replay-paper --spec <spec|-> --config <research egress file> [--max-steps N] [--fault <kind>:N] [--seal]` runs one research pair live on paper through the same version-2 host as Offline pair replay above (`scheduler/basket_replay_paper*.go`, `basket_replay_recorder.go`, `basket_replay_recording.go`, `pair_evidence_recorded.go`).
Every outside read and every process outcome commits to an append-only, hash-chained log before any owner uses it, so a crash resumes from committed inputs and a sealed run reproduces offline through `go-trader basket-replay` version 2.
It adds no second coordinator, policy caller, book writer or storage owner, never reads a deployed configuration, credential or account, and changes no default, promotion or live behaviour; `scored` and `promotion_eligible` stay false.

**Egress gate.** `basket-replay-paper` is a network subcommand with its own branch (`egressResearchSubcommands`, `gateResearchEgress`): `--config` must be an absolute clean path outside the deployed prefixes, a regular file (no symbolic link, real path equal, this user's, one hard link), different from the default configuration, read once through an `O_NOFOLLOW` descriptor whose identity equals the path before and after the read, one strict JSON object whose only key is `egress`.
The gate establishes the process egress from that value (proxy mode attests first, no direct fallback) and passes the decoded value to the command (`researchEgressAdmission`), so the path is never parsed or opened again; any refusal prints one refusal document and exits 3 before any read.

**Specification.** The version-2 replay fields with three changes: `input_manifest.objects` holds exactly one seed `evidence` object (capabilities, synthetic accounts and conversions; funding, quotes, books and candles are recorded live), a `collection` block `{from_ms, through_ms, drain_through_ms}` with `0 < from_ms < through_ms <= drain_through_ms`, and `scenario` adds `paper_poll_interval_ms` (1 ms to the pair interval bound), `paper_funding_poll_ms` (at least 1000), `paper_capability_refresh_ms` (0 = seed capabilities only) and the optional `funding_policy` (Pair funding, Research funding oracle above).
A new run with a policy writes paper manifest version 3 and the asynchronous event order (`pairPaperEventOrderAsync`); a run without one keeps version 1 and the legacy order. Resume refuses a changed version, policy or event order before any writable open. A saved version-2 manifest is not resumed live: `openPairPaper` returns `resume_refused`, naming family 2 and playback of a sealed family-2 export, before compare, restore, schema setup, probes or any run-file write. Family-2 playback keeps version 2 and `pairPaperEventOrderResearch`. The recording schema is per run: 1 for families 1 and 2, 2 for family 3. The header string stays `go-trader.paper-recording/1`; only the schema integer changes. A log that mixes schemas, or whose schema does not match the manifest family, is refused. No new table stores the oracle lifecycle; `call_consume` is a row in the existing append-only log and the recording cursor already moves when that row commits.
`bounds.start` must equal `from_ms` and `bounds.end` must equal `drain_through_ms`; `venue_failures` must be empty and `receipt_model` is `recorded_first_receipt`.
Every member must be `public_snapshot`: a `sealed_feed` or `archive` member is refused before the claim, since the recorder has no live sealed or archive producer and never relabels a public snapshot as one.
Before the claim the recorder checks `max_steps × 3 records × the encoded worst-case payload` against the 1 GiB object bound (`pairPaperMaxQuotesBytes`), so the quotes object of the longest admitted run still exports as one valid object.

**Collection and drain.** Collection reserves slots only for starts in `[from_ms, through_ms)` and makes no new decision at or after `through_ms`: a slot frozen before `through_ms` and still undecided then holds without a policy run (the terminal `decideHold` answer), so no slot ends the run undecided; the drain through `drain_through_ms` runs saved actions, funding, protection, recovery and emergency work only.
The recording hook `collectHold` refuses a slot before reservation, so a refusing period holds no slot.

**Recorder.** One call runner (`pairRecorder.call`) for every recorded kind: `collect` and its `member_read` children, `reference`, `match_poll`, `marks`, `management_candles`, `funding`, `funding_oracle` (#1934, never a process kind), `capability`, the bootstrap `account` and `conversions`, `policy` and `native_check` (`position` is reserved).
Live mode commits `call_start` (kind, phase, member, slot, action, step, ordinal, request and its hash), runs the call, commits `call_end` (class `result`, `infrastructure_failure` or `cancelled`, the result and its hash, raw receipt and completion times, redacted stderr, exit status) and only then returns; the logical clock moves to the larger of the previous logical time plus 1 ms and the raw completion.
A family-3 funding-oracle start is the exception: it commits `call_start` with `async` before the outside read, returns while the read is still pending, and does not move the logical clock.
Its `call_end` and `call_recovery` stay out of the consumable stream, so they can commit inside a step, at restart or at chunk stop without moving the cursor.
`call_consume` is the only new consumable item.
It names the call, the request hash, the terminal hash and kind, the wake, the step and the logical time, and it is the only event that publishes the observation and advances logical time for that call.
The call id is `<step_id>/<ordinal>` per host step (children `<parent>.<n>`, bootstrap `boot<epoch>/<n>`); bootstrap calls (probes, seed records) are never consumed.
Host wakes commit `host_wake` with the logical time `max(previous + 1 ms, wall)`; a wall clock that moves backward is marked and keeps the logical clock monotonic.
Wall budgets go through `bc.pairWallBudget(site, …)` at the `collect`, `reference`, `valuation` and `policy` sites, each started from `bc.pairBudgetStart()` (the recorder wall in paper): live mode commits a `budget` event with the elapsed time and cause, consume mode returns the recorded disposition.
Every paper wall read (wakes, call times, budgets, reader launch windows, the capability receipt, the wait for the next wake) goes through `pairPaperWall`, which is the real clock in every shipped build.
Funding settlement, settlement conversions and the reduction position refetch read only committed in-memory records in paper and playback, so their timeout contexts carry no recorded disposition.
The lock order is `mu`, then the recorder lock, then the one SQLite connection; nothing inside a save transaction, `saveExtra` or `storeCommitHook` takes the recorder lock, and the cycle snapshots the recorder position before its final save.
